## `probstat.inference._penalized`

### EQ-SMOOTH-002 — Penalized least squares and the P-spline

`beta_hat = argmin ||y - X beta||_W^2 + sum_j lambda_j beta' S_j beta
= (X'WX + S_lambda)^-1 X'Wy`, `S_lambda = sum_j lambda_j S_j`; for a P-spline
`X_ik = B_k(x_i)` on equally spaced knots and `S = D_d' D_d`, so that
`beta' S beta = sum_k (Delta^d beta_k)^2`.

- Domain: `W = diag(w_i)`, `w_i > 0`; `S_j` symmetric positive
  semidefinite; `X'WX + S_lambda` positive definite.
- Meaning: the penalty shrinks the fit towards the null space of `S`
  (polynomials of degree `< d` for a P-spline), never towards zero;
  `lambda = 0` is ordinary regression on the basis and `lambda -> infinity`
  the polynomial fit of degree `d - 1`.
- Code: `inference._penalized.PenalizedProblem.solve`,
  `inference.splines.fit_pspline`, `inference.splines.difference_penalty`,
  `inference.splines.uniform_knots`.
- Source: [REF-EILERS-MARX-1996], [REF-WOOD-2017, Chapters 4 and 5].
  - [REF-EILERS-MARX-1996](../references.md#ref-eilers-marx-1996): Paul H. C. Eilers and Brian D. Marx. "Flexible Smoothing with B-splines and Penalties." *Statistical Science* 11(2), 89-121, 1996. P-splines: a rich B-spline basis on equally spaced knots with a difference penalty on adjacent coefficients. DOI: [10.1214/ss/1038425655](https://doi.org/10.1214/ss/1038425655).
  - [REF-WOOD-2017](../references.md#ref-wood-2017): Simon N. Wood. *Generalized Additive Models: An Introduction with R*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall/CRC, Boca Raton, 2017. Penalized regression splines, additive models with identifiability constraints, effective degrees of freedom, GCV and REML smoothness selection, and Bayesian confidence intervals. DOI: [10.1201/9781315370279](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781315370279).
- Registry: [EQ-SMOOTH-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-smooth-002--penalized-least-squares-and-the-p-spline)

### EQ-SMOOTH-003 — Effective degrees of freedom and scale

`H = X (X'WX + S_lambda)^-1 X'W`, `edf = tr(H) = tr((X'WX + S_lambda)^-1 X'WX)`,
`phi_hat = RSS_w / (n - edf)`.

- Domain: as [EQ-SMOOTH-002]; `n > edf`.
- Meaning: `edf` decreases from the number of basis functions at
  `lambda = 0` to the null-space dimension of `S` as `lambda -> infinity`;
  it counts the parameters the data effectively estimate, and per-term
  values are sums of the diagonal of `(X'WX + S_lambda)^-1 X'WX` over the
  term's columns.
- Code: `inference._penalized.PenalizedProblem.solve`,
  `inference.splines.fit_pspline`, `inference.splines.fit_smoothing_spline`,
  `inference.additive_models.fit_additive_model`.
- Source: [REF-WOOD-2017, Chapter 6], [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-1990].
  - [REF-WOOD-2017](../references.md#ref-wood-2017): Simon N. Wood. *Generalized Additive Models: An Introduction with R*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall/CRC, Boca Raton, 2017. Penalized regression splines, additive models with identifiability constraints, effective degrees of freedom, GCV and REML smoothness selection, and Bayesian confidence intervals. DOI: [10.1201/9781315370279](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781315370279).
  - [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-1990](../references.md#ref-hastie-tibshirani-1990): T. J. Hastie and R. J. Tibshirani. *Generalized Additive Models*. Chapman and Hall, London, 1990 (Routledge reissue 2017). Additive models, the backfitting algorithm, and centred smooth terms. DOI: [10.1201/9780203753781](https://doi.org/10.1201/9780203753781).
- Registry: [EQ-SMOOTH-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-smooth-003--effective-degrees-of-freedom-and-scale)

### EQ-SMOOTH-004 — Generalized cross-validation

`GCV(lambda) = n RSS_w(lambda) / (n - tr H(lambda))^2`.

- Domain: `tr H < n`.
- Meaning: a rotation-invariant approximation of leave-one-out
  cross-validation; its minimizer estimates the `lambda` of smallest
  prediction error, and it is flat (and can undersmooth) when the noise
  is small relative to the signal.
- Code: `inference._penalized.PenalizedProblem.criterion`,
  `inference.splines.fit_smoothing_spline`, `inference.splines.fit_pspline`.
- Source: [REF-CRAVEN-WAHBA-1979], [REF-WOOD-2017, Chapter 6].
  - [REF-CRAVEN-WAHBA-1979](../references.md#ref-craven-wahba-1979): Peter Craven and Grace Wahba. "Smoothing Noisy Data with Spline Functions." *Numerische Mathematik* 31(4), 377-403 (issue dated December 1978, usually cited as 1979). The generalized cross-validation score `n RSS / (n - tr H)^2`. DOI: [10.1007/BF01404567](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01404567).
  - [REF-WOOD-2017](../references.md#ref-wood-2017): Simon N. Wood. *Generalized Additive Models: An Introduction with R*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall/CRC, Boca Raton, 2017. Penalized regression splines, additive models with identifiability constraints, effective degrees of freedom, GCV and REML smoothness selection, and Bayesian confidence intervals. DOI: [10.1201/9781315370279](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781315370279).
- Registry: [EQ-SMOOTH-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-smooth-004--generalized-cross-validation)

### EQ-SMOOTH-005 — Restricted likelihood (REML) of a Gaussian penalized model

`V_R(lambda) = ((n - M_p)/2)(1 + log(2 pi phi_hat)) + (1/2) log|X'WX + S_lambda|
- (1/2) sum_j r_j log lambda_j + c`, with
`phi_hat = (RSS_w + beta_hat' S_lambda beta_hat)/(n - M_p)`, `r_j = rank S_j`,
`M_p = p - sum_j r_j`, and `c` free of `lambda`.

- Domain: `beta ~ N(0, phi S_lambda^-)` improper on the null space,
  `y | beta ~ N(X beta, phi W^-1)`; non-overlapping penalty blocks;
  `n > M_p`, `lambda_j > 0`.
- Meaning: minus the exact restricted log-likelihood of the equivalent
  mixed model with `phi` profiled out, so `lambda` is estimated like a
  variance ratio; it penalizes overfitting more strongly than GCV and has
  fewer spurious minima. For a smoothing spline `X = I`, `S = K`, `M_p = 2`
  and `log|W + lambda K| = log|W| + log|R + lambda Q'W^-1Q| - log|R|`.
- Code: `inference._penalized.PenalizedProblem.criterion`,
  `inference.splines.fit_pspline`, `inference.splines.fit_smoothing_spline`,
  `inference.additive_models.fit_additive_model`.
- Source: [REF-WOOD-2011], [REF-WOOD-2017, Chapter 6].
  - [REF-WOOD-2011](../references.md#ref-wood-2011): Simon N. Wood. "Fast Stable Restricted Maximum Likelihood and Marginal Likelihood Estimation of Semiparametric Generalized Linear Models." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society Series B: Statistical Methodology* 73(1), 3-36, 2011 (published online 2010). The Laplace-approximate restricted likelihood of a penalized model, exact in the Gaussian case, with the pseudo-determinant of the penalty. DOI: [10.1111/j.1467-9868.2010.00749.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1467-9868.2010.00749.x).
  - [REF-WOOD-2017](../references.md#ref-wood-2017): Simon N. Wood. *Generalized Additive Models: An Introduction with R*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall/CRC, Boca Raton, 2017. Penalized regression splines, additive models with identifiability constraints, effective degrees of freedom, GCV and REML smoothness selection, and Bayesian confidence intervals. DOI: [10.1201/9781315370279](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781315370279).
- Registry: [EQ-SMOOTH-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-smooth-005--restricted-likelihood-reml-of-a-gaussian-penalized-model)

## `probstat.inference.additive_models`

### EQ-GAM-001 — Gaussian additive model with centred P-spline terms

`y_i = alpha + sum_j f_j(x_ij) + e_i`, `f_j = B_j beta_j`,
`beta_j = Z_j theta_j` with `1'B_j Z_j = 0`, `Z_j` the last `k_j - 1`
columns of the Householder reflection that maps `B_j'1` to a multiple of
`e_1`; penalties `lambda_j theta_j' Z_j' D'D Z_j theta_j` of rank
`k_j - d`.

- Domain: `e_i ~ N(0, phi / w_i)` independent; difference order `d >= 1`;
  each predictor non-constant.
- Meaning: the constraint `sum_i f_j(x_ij) = 0` identifies the intercept
  without changing the space of fitted curves, because constants are
  unpenalized; each term carries its own smoothing parameter and EDF.
- Code: `inference.additive_models.fit_additive_model`,
  `inference.additive_models.AdditiveModelFit.predict`,
  `inference.additive_models.AdditiveTerm.evaluate`.
- Source: [REF-WOOD-2017, Chapter 4], [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-1990].
  - [REF-WOOD-2017](../references.md#ref-wood-2017): Simon N. Wood. *Generalized Additive Models: An Introduction with R*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall/CRC, Boca Raton, 2017. Penalized regression splines, additive models with identifiability constraints, effective degrees of freedom, GCV and REML smoothness selection, and Bayesian confidence intervals. DOI: [10.1201/9781315370279](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781315370279).
  - [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-1990](../references.md#ref-hastie-tibshirani-1990): T. J. Hastie and R. J. Tibshirani. *Generalized Additive Models*. Chapman and Hall, London, 1990 (Routledge reissue 2017). Additive models, the backfitting algorithm, and centred smooth terms. DOI: [10.1201/9780203753781](https://doi.org/10.1201/9780203753781).
- Registry: [EQ-GAM-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-gam-001--gaussian-additive-model-with-centred-p-spline-terms)

### EQ-GAM-002 — Backfitting

Repeat until the terms stop changing: for `j = 1, ..., m`,
`f_j <- S_j (y - alpha - sum_{k != j} f_k)` followed by centring,
`S_j = B_j (B_j'WB_j + lambda_j D'D)^-1 B_j'W`, and then
`alpha <- sum_i w_i (y_i - sum_j f_j(x_ij)) / sum_i w_i`.

- Domain: fixed `lambda_j > 0`; symmetric penalized smoothers.
- Meaning: Gauss-Seidel iteration on the penalized normal equations of
  [EQ-GAM-001]; it converges to the joint penalized solution, so the joint
  fit and the backfit agree for the same `lambda_j`.
- Code: `inference.additive_models.backfit_additive_model`.
- Source: [REF-BUJA-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-1989], [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-1990].
  - [REF-BUJA-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-1989](../references.md#ref-buja-hastie-tibshirani-1989): Andreas Buja, Trevor Hastie, and Robert Tibshirani. "Linear Smoothers and Additive Models." *The Annals of Statistics* 17(2), 453-510, 1989. Backfitting as a Gauss-Seidel solution of the penalized normal equations and its convergence for symmetric smoothers. DOI: [10.1214/aos/1176347115](https://doi.org/10.1214/aos/1176347115).
  - [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-1990](../references.md#ref-hastie-tibshirani-1990): T. J. Hastie and R. J. Tibshirani. *Generalized Additive Models*. Chapman and Hall, London, 1990 (Routledge reissue 2017). Additive models, the backfitting algorithm, and centred smooth terms. DOI: [10.1201/9780203753781](https://doi.org/10.1201/9780203753781).
- Registry: [EQ-GAM-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-gam-002--backfitting)

### EQ-GAM-003 — Partial dependence

`PD_j(v) = (1/n) sum_i m_hat(x_i1, ..., x_i(j-1), v, x_i(j+1), ..., x_ip)`.

- Domain: any prediction function `m_hat`; the average is over the data.
- Meaning: the average prediction with feature `j` held at `v`; for an
  additive model with centred terms it equals `alpha + f_j(v)` exactly,
  and for a model with interactions it averages over them.
- Code: `inference.additive_models.partial_dependence`.
- Source: [REF-FRIEDMAN-2001],
  [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009, Section 10.13].
  - [REF-FRIEDMAN-2001](../references.md#ref-friedman-2001): Jerome H. Friedman. "Greedy Function Approximation: A Gradient Boosting Machine." *The Annals of Statistics* 29(5), 1189-1232, 2001. Partial dependence functions of a fitted model. DOI: [10.1214/aos/1013203451](https://doi.org/10.1214/aos/1013203451).
  - [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009](../references.md#ref-hastie-tibshirani-friedman-2009): Trevor Hastie, Robert Tibshirani, and Jerome Friedman. *The Elements of Statistical Learning: Data Mining, Inference, and Prediction*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2009.
- Registry: [EQ-GAM-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-gam-003--partial-dependence)

## `probstat.inference.amortized`

### EQ-INF-NPE-001 — Gaussian neural posterior estimation

`theta_n ~ p(theta)`, `x_n ~ p(x | theta_n)`, `n = 1..N`;
`q_phi(theta | x) = prod_j N(theta_j; mu_j(x; phi), sigma_j(x; phi)^2)` with
`(mu, log sigma) = MLP_phi(x)` on standardized coordinates;
`phi_hat = argmax_phi sum_n log q_phi(theta_n | x_n)`.

- Domain: a prior that can be sampled and a simulator returning a
  fixed-width vector of finite summaries; the likelihood need not be
  available.
- Meaning: as `N -> infinity`, maximum likelihood over a family that
  contains the posterior recovers `p(theta | x)` for every `x` when the
  proposal is the prior, so one trained network gives the posterior for any
  new observation (amortization). With a diagonal Gaussian family the result
  is exact only for Gaussian posteriors with independent coordinates.
- Code: `inference.fit_amortized_gaussian_posterior`,
  `inference.AmortizedGaussianPosterior`.
- Source: [REF-PAPAMAKARIOS-MURRAY-2016, Proposition 1, Section 2.3, and
  Algorithm 2], trained with [EQ-OPT-ADAM-002].
  - [REF-PAPAMAKARIOS-MURRAY-2016](../references.md#ref-papamakarios-murray-2016): George Papamakarios and Iain Murray. "Fast epsilon-free Inference of Simulation Models with Bayesian Conditional Density Estimation." In *Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 29* (NeurIPS 2016), 2016. Preprint arXiv:1605.06376v4. [https://arxiv.org/abs/1605.06376](https://arxiv.org/abs/1605.06376).
- Registry: [EQ-INF-NPE-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-npe-001--gaussian-neural-posterior-estimation)

## `probstat.inference.bayesian`

### EQ-BAYES-RULE-001 — Bayes' rule for parameter inference

`p(theta | y) = p(y | theta) p(theta) / p(y)`.

- Domain: a proper prior and finite positive evidence for the observed data.
- Meaning: posterior uncertainty is conditional on prior, likelihood, data,
  and model assumptions.
- Code: `inference.condition_linear_gaussian`.
- Source: [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013, Chapter 1].
  - [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-gelman-etal-2013): Andrew Gelman, John B. Carlin, Hal S. Stern, David B. Dunson, Aki Vehtari, and Donald B. Rubin. *Bayesian Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. CRC Press, 2013. ISBN 978-1-4398-4095-5.
- Registry: [EQ-BAYES-RULE-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-bayes-rule-001--bayes-rule-for-parameter-inference)

### EQ-BAYES-LIKE-001 — Independent Gaussian log-likelihood

`log p(y | mu,sigma^2) = -(n/2)log(2 pi sigma^2)
 - sum_i (y_i-mu_i)^2/(2 sigma^2)`.

- Domain: paired finite observations and predictions with known
  `sigma^2 > 0`.
- Meaning: log density of independent homoscedastic Gaussian measurement
  errors.
- Code: `inference.gaussian_log_likelihood` and
  `inverse.GaussianObservationModel.log_likelihood`.
- Source: [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013, Section 3.2].
  - [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-gelman-etal-2013): Andrew Gelman, John B. Carlin, Hal S. Stern, David B. Dunson, Aki Vehtari, and Donald B. Rubin. *Bayesian Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. CRC Press, 2013. ISBN 978-1-4398-4095-5.
- Also names: [`inverse`](inverse.md)
- Registry: [EQ-BAYES-LIKE-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-bayes-like-001--independent-gaussian-log-likelihood)

### EQ-BAYES-LIN-POST-001 — Linear-Gaussian posterior conditioning

`Sigma_n = (Sigma_0^-1 + H^T H/sigma^2)^-1` and
`mu_n = Sigma_n(Sigma_0^-1 mu_0 + H^T y/sigma^2)`.

- Domain: positive-definite Gaussian prior covariance, finite compatible
  design and observations, and known `sigma^2 > 0`.
- Meaning: exact conjugate posterior mean and covariance for linear parameters.
- Code: `inference.condition_linear_gaussian` and
  `inverse.calibrate_linear_gaussian`.
- Source: [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013, Section 3.3] and
  [REF-BISHOP-2006, Section 3.3].
  - [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-gelman-etal-2013): Andrew Gelman, John B. Carlin, Hal S. Stern, David B. Dunson, Aki Vehtari, and Donald B. Rubin. *Bayesian Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. CRC Press, 2013. ISBN 978-1-4398-4095-5.
  - [REF-BISHOP-2006](../references.md#ref-bishop-2006): Christopher M. Bishop. *Pattern Recognition and Machine Learning*. Springer, 2006. ISBN 978-0-387-31073-2.
- Also names: [`inverse`](inverse.md)
- Registry: [EQ-BAYES-LIN-POST-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-bayes-lin-post-001--linear-gaussian-posterior-conditioning)

### EQ-BAYES-POST-SAMPLE-001 — Gaussian posterior sampling

`theta^(s) = mu_n + L z^(s)`, where `L L^T = Sigma_n` and `z^(s) ~ N(0,I)`.

- Domain: positive-definite posterior covariance and a controlled random stream.
- Meaning: correlated parameter draws preserving posterior covariance.
- Code: `inference.GaussianLinearPosterior.sample`.
- Source: [REF-DEVROYE-1986, Chapter XI].
  - [REF-DEVROYE-1986](../references.md#ref-devroye-1986): Luc Devroye. *Non-Uniform Random Variate Generation*. Springer, 1986. DOI: [10.1007/978-1-4613-8643-8](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4613-8643-8).
- Registry: [EQ-BAYES-POST-SAMPLE-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-bayes-post-sample-001--gaussian-posterior-sampling)

### EQ-BAYES-PRED-001 — Linear posterior-predictive distribution

`y_* | y ~ N(H_* mu_n, H_* Sigma_n H_*^T + sigma_obs^2 I + sigma_delta^2 I)`.

- Domain: [EQ-BAYES-LIN-POST-001], compatible prediction design, and
  non-negative included variance components.
- Meaning: separates parameter, future-observation, and model-discrepancy
  uncertainty; latent prediction omits `sigma_obs^2`.
- Code: `inference.posterior_predictive` and `inference.PosteriorPredictive.sample`.
- Source: [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013, Section 6.3] and
  [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001].
  - [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-gelman-etal-2013): Andrew Gelman, John B. Carlin, Hal S. Stern, David B. Dunson, Aki Vehtari, and Donald B. Rubin. *Bayesian Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. CRC Press, 2013. ISBN 978-1-4398-4095-5.
  - [REF-KENNEDY-OHAGAN-2001](../references.md#ref-kennedy-ohagan-2001): Marc C. Kennedy and Anthony O'Hagan. "Bayesian Calibration of Computer Models." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B* 63(3), 425-464, 2001. DOI: [10.1111/1467-9868.00294](https://doi.org/10.1111/1467-9868.00294).
- Registry: [EQ-BAYES-PRED-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-bayes-pred-001--linear-posterior-predictive-distribution)

### EQ-BAYES-PPC-001 — Posterior-predictive tail diagnostic

`p_B = (1/S) sum_s 1[T(y_rep^s) >= T(y_obs)]`, with
`T(y)=(y-mu_pred)^T Sigma_pred^-1 (y-mu_pred)`.

- Domain: positive-definite joint predictive covariance and `S>0` seeded
  replicates.
- Meaning: simulation-based extremeness diagnostic; it is not a frequentist
  p-value or a model-validation certificate.
- Code: `inference.posterior_predictive_check`.
- Source: [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013, Section 6.3].
  - [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-gelman-etal-2013): Andrew Gelman, John B. Carlin, Hal S. Stern, David B. Dunson, Aki Vehtari, and Donald B. Rubin. *Bayesian Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. CRC Press, 2013. ISBN 978-1-4398-4095-5.
- Registry: [EQ-BAYES-PPC-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-bayes-ppc-001--posterior-predictive-tail-diagnostic)

### EQ-DIST-T-001 — Student-t distribution function

For `t >= 0`, `F_nu(t) = 1 - I_{nu/(nu+t^2)}(nu/2, 1/2) / 2`, and
`F_nu(-t) = 1 - F_nu(t)`; quantiles invert `F_nu` by Brent's method.

- Domain: real `t`, `nu > 0`.
- Meaning: marginal and predictive law of regression quantities when the
  noise variance is integrated out.
- Code: `inference.student_t_cdf`, `inference.student_t_quantile`.
- Source: [REF-NIST-DLMF-BETA, Section 8.17] and
  [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Chapter 5].
  - [REF-NIST-DLMF-BETA](../references.md#ref-nist-dlmf-beta): NIST Digital Library of Mathematical Functions, Section 8.17, "Incomplete Beta Functions." National Institute of Standards and Technology. [https://dlmf.nist.gov/8.17](https://dlmf.nist.gov/8.17).
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-DIST-T-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-dist-t-001--student-t-distribution-function)

### EQ-BAYES-NIG-001 — Normal-inverse-gamma conjugate update

Prior `sigma^2 ~ IG(a_0, b_0)`, `beta | sigma^2 ~ N(m_0, sigma^2 V_0)`; data
`y = X beta + e`, `e ~ N(0, sigma^2 I_n)`. Posterior
`V_n = (V_0^-1 + X'X)^-1`, `m_n = V_n (V_0^-1 m_0 + X'y)`, `a_n = a_0 + n/2`,
`b_n = b_0 + ((y - X m_n)'(y - X m_n) + (m_n - m_0)' V_0^-1 (m_n - m_0)) / 2`.

- Domain: positive-definite `V_0`, `a_0, b_0 > 0`, finite data.
- Meaning: exact joint posterior of coefficients and unknown noise variance.
- Code: `inference.condition_linear_normal_inverse_gamma`,
  `inference.NormalInverseGamma`.
- Source: [REF-MURPHY-2012, Section 7.6.3] and
  [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013, Section 14.2].
  - [REF-MURPHY-2012](../references.md#ref-murphy-2012): Kevin P. Murphy. *Machine Learning: A Probabilistic Perspective*. MIT Press, 2012.
  - [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-gelman-etal-2013): Andrew Gelman, John B. Carlin, Hal S. Stern, David B. Dunson, Aki Vehtari, and Donald B. Rubin. *Bayesian Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. CRC Press, 2013. ISBN 978-1-4398-4095-5.
- Registry: [EQ-BAYES-NIG-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-bayes-nig-001--normal-inverse-gamma-conjugate-update)

### EQ-BAYES-NIG-002 — Normal-inverse-gamma marginals

`beta ~ t_{2a}(m, (b/a) V)`; `sigma^2 ~ IG(a, b)` with
`E[sigma^2] = b/(a-1)` for `a > 1`, mode `b/(a+1)`, and quantiles
`b / G_{1-p}` for unit-rate gamma quantiles `G`.

- Domain: [EQ-BAYES-NIG-001].
- Meaning: coefficient uncertainty inflated (heavier tails) by not knowing
  the noise level.
- Code: `inference.NormalInverseGamma.coefficient_marginal`,
  `inference.NormalInverseGamma.coefficient_interval`,
  `inference.NormalInverseGamma.noise_variance_interval`,
  `inference.NormalInverseGamma.sample`.
- Source: [REF-MURPHY-2012, Section 7.6.3] and
  [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013, Appendix A].
  - [REF-MURPHY-2012](../references.md#ref-murphy-2012): Kevin P. Murphy. *Machine Learning: A Probabilistic Perspective*. MIT Press, 2012.
  - [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-gelman-etal-2013): Andrew Gelman, John B. Carlin, Hal S. Stern, David B. Dunson, Aki Vehtari, and Donald B. Rubin. *Bayesian Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. CRC Press, 2013. ISBN 978-1-4398-4095-5.
- Registry: [EQ-BAYES-NIG-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-bayes-nig-002--normal-inverse-gamma-marginals)

### EQ-BAYES-NIG-003 — Normal-inverse-gamma posterior predictive

`y* | y ~ t_{2a}(X* m, (b/a)(I + X* V X*'))`, with component variance
`nu/(nu-2)` times the scale for `nu = 2a > 2`.

- Domain: [EQ-BAYES-NIG-001]; new design rows `X*`.
- Meaning: prediction of future noisy observations with both parameter and
  noise uncertainty.
- Code: `inference.NormalInverseGamma.predictive`,
  `inference.StudentTPredictive`.
- Source: [REF-MURPHY-2012, Section 7.6.3] and
  [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013, Section 14.2].
  - [REF-MURPHY-2012](../references.md#ref-murphy-2012): Kevin P. Murphy. *Machine Learning: A Probabilistic Perspective*. MIT Press, 2012.
  - [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-gelman-etal-2013): Andrew Gelman, John B. Carlin, Hal S. Stern, David B. Dunson, Aki Vehtari, and Donald B. Rubin. *Bayesian Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. CRC Press, 2013. ISBN 978-1-4398-4095-5.
- Registry: [EQ-BAYES-NIG-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-bayes-nig-003--normal-inverse-gamma-posterior-predictive)

## `probstat.inference.clustering`

### EQ-MVA-008 — k-means objective and Lloyd's iteration

`W(C) = sum_i ||x_i - m_{C(i)}||^2`; alternate
`C(i) = argmin_k ||x_i - m_k||^2` and `m_k = mean{x_i : C(i) = k}`.

- Domain: `1 <= K <= n` points in `R^p`; ties go to the lowest index.
- Meaning: neither step can increase `W`, and there are finitely many
  partitions, so the iteration stops at a local minimum; restarts from
  k-means++ seeds ([EQ-GMM-003]) reduce the risk of a poor one.
- Code: `inference.clustering.kmeans`.
- Source: [REF-LLOYD-1982], [REF-ARTHUR-VASSILVITSKII-2007],
  [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009, Section 14.3].
  - [REF-LLOYD-1982](../references.md#ref-lloyd-1982): S. Lloyd. "Least Squares Quantization in PCM." *IEEE Transactions on Information Theory* 28(2), 129-137, 1982. The alternation of nearest-centre assignment and centroid update that decreases the squared-error distortion. DOI: [10.1109/TIT.1982.1056489](https://doi.org/10.1109/TIT.1982.1056489).
  - [REF-ARTHUR-VASSILVITSKII-2007](../references.md#ref-arthur-vassilvitskii-2007): David Arthur and Sergei Vassilvitskii. "k-means++: The Advantages of Careful Seeding." In *Proceedings of the Eighteenth Annual ACM-SIAM Symposium on Discrete Algorithms (SODA '07)*, 1027-1035. SIAM, 2007.
  - [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009](../references.md#ref-hastie-tibshirani-friedman-2009): Trevor Hastie, Robert Tibshirani, and Jerome Friedman. *The Elements of Statistical Learning: Data Mining, Inference, and Prediction*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2009.
- Registry: [EQ-MVA-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-mva-008--k-means-objective-and-lloyds-iteration)

### EQ-MVA-009 — Silhouette width

`s(i) = (b(i) - a(i)) / max(a(i), b(i))`, `a(i)` the mean distance from
`x_i` to the other members of its cluster, `b(i)` the smallest mean
distance to the members of another cluster; `s(i) = 0` for a singleton.

- Domain: Euclidean distance; between 2 and `n - 1` clusters.
- Meaning: `s(i)` near 1 means `x_i` is much closer to its own cluster than
  to the next one, near 0 that it lies between two, and negative that it
  is probably misassigned; the average width compares numbers of clusters.
- Code: `inference.clustering.silhouette_samples`,
  `inference.clustering.silhouette_score`.
- Source: [REF-ROUSSEEUW-1987].
  - [REF-ROUSSEEUW-1987](../references.md#ref-rousseeuw-1987): Peter J. Rousseeuw. "Silhouettes: A Graphical Aid to the Interpretation and Validation of Cluster Analysis." *Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics* 20, 53-65, 1987. The silhouette width `(b - a)/max(a, b)` and its average as a guide to the number of clusters. DOI: [10.1016/0377-0427(87)90125-7](https://doi.org/10.1016/0377-0427(87)90125-7).
- Registry: [EQ-MVA-009](../equation-registry.md#eq-mva-009--silhouette-width)

## `probstat.inference.confidence`

### EQ-INF-CI-NMEAN-001 — Normal z interval for a mean

`x_bar +- z_(1-alpha/2) sigma/sqrt(n)`.

- Domain: independent normal observations with known positive `sigma`; using
  sample standard deviation instead is explicitly labeled a large-sample
  approximation.
- Meaning: repeated-sampling confidence procedure, not a posterior probability
  statement about a fixed mean.
- Code: `inference.normal_mean_confidence_interval`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Sections 9.2-9.3].
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-CI-NMEAN-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-ci-nmean-001--normal-z-interval-for-a-mean)

### EQ-INF-CI-WILSON-001 — Wilson score interval

`center=(p_hat+z^2/(2n))/(1+z^2/n)` and
`half=z sqrt(p_hat(1-p_hat)/n+z^2/(4n^2))/(1+z^2/n)`.

- Domain: `0<=successes<=n`, positive integer `n`, and confidence in `(0,1)`.
- Meaning: score-based binomial-proportion interval with better boundary
  behavior than the uncorrected Wald interval.
- Code: `inference.wilson_score_interval`.
- Source: [REF-WILSON-1927].
  - [REF-WILSON-1927](../references.md#ref-wilson-1927): Edwin B. Wilson. "Probable Inference, the Law of Succession, and Statistical Inference." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 22(158), 209-212, 1927. DOI: [10.1080/01621459.1927.10502953](https://doi.org/10.1080/01621459.1927.10502953).
- Registry: [EQ-INF-CI-WILSON-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-ci-wilson-001--wilson-score-interval)

### EQ-INF-CI-TMEAN-001 — Student-t interval for a normal mean

`x_bar +- t_(n-1, 1-alpha/2) s / sqrt(n)`.

- Domain: `n >= 2` independent normal observations, `s > 0`.
- Meaning: exact `1 - alpha` repeated-sampling coverage for any `n`; closes
  the large-sample caveat of `EQ-INF-CI-NMEAN-001` when `sigma` is estimated.
- Code: `inference.student_t_mean_confidence_interval`,
  `inference.TTestResult.confidence_interval`.
- Source: [REF-STUDENT-1908] and [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Chapter 9].
  - [REF-STUDENT-1908](../references.md#ref-student-1908): Student (William Sealy Gosset). "The Probable Error of a Mean." *Biometrika* 6(1), 1-25, 1908.
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-CI-TMEAN-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-ci-tmean-001--student-t-interval-for-a-normal-mean)

### EQ-INF-CI-CP-001 — Clopper-Pearson binomial interval

`[B(alpha/2; x, n - x + 1), B(1 - alpha/2; x + 1, n - x)]`, with beta
quantiles `B` and endpoints `0` at `x = 0`, `1` at `x = n`.

- Domain: integers `0 <= x <= n`, `n >= 1`.
- Meaning: inverts two one-sided exact binomial tests; coverage is at least
  `1 - alpha` (conservative).
- Code: `inference.clopper_pearson_interval`, `inference.binomial_test`.
- Source: [REF-CLOPPER-PEARSON-1934] and [REF-AGRESTI-2013, Chapter 1].
  - [REF-CLOPPER-PEARSON-1934](../references.md#ref-clopper-pearson-1934): C. J. Clopper and E. S. Pearson. "The Use of Confidence or Fiducial Limits Illustrated in the Case of the Binomial." *Biometrika* 26(4), 404-413, 1934.
  - [REF-AGRESTI-2013](../references.md#ref-agresti-2013): Alan Agresti. *Categorical Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. Wiley, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-CI-CP-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-ci-cp-001--clopper-pearson-binomial-interval)

## `probstat.inference.contingency`

### EQ-INF-CT-001 — Two-by-two effect measures and their intervals

With `p1 = a/(a+b)`, `p2 = c/(c+d)`: risk difference `RD = p1 - p2` with the
Newcombe hybrid score interval `[RD - sqrt((p1 - l1)^2 + (u2 - p2)^2),
RD + sqrt((u1 - p1)^2 + (p2 - l2)^2)]` from Wilson limits `(l_i, u_i)`; risk
ratio `RR = p1/p2` with `log RR +/- z sqrt(1/a - 1/(a+b) + 1/c - 1/(c+d))`
(Katz); odds ratio `OR = ad/(bc)` with `log OR +/- z sqrt(1/a + 1/b + 1/c +
1/d)` (Woolf), adding 0.5 to every cell when one is zero.

- Domain: independent binomial rows with positive totals; the log intervals
  need `a, c > 0` (RR) or the corrected table (OR).
- Meaning: absolute, relative, and odds-scale contrasts of two risks; the
  Newcombe interval keeps nominal coverage near 0 and 1 where the Wald
  interval fails.
- Code: `inference.contingency.two_by_two_measures`.
- Source: [REF-NEWCOMBE-1998], [REF-KATZ-ETAL-1978], [REF-WOOLF-1955],
  [REF-AGRESTI-2013, Chapter 3].
  - [REF-NEWCOMBE-1998](../references.md#ref-newcombe-1998): Robert G. Newcombe. "Interval Estimation for the Difference Between Independent Proportions: Comparison of Eleven Methods." *Statistics in Medicine* 17(8), 873-890, 1998.
  - [REF-KATZ-ETAL-1978](../references.md#ref-katz-etal-1978): D. Katz, J. Baptista, S. P. Azen, and M. C. Pike. "Obtaining Confidence Intervals for the Risk Ratio in Cohort Studies." *Biometrics* 34(3), 469-474, 1978. doi:10.2307/2530610.
  - [REF-WOOLF-1955](../references.md#ref-woolf-1955): Barnet Woolf. "On Estimating the Relation Between Blood Group and Disease." *Annals of Human Genetics* 19(4), 251-253, 1955. doi:10.1111/j.1469-1809.1955.tb01348.x.
  - [REF-AGRESTI-2013](../references.md#ref-agresti-2013): Alan Agresti. *Categorical Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. Wiley, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-CT-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-ct-001--two-by-two-effect-measures-and-their-intervals)

### EQ-INF-CT-002 — McNemar test for paired proportions

With discordant counts `b` and `c`, `b ~ Binomial(b + c, 1/2)` under marginal
homogeneity; the asymptotic statistic is `(|b - c| - k)^2 / (b + c)` with
`k = 1` (continuity correction) or `0`, compared with chi-square on one
degree of freedom.

- Domain: paired binary outcomes; concordant pairs carry no information.
- Meaning: tests whether the probability of a positive outcome changed
  between the two measurements; `b/c` is the conditional odds ratio.
- Code: `inference.contingency.mcnemar_test`.
- Source: [REF-MCNEMAR-1947], [REF-AGRESTI-2013, Chapter 11].
  - [REF-MCNEMAR-1947](../references.md#ref-mcnemar-1947): Quinn McNemar. "Note on the Sampling Error of the Difference Between Correlated Proportions or Percentages." *Psychometrika* 12(2), 153-157, 1947. doi:10.1007/BF02295996.
  - [REF-AGRESTI-2013](../references.md#ref-agresti-2013): Alan Agresti. *Categorical Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. Wiley, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-CT-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-ct-002--mcnemar-test-for-paired-proportions)

### EQ-INF-CT-003 — Pearson and adjusted residuals under independence

`E_ij = R_i C_j / n`, Pearson residual `(O_ij - E_ij)/sqrt(E_ij)`, adjusted
residual `(O_ij - E_ij)/sqrt(E_ij (1 - R_i/n)(1 - C_j/n))`.

- Domain: an `r x c` table with no empty row or column.
- Meaning: adjusted residuals are approximately standard normal under
  independence, so cells with `|residual| > 2` show where the association
  lives after a significant chi-square test.
- Code: `inference.contingency.contingency_residuals`.
- Source: [REF-HABERMAN-1973], [REF-AGRESTI-2013, Chapter 2].
  - [REF-HABERMAN-1973](../references.md#ref-haberman-1973): Shelby J. Haberman. "The Analysis of Residuals in Cross-Classified Tables." *Biometrics* 29(1), 205-220, 1973.
  - [REF-AGRESTI-2013](../references.md#ref-agresti-2013): Alan Agresti. *Categorical Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. Wiley, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-CT-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-ct-003--pearson-and-adjusted-residuals-under-independence)

## `probstat.inference.count_models`

### EQ-INF-COUNT-001 — Poisson maximum likelihood from counts

`l(lambda) = sum_i (x_i log lambda - lambda - log x_i!)`,
`lambda_hat = x_bar = sum_i x_i / n`, `SE(lambda_hat) = sqrt(lambda_hat / n)`;
for a frequency table `f_k`, `l = sum_k f_k (k log lambda - lambda - log k!)`.

- Domain: `n >= 2` independent non-negative integer counts with a positive
  total.
- Meaning: the sample mean maximizes the Poisson likelihood; the standard
  error is the inverse square root of the observed information
  `n / lambda_hat`. The fitted frequencies are `n p(k; lambda_hat)`.
- Code: `inference.fit_poisson_counts`, `inference.CountFit`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Section 7.2] and [EQ-DIST-POIS-001].
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-COUNT-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-count-001--poisson-maximum-likelihood-from-counts)

### EQ-INF-COUNT-002 — Negative binomial profile likelihood and its Poisson boundary

`p(k; r, mu) = Gamma(k + r) / (Gamma(r) k!) (r/(r+mu))^r (mu/(r+mu))^k`,
mean `mu`, variance `mu + mu^2 / r`; for fixed `r` the likelihood is
maximized at `mu = x_bar`; the profile score is
`S(r) = sum_k f_k sum_{j=0}^{k-1} 1/(r+j) + n log(r / (r + x_bar))`, and
`d^2 l / dr^2 = -sum_k f_k sum_{j<k} 1/(r+j)^2 + n (1/r - 1/(r+x_bar))`.
As `r -> infinity`,
`l_prof(r) = l_Poisson(x_bar) + n (s_n^2 - x_bar) / (2r) + O(r^-2)` with the
divisor-`n` variance `s_n^2`.

- Domain: real size `r > 0`, `mu > 0`, non-negative integer counts.
- Meaning: the expansion shows that the profile likelihood rises towards the
  Poisson limit when `s_n^2 < x_bar`; the fit then reports `r = inf`
  (Poisson boundary) and the Poisson log-likelihood, counting two
  parameters in AIC and BIC. When `s_n^2 > x_bar`, `S(r) > 0` for small `r`
  and `S(r) < 0` for large `r`, and Brent's method finds the root. The size
  and mean are orthogonal at `mu = x_bar`, so `SE(r_hat)` is
  `(-d^2 l / dr^2)^(-1/2)` and `SE(mu_hat) = sqrt((mu + mu^2/r)/n)`.
- Code: `inference.fit_negative_binomial_counts`, `inference.CountFit`.
- Source: unimodality of the negative binomial likelihood
  [REF-LEVIN-REEDS-1977]; the expansion follows from
  `log Gamma(k + r) - log Gamma(r) = k log r + k(k-1)/(2r) + O(r^-2)` and
  `r log(1 + mu/r) = mu - mu^2/(2r) + O(r^-2)`; the pmf with integer size is
  [EQ-DIST-NBIN-001].
  - [REF-LEVIN-REEDS-1977](../references.md#ref-levin-reeds-1977): Bruce Levin and James Reeds. "Compound Multinomial Likelihood Functions are Unimodal: Proof of a Conjecture of I. J. Good." *The Annals of Statistics* 5(1), 79-87, 1977. The abstract states that the technique also yields unimodality for the negative binomial likelihood. DOI: [10.1214/aos/1176343741](https://doi.org/10.1214/aos/1176343741).
- Registry: [EQ-INF-COUNT-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-count-002--negative-binomial-profile-likelihood-and-its-poisson-boundary)

### EQ-INF-COUNT-003 — Garwood exact interval for a Poisson mean

With total `S = sum_i x_i` over `n` units and `alpha = 1 - confidence`:
`lower = chi2_{alpha/2}(2S) / (2n)` (`0` when `S = 0`),
`upper = chi2_{1-alpha/2}(2S + 2) / (2n)`, where `chi2_q(nu)` is the
`q`-quantile of the chi-square law with `nu` degrees of freedom.

- Domain: independent Poisson counts with common mean; `0 < confidence < 1`.
- Meaning: inverts the two one-sided Poisson tail tests through the
  Poisson-gamma identity; the coverage is at least the nominal level.
- Code: `inference.poisson_mean_interval`.
- Source: [REF-GARWOOD-1936].
  - [REF-GARWOOD-1936](../references.md#ref-garwood-1936): F. Garwood. "Fiducial Limits for the Poisson Distribution." *Biometrika* 28(3-4), 437-442, 1936. DOI: [10.1093/biomet/28.3-4.437](https://doi.org/10.1093/biomet/28.3-4.437).
- Registry: [EQ-INF-COUNT-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-count-003--garwood-exact-interval-for-a-poisson-mean)

## `probstat.inference.discriminant`

### EQ-MVA-005 — Linear discriminant analysis

`delta_k(x) = x' Sigma^-1 mu_k - mu_k' Sigma^-1 mu_k / 2 + log pi_k`,
`P(k | x) = exp(delta_k(x)) / sum_l exp(delta_l(x))`,
`Sigma = sum_k sum_{i in k} (x_i - mu_k)(x_i - mu_k)' / (n - K)`.

- Domain: Gaussian classes with a common covariance; `n - K >= p`.
- Meaning: the Bayes classifier of that model; the boundaries between
  classes are hyperplanes.
- Code: `inference.discriminant.fit_linear_discriminant`,
  `inference.discriminant.LinearDiscriminant.decision_function`.
- Source: [REF-FISHER-1936],
  [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009, Section 4.3].
  - [REF-FISHER-1936](../references.md#ref-fisher-1936): R. A. Fisher. "The Use of Multiple Measurements in Taxonomic Problems." *Annals of Eugenics* 7(2), 179-188, 1936. The linear discriminant function that maximizes the ratio of between- to within-group variance. DOI: [10.1111/j.1469-1809.1936.tb02137.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1469-1809.1936.tb02137.x).
  - [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009](../references.md#ref-hastie-tibshirani-friedman-2009): Trevor Hastie, Robert Tibshirani, and Jerome Friedman. *The Elements of Statistical Learning: Data Mining, Inference, and Prediction*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2009.
- Registry: [EQ-MVA-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-mva-005--linear-discriminant-analysis)

### EQ-MVA-006 — Quadratic discriminant analysis

`delta_k(x) = -log|Sigma_k| / 2 - (x - mu_k)' Sigma_k^-1 (x - mu_k) / 2
+ log pi_k`, `Sigma_k` the unbiased covariance of class `k`.

- Domain: Gaussian classes; each class has more than `p` observations.
- Meaning: the Bayes classifier with unequal covariances; the boundaries
  are quadrics, at the cost of `K p (p + 1) / 2` covariance parameters.
- Code: `inference.discriminant.fit_quadratic_discriminant`,
  `inference.discriminant.QuadraticDiscriminant.decision_function`.
- Source: [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009, Section 4.3].
  - [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009](../references.md#ref-hastie-tibshirani-friedman-2009): Trevor Hastie, Robert Tibshirani, and Jerome Friedman. *The Elements of Statistical Learning: Data Mining, Inference, and Prediction*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2009.
- Registry: [EQ-MVA-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-mva-006--quadratic-discriminant-analysis)

### EQ-MVA-007 — Fisher's canonical discriminant directions

`a_r = argmax a' B a / a' W a` subject to `a' W a = 1` and `W`-orthogonality
to `a_1, ..., a_{r-1}`, with `B = sum_k pi_k (mu_k - m)(mu_k - m)'`,
`m = sum_k pi_k mu_k`; with `W = L L'`, `a_r = L^-T e_r` for the
eigenvectors `e_r` of `L^-1 B L^-T`, `r = 1, ..., min(K - 1, p)`.

- Domain: `W` the pooled covariance of [EQ-MVA-005], positive definite.
- Meaning: the projections that separate the class means most relative to
  the within-class spread; for two classes `a_1` is proportional to
  `W^-1 (mu_2 - mu_1)`.
- Code: `inference.discriminant.fit_linear_discriminant`,
  `inference.discriminant.LinearDiscriminant.transform`.
- Source: [REF-FISHER-1936],
  [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009, Section 4.3].
  - [REF-FISHER-1936](../references.md#ref-fisher-1936): R. A. Fisher. "The Use of Multiple Measurements in Taxonomic Problems." *Annals of Eugenics* 7(2), 179-188, 1936. The linear discriminant function that maximizes the ratio of between- to within-group variance. DOI: [10.1111/j.1469-1809.1936.tb02137.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1469-1809.1936.tb02137.x).
  - [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009](../references.md#ref-hastie-tibshirani-friedman-2009): Trevor Hastie, Robert Tibshirani, and Jerome Friedman. *The Elements of Statistical Learning: Data Mining, Inference, and Prediction*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2009.
- Registry: [EQ-MVA-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-mva-007--fishers-canonical-discriminant-directions)

## `probstat.inference.em`

### EQ-EM-001 — EM iteration and its monotonicity

`theta_{t+1} = argmax_theta Q(theta | theta_t)`,
`Q(theta | theta_t) = E[log p(y, Z | theta) | y, theta_t]`, and
`l(theta_{t+1}) >= l(theta_t)` for the observed-data log-likelihood `l`.

- Domain: an exact E-step and an M-step that maximizes `Q` (or a penalized
  `Q` whose penalty is fixed across iterations, in which case the inequality
  holds for the penalized objective).
- Meaning: `Q(theta | theta_t) - l(theta)` is maximized at `theta_t`
  (Jensen), so each step cannot decrease `l`; limit points are stationary
  points under Wu's conditions, not necessarily global maxima. The driver
  flags a decrease larger than `slack (1 + |l_t|)`.
- Code: `inference.expectation_maximization`.
- Source: [REF-DEMPSTER-LAIRD-RUBIN-1977], [REF-WU-1983],
  [REF-MCLACHLAN-KRISHNAN-2008].
  - [REF-DEMPSTER-LAIRD-RUBIN-1977](../references.md#ref-dempster-laird-rubin-1977): A. P. Dempster, N. M. Laird, and D. B. Rubin. "Maximum Likelihood from Incomplete Data via the EM Algorithm." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B (Methodological)* 39(1), 1-38, 1977. JSTOR 2984875.
  - [REF-WU-1983](../references.md#ref-wu-1983): C. F. Jeff Wu. "On the Convergence Properties of the EM Algorithm." *The Annals of Statistics* 11(1), 95-103, 1983. DOI: [10.1214/aos/1176346060](https://doi.org/10.1214/aos/1176346060).
  - [REF-MCLACHLAN-KRISHNAN-2008](../references.md#ref-mclachlan-krishnan-2008): Geoffrey J. McLachlan and Thriyambakam Krishnan. *The EM Algorithm and Extensions*. 2nd edition. Wiley, 2008. ISBN 978-0-471-20170-0.
- Registry: [EQ-EM-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-em-001--em-iteration-and-its-monotonicity)

### EQ-EM-CENS-001 — Observed-data log-likelihood of censored normal data

`l(mu, sigma) = sum_exact [-log sigma - log sqrt(2 pi) - z_i^2/2]
+ sum_censored log(Phi(b_i) - Phi(a_i))`, with `z_i = (y_i - mu)/sigma`,
`a_i = (l_i - mu)/sigma`, `b_i = (u_i - mu)/sigma`.

- Domain: `sigma > 0`; each censored value lies in `[l_i, u_i]` with
  `l_i = -inf` (left), `u_i = inf` (right), or both finite (interval).
- Meaning: exact values contribute densities, censored values contribute
  interval probabilities, evaluated in log space without tail cancellation.
- Code: `inference.censored_normal_log_likelihood`.
- Source: [REF-DEMPSTER-LAIRD-RUBIN-1977], [REF-NIST-DLMF, Section 7.2].
  - [REF-DEMPSTER-LAIRD-RUBIN-1977](../references.md#ref-dempster-laird-rubin-1977): A. P. Dempster, N. M. Laird, and D. B. Rubin. "Maximum Likelihood from Incomplete Data via the EM Algorithm." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B (Methodological)* 39(1), 1-38, 1977. JSTOR 2984875.
  - [REF-NIST-DLMF](../references.md#ref-nist-dlmf): NIST Digital Library of Mathematical Functions, Chapter 7, "Error Functions, Dawson's and Fresnel Integrals." National Institute of Standards and Technology. [https://dlmf.nist.gov/7](https://dlmf.nist.gov/7).
- Registry: [EQ-EM-CENS-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-em-cens-001--observed-data-log-likelihood-of-censored-normal-data)

### EQ-EM-CENS-002 — EM steps for censored normal data

E-step: `E[Y_i] = m_i`, `E[Y_i^2] = v_i + m_i^2`, where `m_i`, `v_i` are the
mean and variance of `N(mu_t, sigma_t^2)` truncated to `[l_i, u_i]` (exact
values: `m_i = y_i`, `v_i = 0`). M-step: `mu = (1/n) sum E[Y_i]`,
`sigma^2 = (1/n) sum E[Y_i^2] - mu^2`.

- Domain: at least two finite values to start; positive completed variance.
- Meaning: the complete-data normal MLE applied to completed sufficient
  statistics; truncated moments from [EQ-DIST-TN-003].
- Code: `inference.fit_censored_normal`.
- Source: [REF-DEMPSTER-LAIRD-RUBIN-1977], [REF-MCLACHLAN-KRISHNAN-2008],
  [REF-JKB-1994, Chapter 13].
  - [REF-DEMPSTER-LAIRD-RUBIN-1977](../references.md#ref-dempster-laird-rubin-1977): A. P. Dempster, N. M. Laird, and D. B. Rubin. "Maximum Likelihood from Incomplete Data via the EM Algorithm." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B (Methodological)* 39(1), 1-38, 1977. JSTOR 2984875.
  - [REF-MCLACHLAN-KRISHNAN-2008](../references.md#ref-mclachlan-krishnan-2008): Geoffrey J. McLachlan and Thriyambakam Krishnan. *The EM Algorithm and Extensions*. 2nd edition. Wiley, 2008. ISBN 978-0-471-20170-0.
  - [REF-JKB-1994](../references.md#ref-jkb-1994): Norman L. Johnson, Samuel Kotz, and N. Balakrishnan. *Continuous Univariate Distributions*, Volume 1. 2nd edition. Wiley, 1994.
- Registry: [EQ-EM-CENS-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-em-cens-002--em-steps-for-censored-normal-data)

## `probstat.inference.estimation`

### EQ-INF-MLE-NORM-001 — Normal maximum-likelihood estimators

`mu_hat=(1/n)sum x_i` and
`sigma_hat^2=(1/n)sum (x_i-mu_hat)^2`.

- Domain: non-empty finite real sample with positive empirical MLE variance.
- Meaning: likelihood maximizers; the variance divisor is `n`, not the
  unbiased-estimator divisor `n-1`.
- Code: `inference.fit_normal_mle`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Sections 7.2-7.3].
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-MLE-NORM-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-mle-norm-001--normal-maximum-likelihood-estimators)

### EQ-INF-MLE-LN-001 — Lognormal maximum-likelihood estimators

`mu_hat=mean(log x_i)` and
`sigma_hat^2=(1/n)sum(log x_i-mu_hat)^2`.

- Domain: strictly positive finite observations with nonzero log variance.
- Meaning: fitting the lognormal is equivalent to fitting a normal law after
  the natural-log transform.
- Code: `inference.fit_lognormal_mle`.
- Source: [REF-NIST-DISTRIBUTIONS], [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Section 7.2].
  - [REF-NIST-DISTRIBUTIONS](../references.md#ref-nist-distributions): NIST/SEMATECH. *e-Handbook of Statistical Methods*, Section 1.3.6, "Probability Distributions." National Institute of Standards and Technology. [https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda36.htm](https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda36.htm).
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-MLE-LN-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-mle-ln-001--lognormal-maximum-likelihood-estimators)

### EQ-INF-MLE-GAM-001 — Gamma maximum-likelihood equations

`lambda_hat = k_hat / mean(x)` and
`log k_hat - psi(k_hat) = s = log(mean(x)) - mean(log x)`; Newton's method on
the decreasing left side, safeguarded by a bracket, from
`k_0 = (3 + sqrt(9 + 12 s))/(12 s)`.

- Domain: positive observations with at least two distinct values
  (`s > 0`).
- Meaning: profile likelihood reduces the two-parameter fit to one
  monotone scalar equation.
- Code: `inference.fit_gamma_mle`.
- Source: [REF-JKB-1994, Chapter 17] and [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Section 7.2].
  - [REF-JKB-1994](../references.md#ref-jkb-1994): Norman L. Johnson, Samuel Kotz, and N. Balakrishnan. *Continuous Univariate Distributions*, Volume 1. 2nd edition. Wiley, 1994.
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-MLE-GAM-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-mle-gam-001--gamma-maximum-likelihood-equations)

### EQ-INF-MLE-WEIB-001 — Weibull maximum-likelihood equations

`sum x_i^k log x_i / sum x_i^k - 1/k - mean(log x) = 0` and
`lambda_hat = (mean(x^k_hat))^(1/k_hat)`; logarithms are centred and power
sums use a log-sum-exp shift; Newton's method with bracket safeguard from
`k_0 = pi / (sqrt 6 sd(log x))`.

- Domain: positive observations with at least two distinct values.
- Meaning: the profile equation is strictly increasing in `k`, so its root
  is the unique shape MLE.
- Code: `inference.fit_weibull_mle`.
- Source: [REF-JKB-1994, Chapter 21] and [REF-NIST-DISTRIBUTIONS].
  - [REF-JKB-1994](../references.md#ref-jkb-1994): Norman L. Johnson, Samuel Kotz, and N. Balakrishnan. *Continuous Univariate Distributions*, Volume 1. 2nd edition. Wiley, 1994.
  - [REF-NIST-DISTRIBUTIONS](../references.md#ref-nist-distributions): NIST/SEMATECH. *e-Handbook of Statistical Methods*, Section 1.3.6, "Probability Distributions." National Institute of Standards and Technology. [https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda36.htm](https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda36.htm).
- Registry: [EQ-INF-MLE-WEIB-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-mle-weib-001--weibull-maximum-likelihood-equations)

### EQ-INF-MLE-MVN-001 — Multivariate normal maximum-likelihood estimators

`mu_hat = (1/n) sum x_i`,
`Sigma_hat = (1/n) sum (x_i - mu_hat)(x_i - mu_hat)'`.

- Domain: `n > d` observations with non-singular sample covariance.
- Meaning: likelihood maximizers; divisor `n`, not `n - 1`.
- Code: `inference.fit_multivariate_normal_mle`.
- Source: [REF-MARDIA-KENT-BIBBY-1979, Chapter 4].
  - [REF-MARDIA-KENT-BIBBY-1979](../references.md#ref-mardia-kent-bibby-1979): Kanti V. Mardia, John T. Kent, and John M. Bibby. *Multivariate Analysis*. Academic Press, 1979. ISBN 978-0-12-471252-2.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-MLE-MVN-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-mle-mvn-001--multivariate-normal-maximum-likelihood-estimators)

## `probstat.inference.estimator_properties`

### EQ-EST-001 — Monte Carlo bias, variance, and MSE with standard errors

From estimates `T_1..T_R`:

- `bias = Tbar - theta`, with SE `s/sqrt(R)`;
- variance `s^2`, with SE `sqrt((m4 - s^4 (R-3)/(R-1))/R)`;
- `MSE = R^-1 sum (T_r - theta)^2`, with SE `sd((T - theta)^2)/sqrt(R)`.

`MSE = bias^2 + Var` for exact risks.

- Domain: `R >= 4` independent replications with finite estimates.
- Meaning: each simulated risk component comes with its own sampling error.
- Code: `inference.evaluate_estimator`, `inference.EstimatorRisk`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002], [REF-ROBERT-CASELLA-2004].
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
  - [REF-ROBERT-CASELLA-2004](../references.md#ref-robert-casella-2004): Christian P. Robert and George Casella. *Monte Carlo Statistical Methods*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2004. DOI: [10.1007/978-1-4757-4145-2](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4757-4145-2).
- Registry: [EQ-EST-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-est-001--monte-carlo-bias-variance-and-mse-with-standard-errors)

### EQ-EST-002 — Paired relative efficiency

`RE = MSE(reference)/MSE(T)` on the same data sets. The standard error is
`sqrt((V_a - 2 RE C_ab + RE^2 V_b)/R) / MSE(T)`, where `a`, `b` are the
squared errors of the reference and of `T`.

- Domain: paired replications, `MSE(T) > 0`.
- Meaning: values above one favour `T`. The delta-method SE accounts for
  the positive correlation created by sharing data sets.
- Code: `inference.evaluate_estimator`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002], [REF-ROBERT-CASELLA-2004].
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
  - [REF-ROBERT-CASELLA-2004](../references.md#ref-robert-casella-2004): Christian P. Robert and George Casella. *Monte Carlo Statistical Methods*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2004. DOI: [10.1007/978-1-4757-4145-2](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4757-4145-2).
- Registry: [EQ-EST-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-est-002--paired-relative-efficiency)

### EQ-EST-003 — Interval coverage

`c = R^-1 sum 1{L_r <= theta <= U_r}`, with SE `sqrt(c (1 - c)/R)`.

- Domain: an interval procedure that returns finite ordered bounds.
- Meaning: the frequentist coverage of a procedure at one `theta`.
- Code: `inference.evaluate_estimator`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002].
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-EST-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-est-003--interval-coverage)

### EQ-EST-004 — Cramer-Rao lower bound

For an estimator with `E T = psi(theta)`, `Var T >= grad psi' I(theta)^-1
grad psi / n`, with `I` the per-observation Fisher information. If `T` is
unbiased for `tau(theta)`, `psi = tau`.

- Domain: a regular family (differentiation under the integral), `I`
  positive definite.
- Meaning: an unbiased estimator cannot beat the bound, but a biased one
  can beat the *unbiased* bound in MSE (normal-variance MLE). Efficiency is
  reported as `crlb / MSE`.
- Code: `inference.cramer_rao_bound`, `inference.mle_efficiency`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002], [REF-LEHMANN-CASELLA-1998].
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
  - [REF-LEHMANN-CASELLA-1998](../references.md#ref-lehmann-casella-1998): E. L. Lehmann and George Casella. *Theory of Point Estimation*. 2nd edition. Springer Texts in Statistics. Springer, New York, 1998. ISBN 0-387-98502-6. DOI: [10.1007/b98854](https://doi.org/10.1007/b98854).
- Registry: [EQ-EST-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-est-004--cramer-rao-lower-bound)

### EQ-EST-005 — Normal-variance divisor family

`SS = sum (X_i - Xbar)^2 ~ sigma^2 chi^2_{n-1}`, so
`MSE(SS/d) = sigma^4 (2 (n-1) + (n-1-d)^2)/d^2`. The minimum is at
`d = n + 1`, with value `2 sigma^4/(n + 1)`.

- Domain: i.i.d. normal data, `n >= 2`, `d > 0`.
- Meaning: the unbiased `d = n - 1` is not the minimum-MSE choice.
- Code: `inference.normal_variance_estimator_risk`,
  `inference.minimum_mse_variance_divisor`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002].
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-EST-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-est-005--normal-variance-divisor-family)

### EQ-EST-006 — Uniform(0, theta) maximum

With `M = max X_i`: `E M = n theta/(n+1)` and
`Var M = n theta^2/((n+1)^2 (n+2))`.

- MLE `M`: `MSE = 2 theta^2/((n+1)(n+2))`.
- UMVUE `(n+1) M/n`: variance `theta^2/(n (n+2))`.
- `(n+2) M/(n+1)`: minimum MSE, `theta^2/(n+1)^2`.
- `2 Xbar`: variance `theta^2/(3n)`.

- Domain: `theta > 0`, `n >= 1`.
- Meaning: the sufficient-statistic estimators converge at rate `1/n`
  rather than `1/sqrt(n)`, and the unbiased choice is not the minimum-MSE
  choice.
- Code: `inference.uniform_maximum_risks`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002], [REF-LEHMANN-CASELLA-1998].
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
  - [REF-LEHMANN-CASELLA-1998](../references.md#ref-lehmann-casella-1998): E. L. Lehmann and George Casella. *Theory of Point Estimation*. 2nd edition. Springer Texts in Statistics. Springer, New York, 1998. ISBN 0-387-98502-6. DOI: [10.1007/b98854](https://doi.org/10.1007/b98854).
- Registry: [EQ-EST-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-est-006--uniform0-theta-maximum)

### EQ-EST-007 — Rao-Blackwellization of the Poisson zero probability

`E[1{X_1 = 0} | S] = ((n - 1)/n)^S`, with `S ~ Poisson(n lambda)`. Using
`E a^S = exp(n lambda (a - 1))`:

- the crude estimator has variance `e^{-lambda}(1 - e^{-lambda})`;
- the Rao-Blackwellized estimator has variance
  `e^{-2 lambda}(e^{lambda/n} - 1)`;
- the plug-in `exp(-Xbar)` has mean `exp(n lambda (e^{-1/n} - 1))`.

The bound is `lambda e^{-2 lambda}/n`.

- Domain: `lambda > 0`, `n >= 1`.
- Meaning: conditioning an unbiased estimator on a sufficient statistic
  keeps it unbiased and cannot increase its variance.
- Code: `inference.poisson_zero_indicator`,
  `inference.poisson_zero_rao_blackwell`,
  `inference.poisson_zero_probability_risks`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002], [REF-LEHMANN-CASELLA-1998].
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
  - [REF-LEHMANN-CASELLA-1998](../references.md#ref-lehmann-casella-1998): E. L. Lehmann and George Casella. *Theory of Point Estimation*. 2nd edition. Springer Texts in Statistics. Springer, New York, 1998. ISBN 0-387-98502-6. DOI: [10.1007/b98854](https://doi.org/10.1007/b98854).
- Registry: [EQ-EST-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-est-007--rao-blackwellization-of-the-poisson-zero-probability)

### EQ-EST-008 — Factorization check for sufficiency

If `L(theta; x) = g(T(x), theta) h(x)`, then `T(a) = T(b)` implies that
`log L(theta; a) - log L(theta; b)` is constant in `theta`. For an
exponential family `h(x) exp(eta(theta)' T(x) - A(theta))`,
`sum T(x_i)` is sufficient.

- Domain: two samples with equal statistics and a grid of at least two
  parameter values.
- Meaning: a non-constant ratio refutes sufficiency. A constant ratio on a
  finite grid is necessary evidence, not a proof.
- Code: `inference.check_sufficiency`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002], [REF-LEHMANN-CASELLA-1998].
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
  - [REF-LEHMANN-CASELLA-1998](../references.md#ref-lehmann-casella-1998): E. L. Lehmann and George Casella. *Theory of Point Estimation*. 2nd edition. Springer Texts in Statistics. Springer, New York, 1998. ISBN 0-387-98502-6. DOI: [10.1007/b98854](https://doi.org/10.1007/b98854).
- Registry: [EQ-EST-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-est-008--factorization-check-for-sufficiency)

### EQ-EST-009 — Consistency from vanishing MSE

`P(|T_n - theta| >= eps) <= MSE(T_n)/eps^2`, by Markov's inequality on
`(T_n - theta)^2`.

- Domain: finite second moments, `eps > 0`.
- Meaning: if bias and variance both tend to zero, `T_n` is consistent.
- Code: `inference.consistency_profile`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002], [REF-WASSERMAN-2004].
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
  - [REF-WASSERMAN-2004](../references.md#ref-wasserman-2004): Larry Wasserman. *All of Statistics: A Concise Course in Statistical Inference*. Springer, 2004. DOI: [10.1007/978-0-387-21736-9](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-0-387-21736-9).
- Registry: [EQ-EST-009](../equation-registry.md#eq-est-009--consistency-from-vanishing-mse)

### EQ-EST-010 — Exact moments of the exponential-rate MLE

`Xbar ~ Gamma(n, n lambda)`, so for `lambda_hat = 1/Xbar`:
`E lambda_hat = n lambda/(n-1)` and
`Var lambda_hat = n^2 lambda^2/((n-1)^2 (n-2))`. The biased-estimator
bound is `n lambda^2/(n-1)^2`, and the efficiency is
`crlb/MSE = (n-1)(n-2)/(n (n+2))`.

- Domain: `n >= 3`.
- Meaning: the MLE is biased and never attains the bound at finite `n`,
  but its efficiency tends to one.
- Code: `inference.mle_efficiency`,
  `probability.delta_method_exponential_reciprocal`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002].
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Also names: [`probability`](probability.md)
- Registry: [EQ-EST-010](../equation-registry.md#eq-est-010--exact-moments-of-the-exponential-rate-mle)

## `probstat.inference.fitting`

### EQ-INF-MLE-EXP-001 — Exponential rate maximum-likelihood estimator

`lambda_hat = n / sum x_i = 1 / mean(x)`.

- Domain: non-negative finite observations with positive mean.
- Meaning: the score `n/lambda - sum x_i` vanishes at the reciprocal mean;
  observed information `n / lambda^2` gives `se(lambda_hat) = lambda_hat/sqrt(n)`.
- Code: `inference.fitting.EXPONENTIAL_FAMILY`, `inference.fit_distribution`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Section 7.2].
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-MLE-EXP-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-mle-exp-001--exponential-rate-maximum-likelihood-estimator)

### EQ-INF-MLE-UNIF-001 — Uniform endpoint maximum-likelihood estimator

`(a_hat, b_hat) = (min x_i, max x_i)`, `log L = -n log(b_hat - a_hat)`.

- Domain: finite real observations with at least two distinct values.
- Meaning: the likelihood `(b - a)^-n` on `a <= min x, b >= max x` is
  maximized at the sample range. The support depends on the parameters, so
  the model is non-regular: observed-information errors are undefined and AIC
  is used only as a descriptive score.
- Code: `inference.fitting.UNIFORM_FAMILY`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Section 7.2].
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-MLE-UNIF-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-mle-unif-001--uniform-endpoint-maximum-likelihood-estimator)

### EQ-INF-MLE-GEN-001 — Generic likelihood maximization in unconstrained coordinates

`theta_hat = argmin_theta -sum_i log f(x_i; theta)`, searched over `u` with
`theta_j = a_j + s_j u_j` (location: `s_j` = data spread; real: `s_j = 1`),
`theta_j = a_j exp(u_j)` (positive), or
`theta_j = b_j / (1 + (b_j/a_j - 1) exp(-u_j))` (positive, bounded by `b_j`),
anchored at start values `a`, by BFGS with Armijo backtracking and a
Nelder-Mead fallback.

- Domain: `log_pdf` defined for every observation; invalid parameters or zero
  likelihood map to `+inf`.
- Meaning: transforms remove positivity constraints and equalize scales, so a
  single quasi-Newton routine fits every family; the MLE is invariant to the
  reparameterization.
- Code: `inference.fit_distribution`, `inference.fitting._numerical_mle`,
  `modeling.fit_joint_input`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Section 7.2] and
  [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006, Chapter 6].
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
  - [REF-NOCEDAL-WRIGHT-2006](../references.md#ref-nocedal-wright-2006): Jorge Nocedal and Stephen J. Wright. *Numerical Optimization*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2006.
- Also names: [`modeling`](modeling.md)
- Registry: [EQ-INF-MLE-GEN-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-mle-gen-001--generic-likelihood-maximization-in-unconstrained-coordinates)

### EQ-INF-MLE-GEN-002 — Observed-information covariance by the delta method

`Cov(theta_hat) ~= J H_u^-1 J`, with `H_u` the central-difference Hessian of
the negative log-likelihood in `u` at the estimate and diagonal Jacobian
`J = diag(d theta_j / d u_j)` (`s_j`, `theta_j`, or
`theta_j (1 - theta_j/b_j)`); `se_j = sqrt(Cov_jj)`.

- Domain: regular interior maximum with positive-definite `H_u`; parameters
  within `0.1 %` of an upper bound are held fixed and get `se = nan`.
- Meaning: inverse observed Fisher information gives large-sample standard
  errors; at a stationary point the Hessian transforms exactly by `J`.
- Code: `inference.fitting._observed_information`, `inference.fit_distribution`.
- Source: [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Section 10.1].
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-MLE-GEN-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-mle-gen-002--observed-information-covariance-by-the-delta-method)

### EQ-INF-IC-001 — Akaike information criterion

`AIC = 2k - 2 log L(theta_hat)`.

- Domain: maximized log-likelihood with `k` estimated parameters.
- Meaning: estimate of relative expected Kullback-Leibler information loss;
  only differences between models fitted to the same data are meaningful.
- Code: `inference.information_criteria`, `inference.FitResult`.
- Source: [REF-AKAIKE-1974] and [REF-BURNHAM-ANDERSON-2002, Chapter 2].
  - [REF-AKAIKE-1974](../references.md#ref-akaike-1974): Hirotugu Akaike. "A New Look at the Statistical Model Identification." *IEEE Transactions on Automatic Control* 19(6), 716-723, 1974.
  - [REF-BURNHAM-ANDERSON-2002](../references.md#ref-burnham-anderson-2002): Kenneth P. Burnham and David R. Anderson. *Model Selection and Multimodel Inference: A Practical Information-Theoretic Approach*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-IC-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-ic-001--akaike-information-criterion)

### EQ-INF-IC-002 — Small-sample corrected AIC

`AICc = AIC + 2k(k+1)/(n-k-1)`, `inf` when `n <= k + 1`.

- Domain: `n > k + 1`.
- Meaning: bias correction of AIC that matters when `n/k` is small.
- Code: `inference.information_criteria`.
- Source: [REF-HURVICH-TSAI-1989] and [REF-BURNHAM-ANDERSON-2002, Chapter 2].
  - [REF-HURVICH-TSAI-1989](../references.md#ref-hurvich-tsai-1989): Clifford M. Hurvich and Chih-Ling Tsai. "Regression and Time Series Model Selection in Small Samples." *Biometrika* 76(2), 297-307, 1989.
  - [REF-BURNHAM-ANDERSON-2002](../references.md#ref-burnham-anderson-2002): Kenneth P. Burnham and David R. Anderson. *Model Selection and Multimodel Inference: A Practical Information-Theoretic Approach*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-IC-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-ic-002--small-sample-corrected-aic)

### EQ-INF-IC-003 — Bayesian (Schwarz) information criterion

`BIC = k log n - 2 log L(theta_hat)`.

- Domain: maximized log-likelihood, `n` independent observations.
- Meaning: leading terms of the log marginal likelihood; penalizes
  parameters more strongly than AIC for `n >= 8`.
- Code: `inference.information_criteria`.
- Source: [REF-SCHWARZ-1978].
  - [REF-SCHWARZ-1978](../references.md#ref-schwarz-1978): Gideon Schwarz. "Estimating the Dimension of a Model." *The Annals of Statistics* 6(2), 461-464, 1978.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-IC-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-ic-003--bayesian-schwarz-information-criterion)

### EQ-INF-IC-004 — Akaike weights

`Delta_i = IC_i - min_j IC_j`, `w_i = exp(-Delta_i/2) / sum_j exp(-Delta_j/2)`.

- Domain: finite criterion values over one candidate set and one data set.
- Meaning: relative support of each candidate within the set; they do not
  measure absolute adequacy.
- Code: `inference.akaike_weights`, `inference.select_distribution`.
- Source: [REF-BURNHAM-ANDERSON-2002, Chapter 2].
  - [REF-BURNHAM-ANDERSON-2002](../references.md#ref-burnham-anderson-2002): Kenneth P. Burnham and David R. Anderson. *Model Selection and Multimodel Inference: A Practical Information-Theoretic Approach*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-IC-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-ic-004--akaike-weights)

### EQ-INF-GOF-PB-001 — Parametric-bootstrap p-value with estimated parameters

`T = T(x, F(.; theta_hat(x)))`; for `b = 1..B`: `x*_b ~ F(.; theta_hat(x))`,
`T*_b = T(x*_b, F(.; theta_hat(x*_b)))`;
`p = (1 + #{T*_b >= T}) / (1 + B_ok)`, with `B_ok` successful refits.

- Domain: EDF statistic `T` (KS `D` or AD `A^2`); the refit uses the same
  estimator as the original fit; `p = nan` for an infinite observed `A^2`.
- Meaning: reproduces the null law of the statistic when parameters are
  estimated from the tested data, which the fully specified KS/AD tables do
  not (the Lilliefors/Stephens problem); the smallest attainable p-value is
  `1/(B+1)`.
- Code: `inference.bootstrap_goodness_of_fit`, `inference.select_distribution`.
- Source: [REF-STEPHENS-1974], [REF-LILLIEFORS-1967],
  [REF-DAVISON-HINKLEY-1997, Chapter 4], and [REF-EFRON-TIBSHIRANI-1993,
  Chapter 16].
  - [REF-STEPHENS-1974](../references.md#ref-stephens-1974): Michael A. Stephens. "EDF Statistics for Goodness of Fit and Some Comparisons." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 69(347), 730-737, 1974.
  - [REF-LILLIEFORS-1967](../references.md#ref-lilliefors-1967): Hubert W. Lilliefors. "On the Kolmogorov-Smirnov Test for Normality with Mean and Variance Unknown." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 62(318), 399-402, 1967.
  - [REF-DAVISON-HINKLEY-1997](../references.md#ref-davison-hinkley-1997): A. C. Davison and D. V. Hinkley. *Bootstrap Methods and Their Application*. Cambridge University Press, 1997.
  - [REF-EFRON-TIBSHIRANI-1993](../references.md#ref-efron-tibshirani-1993): Bradley Efron and Robert J. Tibshirani. *An Introduction to the Bootstrap*. Chapman & Hall, 1993.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-GOF-PB-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-gof-pb-001--parametric-bootstrap-p-value-with-estimated-parameters)

### EQ-INF-GOF-QQ-001 — Q-Q and P-P plotting positions

`p_i = (i - 1/2)/n`; Q-Q pairs `(F^-1(p_i), x_(i))`; P-P pairs
`(F(x_(i)), p_i)`.

- Domain: ordered finite sample and a continuous model law.
- Meaning: points near the diagonal indicate agreement; Q-Q emphasizes tails,
  P-P the centre.
- Code: `inference.qq_points`, `inference.pp_points`.
- Source: [REF-WILK-GNANADESIKAN-1968] and [REF-NIST-EDA, Section 1.3.3].
  - [REF-WILK-GNANADESIKAN-1968](../references.md#ref-wilk-gnanadesikan-1968): Martin B. Wilk and Ram Gnanadesikan. "Probability Plotting Methods for the Analysis of Data." *Biometrika* 55(1), 1-17, 1968.
  - [REF-NIST-EDA](../references.md#ref-nist-eda): NIST/SEMATECH. *e-Handbook of Statistical Methods*, Section 1.3, "Exploratory Data Analysis." National Institute of Standards and Technology. [https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda3.htm](https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda3.htm).
- Registry: [EQ-INF-GOF-QQ-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-gof-qq-001--q-q-and-p-p-plotting-positions)

## `probstat.inference.glm`

### EQ-GLM-FAM-001 — GLM families, links, and variance functions

`g(mu_i) = eta_i = x_i'beta + o_i`, `Var(y_i) = phi V(mu_i)/w_i`, with
Gaussian `V = 1`, identity link; binomial (proportions, `w` = trials)
`V = mu(1-mu)`, logit `log(mu/(1-mu))` or probit `Phi^-1(mu)`; Poisson
`V = mu`, log link; Gamma `V = mu^2`, inverse `1/mu` or log link.

- Domain: responses in `[0, 1]` (binomial), `[0, inf)` (Poisson),
  `(0, inf)` (Gamma); logit and probit predictors are clipped to `[-30, 30]`
  and `[-8, 8]` when inverted so fitted probabilities stay inside `(0, 1)`.
- Meaning: the mean model, the link that makes it linear, and the
  mean-variance relation that determines the IRLS weights.
- Code: `inference.GLMFamily`, `inference.glm_family`,
  `inference.GLMResult.predict`.
- Source: [REF-NELDER-WEDDERBURN-1972],
  [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989, Chapters 2, 4, 6, and 8].
  - [REF-NELDER-WEDDERBURN-1972](../references.md#ref-nelder-wedderburn-1972): J. A. Nelder and R. W. M. Wedderburn. "Generalized Linear Models." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series A* 135(3), 370-384, 1972.
  - [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989](../references.md#ref-mccullagh-nelder-1989): P. McCullagh and J. A. Nelder. *Generalized Linear Models*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-GLM-FAM-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-glm-fam-001--glm-families-links-and-variance-functions)

### EQ-GLM-IRLS-001 — Iteratively reweighted least squares (Fisher scoring)

`z_i = eta_i - o_i + (y_i - mu_i)/mu'(eta_i)`,
`W_i = w_i mu'(eta_i)^2 / V(mu_i)`, `beta_new = (X'WX)^-1 X'Wz`, repeated
with step halving when a step leaves the mean space or raises the deviance.

- Domain: full-rank design; convergence when
  `max_j |delta beta_j|/(|beta_j| + 0.1) < tol`; for canonical links Fisher
  scoring equals Newton-Raphson.
- Meaning: maximum-likelihood fitting of a GLM by a sequence of weighted
  least-squares problems.
- Code: `inference.fit_glm`.
- Source: [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989, Section 2.5],
  [REF-NELDER-WEDDERBURN-1972].
  - [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989](../references.md#ref-mccullagh-nelder-1989): P. McCullagh and J. A. Nelder. *Generalized Linear Models*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall, 1989.
  - [REF-NELDER-WEDDERBURN-1972](../references.md#ref-nelder-wedderburn-1972): J. A. Nelder and R. W. M. Wedderburn. "Generalized Linear Models." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series A* 135(3), 370-384, 1972.
- Registry: [EQ-GLM-IRLS-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-glm-irls-001--iteratively-reweighted-least-squares-fisher-scoring)

### EQ-GLM-DEV-001 — Unit deviances and the deviance

`D = sum_i w_i d(y_i, mu_i)` with `d = (y - mu)^2` (Gaussian),
`2[y log(y/mu) + (1-y) log((1-y)/(1-mu))]` (binomial),
`2[y log(y/mu) - (y - mu)]` (Poisson), `2[-log(y/mu) + (y - mu)/mu]`
(Gamma), using `0 log 0 = 0`.

- Domain: [EQ-GLM-FAM-001].
- Meaning: twice the log-likelihood gap to the saturated model (scaled by
  `phi`); differences of deviances of nested models are likelihood-ratio
  statistics. With the canonical log link and an intercept,
  `sum (y - mu) = 0`, so the Poisson deviance reduces to
  `2 sum y log(y/mu)`.
- Code: `inference.GLMFamily.unit_deviance`, `inference.fit_glm`.
- Source: [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989, Section 2.3].
  - [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989](../references.md#ref-mccullagh-nelder-1989): P. McCullagh and J. A. Nelder. *Generalized Linear Models*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-GLM-DEV-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-glm-dev-001--unit-deviances-and-the-deviance)

### EQ-GLM-PEARSON-001 — Pearson chi-square and dispersion estimate

`X^2 = sum_i w_i (y_i - mu_i)^2 / V(mu_i)`; `phi_hat = X^2/(n - p)` for the
Gaussian and Gamma families, `phi = 1` for binomial and Poisson.

- Domain: [EQ-GLM-FAM-001], `n > p`.
- Meaning: moment estimate of the dispersion; `X^2/(n-p)` much larger than
  one for binomial or Poisson data signals overdispersion.
- Code: `inference.fit_glm`.
- Source: [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989, Section 2.5].
  - [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989](../references.md#ref-mccullagh-nelder-1989): P. McCullagh and J. A. Nelder. *Generalized Linear Models*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-GLM-PEARSON-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-glm-pearson-001--pearson-chi-square-and-dispersion-estimate)

### EQ-GLM-WALD-001 — Wald standard errors for GLM coefficients

`Cov(beta_hat) = phi (X'WX)^-1` at the final IRLS weights;
`z_j = beta_hat_j/se_j` compared with `N(0,1)` (known `phi`) or `t_(n-p)`
(estimated `phi`); intervals `beta_hat_j +- q se_j`.

- Domain: large-sample regularity; meaningless under separation.
- Meaning: inverse expected Fisher information as the coefficient
  covariance.
- Code: `inference.fit_glm`, `inference.GLMResult.coefficient_intervals`.
- Source: [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989, Section 2.5].
  - [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989](../references.md#ref-mccullagh-nelder-1989): P. McCullagh and J. A. Nelder. *Generalized Linear Models*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-GLM-WALD-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-glm-wald-001--wald-standard-errors-for-glm-coefficients)

### EQ-GLM-AIC-001 — GLM log-likelihood and AIC

Binomial `sum [log C(w_i, w_i y_i) + w_i y_i log mu_i + w_i(1-y_i) log(1-mu_i)]`;
Poisson `sum w_i [y_i log mu_i - mu_i - log y_i!]`; Gaussian
`-(n/2)(log(2 pi D/n) + 1) + (1/2) sum log w_i`; Gamma
`sum w_i log f_Gamma(y_i; shape 1/phi, scale mu_i phi)` with the plug-in
`phi = D / sum w_i`. `AIC = -2 log L + 2k`, `BIC = -2 log L + k log n`, with
`k = p` plus one for an estimated dispersion.

- Domain: [EQ-GLM-FAM-001]; the Gamma plug-in dispersion is an approximation,
  not the exact shape MLE.
- Meaning: likelihood-based comparison of models for the same response.
- Code: `inference.fit_glm`.
- Source: [REF-AKAIKE-1974], [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989, Chapter 2].
  - [REF-AKAIKE-1974](../references.md#ref-akaike-1974): Hirotugu Akaike. "A New Look at the Statistical Model Identification." *IEEE Transactions on Automatic Control* 19(6), 716-723, 1974.
  - [REF-MCCULLAGH-NELDER-1989](../references.md#ref-mccullagh-nelder-1989): P. McCullagh and J. A. Nelder. *Generalized Linear Models*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall, 1989.
- Registry: [EQ-GLM-AIC-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-glm-aic-001--glm-log-likelihood-and-aic)

### EQ-GLM-SEP-001 — Separation certificate for binomial models

The binomial MLE does not exist when some direction `d` satisfies
`x_i'd >= 0` for `y_i = 1`, `x_i'd <= 0` for `y_i = 0`, and `x_i'd = 0` for
`0 < y_i < 1`, with at least one strict inequality: the log-likelihood is
then non-decreasing along `beta + t d` for every `t > 0`.

- Domain: logit or probit link. The library takes `d` as the last IRLS step,
  requires it to move some `x_i'd` by more than `1e-4`, and checks the
  inequalities to the relative tolerance `1e-6 max_i |x_i'd|`.
- Meaning: complete or quasi-complete separation; the coefficients diverge,
  and the fit is rejected (or flagged with a warning) instead of returning
  meaningless estimates and standard errors.
- Code: `inference.fit_glm`, `inference.SeparationError`,
  `inference.SeparationWarning`.
- Source: [REF-ALBERT-ANDERSON-1984].
  - [REF-ALBERT-ANDERSON-1984](../references.md#ref-albert-anderson-1984): A. Albert and J. A. Anderson. "On the Existence of Maximum Likelihood Estimates in Logistic Regression Models." *Biometrika* 71(1), 1-10, 1984.
- Registry: [EQ-GLM-SEP-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-glm-sep-001--separation-certificate-for-binomial-models)

## `probstat.inference.goodness_of_fit`

### EQ-INF-GOF-KS-001 — One-sample Kolmogorov-Smirnov statistic

`D_n=max_i{i/n-F(x_(i)), F(x_(i))-(i-1)/n}`.

- Domain: ordered finite sample and a fully specified continuous null CDF `F`.
- Meaning: maximum vertical distance between empirical and null CDFs.
- Code: `inference.kolmogorov_smirnov_test`.
- Source: [REF-NIST-GOF, Kolmogorov-Smirnov Test].
  - [REF-NIST-GOF](../references.md#ref-nist-gof): NIST/SEMATECH. *e-Handbook of Statistical Methods*, Section 1.3.5, "Quantitative Techniques," including Kolmogorov-Smirnov and Anderson-Darling goodness-of-fit tests. National Institute of Standards and Technology. [https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda35.htm](https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda35.htm).
- Registry: [EQ-INF-GOF-KS-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-gof-ks-001--one-sample-kolmogorov-smirnov-statistic)

### EQ-INF-GOF-KS-002 — Limiting Kolmogorov tail probability

`P(K>z)=2 sum_(j>=1)(-1)^(j-1) exp(-2j^2z^2)`, with a transformed small-`z`
series and Stephens finite-sample argument correction.

- Domain: non-negative corrected statistic; asymptotic one-sample calibration
  for a fully specified continuous null, used for `n > 100` (smaller samples
  use the exact law of [EQ-INF-GOF-KS-003] and [EQ-INF-GOF-KS-004]).
- Meaning: approximate KS p-value; it is invalid without correction when null
  parameters are estimated from the tested data.
- Code: `inference.goodness_of_fit._kolmogorov_survival`.
- Source: [REF-NIST-GOF, Kolmogorov-Smirnov Test].
  - [REF-NIST-GOF](../references.md#ref-nist-gof): NIST/SEMATECH. *e-Handbook of Statistical Methods*, Section 1.3.5, "Quantitative Techniques," including Kolmogorov-Smirnov and Anderson-Darling goodness-of-fit tests.
