sqrt(V)` is referred to Student t on `nu` df.

- Domain: interior `psi_hat` with a positive-definite `H`; `nu` is reported
  as `nan` otherwise. `H` and `g` are central differences.
- Meaning: matches the first two moments of `V(psi_hat)` to a scaled
  chi-square. When `Cov(beta)` is one mean square (balanced one-way, nested,
  and random-coefficient designs) `nu` is that mean square's df, `a - 1`.
- Code: `inference.LinearMixedModelResult.degrees_of_freedom`,
  `inference.LinearMixedModelResult.test_contrast`.
- Source: [REF-SATTERTHWAITE-1946], [REF-KUZNETSOVA-ETAL-2017, Section 3].
  - [REF-SATTERTHWAITE-1946](../references.md#ref-satterthwaite-1946): F. E. Satterthwaite. "An Approximate Distribution of Estimates of Variance Components." *Biometrics Bulletin* 2(6), 110-114, 1946.
  - [REF-KUZNETSOVA-ETAL-2017](../references.md#ref-kuznetsova-etal-2017): Alexandra Kuznetsova, Per B. Brockhoff, and Rune H. B. Christensen. "lmerTest Package: Tests in Linear Mixed Effects Models." *Journal of Statistical Software* 82(13), 1-26, 2017. Satterthwaite denominator degrees of freedom from the Hessian of the deviance in `(theta, sigma)` and the gradient of the fixed-effect covariance. DOI: [10.18637/jss.v082.i13](https://doi.org/10.18637/jss.v082.i13).
- Registry: [EQ-LMM-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-lmm-006--satterthwaite-denominator-degrees-of-freedom)

### EQ-LMM-007 — Likelihood-ratio test with boundary and bootstrap references

`T = 2 (l_full - l_reduced) >= 0`, `df = k_full - k_reduced`;
`p = P(chi^2_df >= T)`, `p_mix = (1/2) P(chi^2_(df-1) >= T) + (1/2)
P(chi^2_df >= T)` (`chi^2_0` a point mass at zero), and
`p_boot = (1 + #{T*_b >= T}) / (B + 1)` with `T*_b` from `B` responses
simulated from the reduced fit and refitted under both models.

- Domain: nested models on the same response; REML only when both share
  the fixed-effect design.
- Meaning: a variance tested at zero lies on the boundary, where the
  chi-square reference is conservative; `p_mix` is the Self-Liang /
  Stram-Lee limit when the full model adds one random effect with its
  covariances, and the parametric bootstrap estimates the finite-sample
  null law in general.
- Code: `inference.mixed_model_likelihood_ratio_test`.
- Source: [REF-SELF-LIANG-1987], [REF-STRAM-LEE-1994],
  [REF-EFRON-TIBSHIRANI-1993, Chapter 6].
  - [REF-SELF-LIANG-1987](../references.md#ref-self-liang-1987): Steven G. Self and Kung-Yee Liang. "Asymptotic Properties of Maximum Likelihood Estimators and Likelihood Ratio Tests under Nonstandard Conditions." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 82(398), 605-610, 1987. Mixtures of chi-square laws as the limit of the likelihood ratio when the null parameter lies on the boundary. DOI: [10.1080/01621459.1987.10478472](https://doi.org/10.1080/01621459.1987.10478472).
  - [REF-STRAM-LEE-1994](../references.md#ref-stram-lee-1994): Daniel O. Stram and Jae Won Lee. "Variance Components Testing in the Longitudinal Mixed Effects Model." *Biometrics* 50(4), first page 1171, 1994. The 50:50 mixture of chi-square laws with `q` and `q + 1` degrees of freedom for adding one random effect. DOI: [10.2307/2533455](https://doi.org/10.2307/2533455).
  - [REF-EFRON-TIBSHIRANI-1993](../references.md#ref-efron-tibshirani-1993): Bradley Efron and Robert J. Tibshirani. *An Introduction to the Bootstrap*. Chapman & Hall, 1993.
- Registry: [EQ-LMM-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-lmm-007--likelihood-ratio-test-with-boundary-and-bootstrap-references)

### EQ-LMM-008 — Balanced one-way random-effects ANOVA estimators

With `a` groups of size `m`, `N = a m`, `MSA = SSA/(a - 1)`,
`MSE = SSE/(a(m - 1))`: REML `sigma^2 = MSE`,
`sigma_b^2 = (MSA - MSE)/m` when `MSA > MSE`, else `sigma_b^2 = 0`,
`sigma^2 = (SSA + SSE)/(N - 1)`; ML `sigma^2 = MSE`,
`sigma_b^2 = (SSA/a - MSE)/m` when positive, else `sigma_b^2 = 0`,
`sigma^2 = (SSA + SSE)/N`; `Var(beta_hat) = (sigma^2 + m sigma_b^2)/N`.

- Domain: balanced data, one random intercept, `X = 1`.
- Meaning: closed forms reproduced by the profiled deviances; the boundary
  occurs exactly when the ANOVA solution is negative. Under the null
  `sigma_b = 0`, `MSA/MSE ~ F(a - 1, a(m - 1))`, so the ML estimate is zero
  with probability `P(F <= a/(a - 1))`.
- Code: `inference.fit_linear_mixed_model`,
  `tests/test_mixed_models.py::OneWayClosedFormTests`.
- Source: [REF-SEARLE-CASELLA-MCCULLOCH-1992, Chapters 3-4].
  - [REF-SEARLE-CASELLA-MCCULLOCH-1992](../references.md#ref-searle-casella-mcculloch-1992): Shayle R. Searle, George Casella, and Charles E. McCulloch. *Variance Components*. Wiley Series in Probability and Statistics. Wiley, 1992. ANOVA, ML, and REML estimators of balanced one-way and nested random-effects models, and their boundary solutions. DOI: [10.1002/9780470316856](https://doi.org/10.1002/9780470316856).
- Registry: [EQ-LMM-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-lmm-008--balanced-one-way-random-effects-anova-estimators)

### EQ-GLMM-001 — Laplace approximation by penalized IRLS

`h(u) = sum_i log p(y_i | eta_i) - ||u||^2/2`,
`eta = X beta + o + Z Lambda u`; the mode `u_hat` solves Newton steps
`(Lambda' Z' D Z Lambda + I) delta = Lambda' Z' s - u` with
`s_i = w_i (y_i - mu_i)` and `D_i = w_i V(mu_i)` (canonical links);
`-2 log L ~= -2 sum_i log p(y_i | u_hat) + ||u_hat||^2 + log|L|^2`,
`L L' = Lambda' Z' D(u_hat) Z Lambda + I`.

- Domain: binomial with logit link (`y` a proportion, `w` trials) and
  Poisson with log link; `u ~ N(0, I)`, `Var(b) = Lambda Lambda'`.
- Meaning: second-order expansion of the integrand around its mode; the
  error shrinks as the information per random effect grows and is largest
  for small binomial clusters. `theta` and `beta` are optimized jointly and
  `Cov(beta)` is the `beta` block of `2 H^-1`.
- Code: `inference.fit_generalized_linear_mixed_model(method="laplace")`,
  `inference.glmm_deviance`.
- Source: [REF-BATES-ETAL-2015, Section 3], [REF-PINHEIRO-BATES-1995].
  - [REF-BATES-ETAL-2015](../references.md#ref-bates-etal-2015): Douglas Bates, Martin Mächler, Ben Bolker, and Steve Walker. "Fitting Linear Mixed-Effects Models Using lme4." *Journal of Statistical Software* 67(1), 1-48, 2015. The relative covariance factor, penalized least squares by sparse Cholesky, the profiled ML and REML deviances, conditional modes and variances, and the penalized IRLS Laplace approximation for GLMMs. DOI: [10.18637/jss.v067.i01](https://doi.org/10.18637/jss.v067.i01).
  - [REF-PINHEIRO-BATES-1995](../references.md#ref-pinheiro-bates-1995): José C. Pinheiro and Douglas M. Bates. "Approximations to the Log-Likelihood Function in the Nonlinear Mixed-Effects Model." *Journal of Computational and Graphical Statistics* 4(1), 12-35, 1995. Laplace and adaptive Gauss-Hermite approximations of the marginal likelihood. DOI: [10.1080/10618600.1995.10474663](https://doi.org/10.1080/10618600.1995.10474663).
- Registry: [EQ-GLMM-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-glmm-001--laplace-approximation-by-penalized-irls)

### EQ-GLMM-002 — Adaptive Gauss-Hermite quadrature for a scalar random effect

For group `j`, `h_j(u) = sum_(i in j) log p(y_i | x_i' beta + o_i + theta u)
- u^2/2`, mode `u_j`, `s_j = (-h_j''(u_j))^(-1/2)`;
`L_j ~= s_j sum_(k=1..K) (w_k e^(x_k^2) / sqrt(pi)) exp(h_j(u_j + sqrt(2)
s_j x_k))` with Gauss-Hermite nodes `x_k` and weights `w_k` for
`exp(-x^2)` (Golub-Welsch eigenvalues refined by Newton steps on the
orthonormal Hermite polynomial, Christoffel weights
`1 / sum_(j<K) h_j(x_k)^2`).

- Domain: one random-effect term with one column (a random intercept),
  `1 <= K <= 64`.
- Meaning: the rule is centred and scaled at each group's mode, so it is
  exact for a Gaussian integrand and converges quickly; `K = 1` is the
  Laplace value `s_j exp(h_j(u_j))` of [EQ-GLMM-001].
- Code: `inference.fit_generalized_linear_mixed_model(method="adaptive_quadrature")`,
  `inference.glmm_deviance`, `inference.mixed_models._gauss_hermite`.
- Source: [REF-LIU-PIERCE-1994], [REF-PINHEIRO-BATES-1995],
  [REF-GOLUB-WELSCH-1969].
  - [REF-LIU-PIERCE-1994](../references.md#ref-liu-pierce-1994): Qing Liu and Donald A. Pierce. "A Note on Gauss-Hermite Quadrature." *Biometrika* 81(3), 624-629, 1994. Gauss-Hermite nodes centred at the mode and scaled by the curvature of the integrand; one node gives the Laplace approximation. DOI: [10.1093/biomet/81.3.624](https://doi.org/10.1093/biomet/81.3.624).
  - [REF-PINHEIRO-BATES-1995](../references.md#ref-pinheiro-bates-1995): José C. Pinheiro and Douglas M. Bates. "Approximations to the Log-Likelihood Function in the Nonlinear Mixed-Effects Model." *Journal of Computational and Graphical Statistics* 4(1), 12-35, 1995. Laplace and adaptive Gauss-Hermite approximations of the marginal likelihood. DOI: [10.1080/10618600.1995.10474663](https://doi.org/10.1080/10618600.1995.10474663).
  - [REF-GOLUB-WELSCH-1969](../references.md#ref-golub-welsch-1969): Gene H. Golub and John H. Welsch. "Calculation of Gauss Quadrature Rules." *Mathematics of Computation* 23(106), 221-230, 1969.
- Registry: [EQ-GLMM-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-glmm-002--adaptive-gauss-hermite-quadrature-for-a-scalar-random-effect)

## `probstat.inference.mixtures`

### EQ-GMM-001 — Gaussian-mixture log-likelihood and responsibilities

`l = sum_i log sum_k w_k N(x_i | m_k, S_k)` and
`r_ik = w_k N(x_i | m_k, S_k) / sum_l w_l N(x_i | m_l, S_l)`, computed with
`log-sum-exp` and Cholesky factors of `S_k`.

- Domain: weights on the simplex, symmetric positive-definite `S_k`.
- Meaning: `r_ik` is the posterior probability that point `i` came from
  component `k`; it is the E-step of mixture EM.
- Code: `inference.GaussianMixture.log_pdf`,
  `inference.GaussianMixture.responsibilities`, `inference.fit_gaussian_mixture`.
- Source: [REF-MCLACHLAN-PEEL-2000], [REF-BISHOP-2006, Section 9.2].
  - [REF-MCLACHLAN-PEEL-2000](../references.md#ref-mclachlan-peel-2000): Geoffrey McLachlan and David Peel. *Finite Mixture Models*. Wiley, 2000. ISBN 978-0-471-00626-8.
  - [REF-BISHOP-2006](../references.md#ref-bishop-2006): Christopher M. Bishop. *Pattern Recognition and Machine Learning*. Springer, 2006. ISBN 978-0-387-31073-2.
- Registry: [EQ-GMM-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-gmm-001--gaussian-mixture-log-likelihood-and-responsibilities)

### EQ-GMM-002 — Penalized M-step with a fixed covariance floor

Objective `l_pen = l - (1/2) sum_k tr(Lambda S_k^-1)`,
`Lambda = diag(lambda_j)`, `lambda_j = c var_j`. M-step:
`N_k = sum_i r_ik`, `w_k = N_k/n`, `m_k = sum_i r_ik x_i / N_k`,
`S_k = (sum_i r_ik (x_i - m_k)(x_i - m_k)' + Lambda) / N_k` (diagonal part only
for diagonal covariances).

- Domain: `c >= 0`; with `c > 0` every `S_k` is positive definite and
  `l_pen` is bounded above.
- Meaning: the exact maximizer of `Q - (1/2) sum_k tr(Lambda S_k^-1)`; since
  `Lambda` does not change between iterations, EM is monotone for `l_pen`
  ([EQ-EM-001]). Information criteria use the unpenalized `l`.
- Code: `inference.fit_gaussian_mixture`.
- Source: [REF-MCLACHLAN-PEEL-2000], [REF-DEMPSTER-LAIRD-RUBIN-1977].
  - [REF-MCLACHLAN-PEEL-2000](../references.md#ref-mclachlan-peel-2000): Geoffrey McLachlan and David Peel. *Finite Mixture Models*. Wiley, 2000. ISBN 978-0-471-00626-8.
  - [REF-DEMPSTER-LAIRD-RUBIN-1977](../references.md#ref-dempster-laird-rubin-1977): A. P. Dempster, N. M. Laird, and D. B. Rubin. "Maximum Likelihood from Incomplete Data via the EM Algorithm." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B (Methodological)* 39(1), 1-38, 1977. JSTOR 2984875.
- Registry: [EQ-GMM-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-gmm-002--penalized-m-step-with-a-fixed-covariance-floor)

### EQ-GMM-003 — k-means++ seeding

First center uniform over the data; each next center is `x_i` with
probability `D(x_i)^2 / sum_j D(x_j)^2`, `D(x)` the distance from `x` to the
nearest chosen center.

- Domain: `1 <= K <= n`; if all distances are zero the choice is uniform.
- Meaning: the `D^2` weighting of Arthur and Vassilvitskii; the library
  follows it with up to 20 Lloyd iterations before forming EM starting values.
- Code: `inference.kmeans_plus_plus`, `inference.fit_gaussian_mixture`.
- Source: [REF-ARTHUR-VASSILVITSKII-2007].
  - [REF-ARTHUR-VASSILVITSKII-2007](../references.md#ref-arthur-vassilvitskii-2007): David Arthur and Sergei Vassilvitskii. "k-means++: The Advantages of Careful Seeding." In *Proceedings of the Eighteenth Annual ACM-SIAM Symposium on Discrete Algorithms (SODA '07)*, 1027-1035. SIAM, 2007.
- Registry: [EQ-GMM-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-gmm-003--k-means-seeding)

### EQ-GMM-004 — Free parameters of a Gaussian mixture

`p = (K - 1) + K d + K d (d + 1)/2` (full covariances) or
`p = (K - 1) + 2 K d` (diagonal covariances); `AIC = 2p - 2 l`,
`BIC = p log n - 2 l`.

- Domain: maximized unpenalized log-likelihood `l`, `n` data points.
- Meaning: BIC penalizes each extra component by its parameter count; used to
  choose `K` ([EQ-INF-IC-001], [EQ-INF-IC-003]).
- Code: `inference.GaussianMixture.parameter_count`,
  `inference.select_gaussian_mixture`.
- Source: [REF-MCLACHLAN-PEEL-2000], [REF-SCHWARZ-1978], [REF-AKAIKE-1974].
  - [REF-MCLACHLAN-PEEL-2000](../references.md#ref-mclachlan-peel-2000): Geoffrey McLachlan and David Peel. *Finite Mixture Models*. Wiley, 2000. ISBN 978-0-471-00626-8.
  - [REF-SCHWARZ-1978](../references.md#ref-schwarz-1978): Gideon Schwarz. "Estimating the Dimension of a Model." *The Annals of Statistics* 6(2), 461-464, 1978.
  - [REF-AKAIKE-1974](../references.md#ref-akaike-1974): Hirotugu Akaike. "A New Look at the Statistical Model Identification." *IEEE Transactions on Automatic Control* 19(6), 716-723, 1974.
- Registry: [EQ-GMM-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-gmm-004--free-parameters-of-a-gaussian-mixture)

## `probstat.inference.multiple_testing`

### EQ-INF-MT-001 — Bonferroni and Holm adjusted p-values

Bonferroni `min(1, m p_i)`; Holm
`p~_(i) = max_{j <= i} min(1, (m - j + 1) p_(j))` for sorted `p_(1) <= ...`.

- Domain: `m >= 1` p-values in `[0, 1]`, arbitrary dependence.
- Meaning: control of the family-wise error rate at `alpha` when rejecting
  `p~ <= alpha`; Holm is uniformly more powerful than Bonferroni.
- Code: `inference.adjust_p_values`.
- Source: [REF-HOLM-1979] and [REF-LEHMANN-ROMANO-2005, Chapter 9].
  - [REF-HOLM-1979](../references.md#ref-holm-1979): Sture Holm. "A Simple Sequentially Rejective Multiple Test Procedure." *Scandinavian Journal of Statistics* 6(2), 65-70, 1979.
  - [REF-LEHMANN-ROMANO-2005](../references.md#ref-lehmann-romano-2005): E. L. Lehmann and Joseph P. Romano. *Testing Statistical Hypotheses*. 3rd edition. Springer, 2005.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-MT-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-mt-001--bonferroni-and-holm-adjusted-p-values)

### EQ-INF-MT-002 — Benjamini-Hochberg and Benjamini-Yekutieli adjusted p-values

BH `p~_(i) = min_{j >= i} min(1, m p_(j)/j)`; BY multiplies by
`c(m) = sum_{k=1}^{m} 1/k`.

- Domain: BH for independent or positively regression-dependent p-values;
  BY for arbitrary dependence.
- Meaning: control of the false discovery rate at `alpha` when rejecting
  `p~ <= alpha`.
- Code: `inference.adjust_p_values`.
- Source: [REF-BENJAMINI-HOCHBERG-1995] and [REF-BENJAMINI-YEKUTIELI-2001].
  - [REF-BENJAMINI-HOCHBERG-1995](../references.md#ref-benjamini-hochberg-1995): Yoav Benjamini and Yosef Hochberg. "Controlling the False Discovery Rate: A Practical and Powerful Approach to Multiple Testing." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society, Series B* 57(1), 289-300, 1995.
  - [REF-BENJAMINI-YEKUTIELI-2001](../references.md#ref-benjamini-yekutieli-2001): Yoav Benjamini and Daniel Yekutieli. "The Control of the False Discovery Rate in Multiple Testing under Dependency." *The Annals of Statistics* 29(4), 1165-1188, 2001.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-MT-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-mt-002--benjamini-hochberg-and-benjamini-yekutieli-adjusted-p-values)

## `probstat.inference.multivariate_analysis`

### EQ-MVA-001 — Principal components

`S = A L A'`, `L = diag(l_1 >= ... >= l_p)`, `A = (a_1, ..., a_p)`
orthonormal; scores `z_ik = a_k' D^-1 (x_i - x_bar)`, explained ratio
`l_k / sum_j l_j`, loadings `a_jk sqrt(l_k)`; `D = I` for the covariance
and `D = diag(s_j)` for the correlation analysis.

- Domain: `S` the unbiased sample covariance (or correlation) of `n >= 2`
  rows; the sign of each `a_k` is fixed by making its largest-magnitude
  entry positive.
- Meaning: `a_k` maximizes `a'Sa` over unit vectors orthogonal to
  `a_1, ..., a_{k-1}`, and the scores are uncorrelated with variances
  `l_k`; the analysis depends on the units unless the variables are
  standardized.
- Code: `inference.multivariate_analysis.principal_components`,
  `inference.multivariate_analysis.PrincipalComponents.transform`,
  `inference.multivariate_analysis.PrincipalComponents.reconstruct`.
- Source: [REF-PEARSON-1901], [REF-HOTELLING-1933], [REF-JOLLIFFE-2002].
  - [REF-PEARSON-1901](../references.md#ref-pearson-1901): Karl Pearson. "On Lines and Planes of Closest Fit to Systems of Points in Space." *The London, Edinburgh, and Dublin Philosophical Magazine and Journal of Science* 2(11), 559-572, 1901. The best-fitting line and plane through a point cloud: the first principal axes. DOI: [10.1080/14786440109462720](https://doi.org/10.1080/14786440109462720).
  - [REF-HOTELLING-1933](../references.md#ref-hotelling-1933): H. Hotelling. "Analysis of a Complex of Statistical Variables into Principal Components." *Journal of Educational Psychology* 24(6), 417-441, 1933. Principal components as successive variance-maximizing linear combinations. DOI: [10.1037/h0071325](https://doi.org/10.1037/h0071325).
  - [REF-JOLLIFFE-2002](../references.md#ref-jolliffe-2002): I. T. Jolliffe. *Principal Component Analysis*. 2nd edition. Springer Series in Statistics, Springer, New York, 2002. ISBN 0-387-95442-2. Covariance versus correlation PCA, the number of components, and interpretation of loadings. DOI: [10.1007/b98835](https://doi.org/10.1007/b98835).
- Registry: [EQ-MVA-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-mva-001--principal-components)

### EQ-MVA-002 — Maximum-likelihood factor analysis

`R = Lambda Lambda' + Psi`; for fixed `Psi`,
`Lambda = Psi^(1/2) Omega_q (Theta_q - I)^(1/2)` from the `q` largest
eigenvalues `theta_1 >= ... >= theta_q` and eigenvectors `Omega_q` of
`Psi^(-1/2) R Psi^(-1/2)`, and the discrepancy
`F = log|Sigma| - log|R| + tr(Sigma^-1 R) - p` concentrates to
`F(Psi) = sum_{j>q} (theta_j - log theta_j - 1)` with gradient
`dF/dpsi_j = (Lambda Lambda' + Psi - R)_jj / psi_j^2`.

- Domain: `R` a positive-definite correlation matrix, `1 <= q`,
  `0.005 <= psi_j <= 1`; `theta_j > 1` for the retained factors.
- Meaning: the Gaussian likelihood of the factor model profiled over the
  loadings; at an interior optimum the fitted diagonal equals the observed
  one. The loadings are determined only up to an orthogonal rotation.
- Code: `inference.multivariate_analysis.factor_analysis`.
- Source: [REF-JORESKOG-1967], [REF-LAWLEY-MAXWELL-1971].
  - [REF-JORESKOG-1967](../references.md#ref-joreskog-1967): K. G. Jöreskog. "Some Contributions to Maximum Likelihood Factor Analysis." *Psychometrika* 32(4), 443-482, 1967. The concentrated likelihood in the uniquenesses, the loadings from the eigenvectors of `Psi^-1/2 S Psi^-1/2`, and its gradient. DOI: [10.1007/BF02289658](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02289658).
  - [REF-LAWLEY-MAXWELL-1971](../references.md#ref-lawley-maxwell-1971): D. N. Lawley and A. E. Maxwell. *Factor Analysis as a Statistical Method*. 2nd edition. Butterworths, London, 1971. Maximum-likelihood estimation of the factor model and the likelihood-ratio test of the number of factors.
- Registry: [EQ-MVA-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-mva-002--maximum-likelihood-factor-analysis)

### EQ-MVA-003 — Varimax rotation

Maximize `V = sum_k [p sum_i l_ik^4 - (sum_i l_ik^2)^2] / p^2` over
orthogonal rotations `Lambda T`; for columns `a, b` with `x = l_ia`,
`y = l_ib`, `u = x^2 - y^2`, `v = 2xy`, `A = sum u`, `B = sum v`,
`C = sum (u^2 - v^2)`, `D = 2 sum uv`, the optimal planar angle is
`phi = atan2(D - 2AB/p, C - (A^2 - B^2)/p) / 4`.

- Domain: loadings with `p` rows; Kaiser normalization divides each row
  by its length before the rotation and multiplies back afterwards.
- Meaning: the rotation that makes the squared loadings of each factor as
  spread as possible, so each variable loads mainly on few factors; the
  fitted correlations `Lambda Lambda'` are unchanged.
- Code: `inference.multivariate_analysis.varimax`.
- Source: [REF-KAISER-1958].
  - [REF-KAISER-1958](../references.md#ref-kaiser-1958): Henry F. Kaiser. "The Varimax Criterion for Analytic Rotation in Factor Analysis." *Psychometrika* 23(3), 187-200, 1958. The varimax criterion, row normalization, and the closed-form angle of each planar rotation. DOI: [10.1007/BF02289233](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02289233).
- Registry: [EQ-MVA-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-mva-003--varimax-rotation)

### EQ-MVA-004 — Bartlett-corrected test of the number of factors

`X^2 = (n - 1 - (2p + 5)/6 - 2q/3) F(Psi_hat)`, referred to chi-square with
`((p - q)^2 - p - q)/2` degrees of freedom.

- Domain: `n` independent multivariate normal observations, positive
  degrees of freedom.
- Meaning: a large statistic says that `q` factors do not reproduce the
  correlations; it is a large-sample likelihood-ratio test.
- Code: `inference.multivariate_analysis.factor_analysis`.
- Source: [REF-LAWLEY-MAXWELL-1971].
  - [REF-LAWLEY-MAXWELL-1971](../references.md#ref-lawley-maxwell-1971): D. N. Lawley and A. E. Maxwell. *Factor Analysis as a Statistical Method*. 2nd edition. Butterworths, London, 1971. Maximum-likelihood estimation of the factor model and the likelihood-ratio test of the number of factors.
- Registry: [EQ-MVA-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-mva-004--bartlett-corrected-test-of-the-number-of-factors)

## `probstat.inference.normality`

### EQ-INF-GOF-CVM-001 — Cramer-von Mises statistic

`W^2 = 1/(12 n) + sum_(i=1..n) (F(x_(i)) - (2i - 1)/(2n))^2`.

- Domain: ordered finite sample `x_(1) <= ... <= x_(n)` and a continuous
  null CDF `F`.
- Meaning: `n` times the integrated squared distance between the empirical
  and null CDFs, weighted by the null law; its null distribution depends on
  whether parameters were estimated from the data.
- Code: `inference.cramer_von_mises_statistic`, `inference.normality_tests`.
- Source: [REF-STEPHENS-1974] and [REF-STEPHENS-1986].
  - [REF-STEPHENS-1974](../references.md#ref-stephens-1974): Michael A. Stephens. "EDF Statistics for Goodness of Fit and Some Comparisons." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 69(347), 730-737, 1974.
  - [REF-STEPHENS-1986](../references.md#ref-stephens-1986): Michael A. Stephens. "Tests Based on EDF Statistics." In Ralph B. D'Agostino and Michael A. Stephens (eds.), *Goodness-of-Fit Techniques*. Marcel Dekker, New York, 1986. Reissued by Routledge, 2017, pages 97-194, DOI: [10.1201/9780203753064-4](https://doi.org/10.1201/9780203753064-4).
- Registry: [EQ-INF-GOF-CVM-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-gof-cvm-001--cramer-von-mises-statistic)

### EQ-INF-GOF-NORM-001 — Lilliefors p-value for the fitted-normal KS distance

With `D` the KS distance to `N(x_bar, s^2)` (divisor `n - 1`), `K = D`,
`m = n` for `n <= 100`, and `K = D (n/100)^0.49`, `m = 100` otherwise:
`p = exp(-7.01256 K^2 (m + 2.78019) + 2.99587 K sqrt(m + 2.78019) -
0.122119 + 0.974598/sqrt(m) + 1.67997/m)`. When this exceeds `0.1`,
`Z = D (sqrt(n) - 0.01 + 0.85/sqrt(n))` and `p = 1` for `Z <= 0.302`, a
quartic polynomial in `Z` on `(0.302, 0.5]`, `(0.5, 0.9]`, and `(0.9, 1.31]`,
and `p = 0` above `1.31`.

- Domain: `n >= 5`; normal null with both parameters estimated.
- Meaning: calibrated tail probability of the Lilliefors statistic; the
  analytic part is reliable for `p <= 0.1`, the polynomial covers the bulk.
- Code: `inference.lilliefors_p_value`, `inference.normality_tests`.
- Source: [REF-DALLAL-WILKINSON-1986] (analytic approximation),
  [REF-STEPHENS-1974] (modified statistic `Z`), and
  [REF-GROSS-LIGGES-2015, function `lillie.test`] (polynomial coefficients
  fitted by simulation).
  - [REF-DALLAL-WILKINSON-1986](../references.md#ref-dallal-wilkinson-1986): Gerard E. Dallal and Leland Wilkinson. "An Analytic Approximation to the Distribution of Lilliefors's Test Statistic for Normality." *The American Statistician* 40(4), 294-296, 1986. DOI: [10.1080/00031305.1986.10475419](https://doi.org/10.1080/00031305.1986.10475419).
  - [REF-STEPHENS-1974](../references.md#ref-stephens-1974): Michael A. Stephens. "EDF Statistics for Goodness of Fit and Some Comparisons." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 69(347), 730-737, 1974.
  - [REF-GROSS-LIGGES-2015](../references.md#ref-gross-ligges-2015): Juergen Gross and Uwe Ligges. *nortest: Tests for Normality*. R package version 1.0-4, 2015. [https://CRAN.R-project.org/package=nortest](https://CRAN.R-project.org/package=nortest).
- Registry: [EQ-INF-GOF-NORM-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-gof-norm-001--lilliefors-p-value-for-the-fitted-normal-ks-distance)

### EQ-INF-GOF-NORM-002 — Fitted-normal Cramer-von Mises p-value

`W* = W^2 (1 + 0.5/n)`;
`p = 1 - exp(-13.953 + 775.5 W* - 12542.61 W*^2)` for `W* < 0.0275`,
`1 - exp(-5.903 + 179.546 W* - 1515.29 W*^2)` for `W* < 0.051`,
`exp(0.886 - 31.62 W* + 10.897 W*^2)` for `W* < 0.092`,
`exp(1.111 - 34.242 W* + 12.832 W*^2)` for `W* < 1.1`, and `7.37e-10`
beyond.

- Domain: normal null with mean and `n - 1` standard deviation estimated;
  `n >= 8`.
- Meaning: tabulated null law of the fitted statistic, valid for every
  `n` after the modification.
- Code: `inference.normality_tests`.
- Source: [REF-STEPHENS-1986, Table 4.9] as implemented in
  [REF-GROSS-LIGGES-2015, function `cvm.test`].
  - [REF-STEPHENS-1986](../references.md#ref-stephens-1986): Michael A. Stephens. "Tests Based on EDF Statistics." In Ralph B. D'Agostino and Michael A. Stephens (eds.), *Goodness-of-Fit Techniques*. Marcel Dekker, New York, 1986. Reissued by Routledge, 2017, pages 97-194, DOI: [10.1201/9780203753064-4](https://doi.org/10.1201/9780203753064-4).
  - [REF-GROSS-LIGGES-2015](../references.md#ref-gross-ligges-2015): Juergen Gross and Uwe Ligges. *nortest: Tests for Normality*. R package version 1.0-4, 2015. [https://CRAN.R-project.org/package=nortest](https://CRAN.R-project.org/package=nortest).
- Registry: [EQ-INF-GOF-NORM-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-gof-norm-002--fitted-normal-cramer-von-mises-p-value)

### EQ-INF-GOF-NORM-003 — Fitted-normal Anderson-Darling p-value

`A* = A^2 (1 + 0.75/n + 2.25/n^2)`;
`p = 1 - exp(-13.436 + 101.14 A* - 223.73 A*^2)` for `A* < 0.2`,
`1 - exp(-8.318 + 42.796 A* - 59.938 A*^2)` for `A* < 0.34`,
`exp(0.9177 - 4.279 A* - 1.38 A*^2)` for `A* < 0.6`,
`exp(1.2937 - 5.709 A* + 0.0186 A*^2)` for `A* < 10`, and `3.7e-24` beyond.

- Domain: normal null with mean and `n - 1` standard deviation estimated;
  `n >= 8`.
- Meaning: tabulated null law of the fitted Anderson-Darling statistic.
- Code: `inference.normality_tests`.
- Source: [REF-STEPHENS-1986, Table 4.9] as implemented in
  [REF-GROSS-LIGGES-2015, function `ad.test`].
  - [REF-STEPHENS-1986](../references.md#ref-stephens-1986): Michael A. Stephens. "Tests Based on EDF Statistics." In Ralph B. D'Agostino and Michael A. Stephens (eds.), *Goodness-of-Fit Techniques*. Marcel Dekker, New York, 1986. Reissued by Routledge, 2017, pages 97-194, DOI: [10.1201/9780203753064-4](https://doi.org/10.1201/9780203753064-4).
  - [REF-GROSS-LIGGES-2015](../references.md#ref-gross-ligges-2015): Juergen Gross and Uwe Ligges. *nortest: Tests for Normality*. R package version 1.0-4, 2015. [https://CRAN.R-project.org/package=nortest](https://CRAN.R-project.org/package=nortest).
- Registry: [EQ-INF-GOF-NORM-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-gof-norm-003--fitted-normal-anderson-darling-p-value)

## `probstat.inference.power`

### EQ-INF-ES-001 — Cohen's d and Hedges' g

`d = (x_bar_1 - x_bar_2)/s_p` (two samples), `(x_bar - mu_0)/s` (one
sample), `d_bar/s_d` (paired); `g = J(m) d`,
`J(m) = Gamma(m/2)/(sqrt(m/2) Gamma((m - 1)/2)) ~ 1 - 3/(4m - 1)`.

- Domain: positive standard deviation; `m > 1` degrees of freedom.
- Meaning: scale-free effect magnitude; `g` removes the small-sample upward
  bias of `|d|`.
- Code: `inference.cohens_d`, `inference.hedges_g`,
  `inference.hedges_correction`.
- Source: [REF-COHEN-1988, Chapter 2] and [REF-HEDGES-1981].
  - [REF-COHEN-1988](../references.md#ref-cohen-1988): Jacob Cohen. *Statistical Power Analysis for the Behavioral Sciences*. 2nd edition. Lawrence Erlbaum Associates, 1988.
  - [REF-HEDGES-1981](../references.md#ref-hedges-1981): Larry V. Hedges. "Distribution Theory for Glass's Estimator of Effect Size and Related Estimators." *Journal of Educational Statistics* 6(2), 107-128, 1981.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-ES-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-es-001--cohens-d-and-hedges-g)

### EQ-INF-POWER-001 — Power of z tests

`delta = d sqrt(n)` (one-sample/paired) or `d sqrt(n/2)` (two groups of `n`);
two-sided `Phi(delta - z_(1-alpha/2)) + Phi(-delta - z_(1-alpha/2))`,
one-sided `Phi(+-delta - z_(1-alpha))`.

- Domain: known variance, standardized effect `d`.
- Meaning: probability of rejecting when the stated effect is true.
- Code: `inference.z_test_power`.
- Source: [REF-COHEN-1988, Chapter 2] and [REF-LEHMANN-ROMANO-2005, Chapter 3].
  - [REF-COHEN-1988](../references.md#ref-cohen-1988): Jacob Cohen. *Statistical Power Analysis for the Behavioral Sciences*. 2nd edition. Lawrence Erlbaum Associates, 1988.
  - [REF-LEHMANN-ROMANO-2005](../references.md#ref-lehmann-romano-2005): E. L. Lehmann and Joseph P. Romano. *Testing Statistical Hypotheses*. 3rd edition. Springer, 2005.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-POWER-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-power-001--power-of-z-tests)

### EQ-INF-POWER-002 — Exact power of t tests

`df = n - 1` or `2n - 2`; two-sided
`1 - F'_(df,delta)(t_c) + F'_(df,delta)(-t_c)` with `t_c = t_(df,1-alpha/2)`;
one-sided analogues with `t_(df,1-alpha)`; `F'` from `EQ-INF-DIST-NCT-001`.

- Domain: normal observations, `n >= 2`.
- Meaning: exact design power under the noncentral t law (no normal
  approximation, no simulation).
- Code: `inference.t_test_power`.
- Source: [REF-COHEN-1988, Chapter 2] and
  [REF-JOHNSON-KOTZ-BALAKRISHNAN-1995].
  - [REF-COHEN-1988](../references.md#ref-cohen-1988): Jacob Cohen. *Statistical Power Analysis for the Behavioral Sciences*. 2nd edition. Lawrence Erlbaum Associates, 1988.
  - [REF-JOHNSON-KOTZ-BALAKRISHNAN-1995](../references.md#ref-johnson-kotz-balakrishnan-1995): Norman L. Johnson, Samuel Kotz, and N. Balakrishnan. *Continuous Univariate Distributions, Volume 2*. 2nd edition. Wiley, 1995.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-POWER-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-power-002--exact-power-of-t-tests)

### EQ-INF-POWER-003 — Required sample size

`n* = min{n : power(n) >= 1 - beta}`, searched from the z start
`k (z_(1-alpha') + z_(1-beta))^2 / d^2` (`k = 2` for two groups).

- Domain: nonzero `d` whose sign agrees with a one-sided alternative,
  `alpha < 1 - beta < 1`.
- Meaning: smallest design achieving the target power.
- Code: `inference.required_sample_size`.
- Source: [REF-COHEN-1988, Chapter 2].
  - [REF-COHEN-1988](../references.md#ref-cohen-1988): Jacob Cohen. *Statistical Power Analysis for the Behavioral Sciences*. 2nd edition. Lawrence Erlbaum Associates, 1988.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-POWER-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-power-003--required-sample-size)

## `probstat.inference.ppl._api`

### EQ-PPL-PREDICTIVE-001 — Prior and posterior predictive simulation

Prior predictive: `x^(s) ~ p(x)` site by site, then `y^(s) ~ p(y | x^(s))`.
Posterior predictive: `y_rep^(s) ~ p(y | x^(s))` for posterior draws
`x^(s) ~ p(x | y)`, so `y_rep` has law `int p(y | x) p(x | y) dx`.

- Domain: any model whose observed sites can be sampled.
- Meaning: simulated data under the prior or the fitted model, the input of
  prior predictive checks and of [EQ-PPC-001].
- Code: `inference.ppl.prior_predictive`, `inference.ppl.posterior_predictive`,
  `inference.ppl.predictive_pvalues`.
- Source: [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013, Chapter 6], [REF-RUBIN-1984].
  - [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-gelman-etal-2013): Andrew Gelman, John B. Carlin, Hal S. Stern, David B. Dunson, Aki Vehtari, and Donald B. Rubin. *Bayesian Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. CRC Press, 2013. ISBN 978-1-4398-4095-5.
  - [REF-RUBIN-1984](../references.md#ref-rubin-1984): Donald B. Rubin. "Bayesianly Justifiable and Relevant Frequency Calculations for the Applied Statistician." *The Annals of Statistics* 12(4), 1151-1172, 1984. DOI: [10.1214/aos/1176346785](https://doi.org/10.1214/aos/1176346785).
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-PREDICTIVE-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-predictive-001--prior-and-posterior-predictive-simulation)

### EQ-PPL-PSEUDO-001 — Moment-matched normal pseudo-priors from a pilot run

For a continuous site `s` that some structure lacks, with pilot draws
`u^(1..S)` and `w_r = P(s declared | u^(r), y)` (the sum of the
configuration and branch probabilities that declare `s`), the pseudo-prior
is `q_s(u_s) = prod_k N(u_(s,k); m_k, v_k)` with
`m_k = sum_r w_r u_(s,k)^(r) / sum_r w_r` and
`v_k = sum_r w_r (u_(s,k)^(r) - m_k)^2 / sum_r w_r`. Because
`E[u 1(s declared) | y] = E[u P(s declared | u, y) | y]`, `m` and `v` are
consistent estimates of the conditional posterior moments of `u_s` given
that `s` exists.

- Domain: at least two effective pilot draws (`(sum w)^2 >= 2 sum w^2`)
  and positive `v_k`; unconstrained coordinates of the site.
- Meaning: any proper `q_s` leaves the posterior probability of every
  structure unchanged; a `q_s` close to the conditional posterior makes the
  summands of different structures overlap on `u_s`, so the sampled density
  has less separated components and the structure probability mixes faster.
  The default (no tuning) keeps the site's prior.
- Code: `inference.ppl.tune_pseudo_priors`, `inference.ppl.PseudoPrior`,
  `inference.ppl.ModelIR.with_pseudo_priors`.
- Source: [REF-CARLIN-CHIB-1995] (pp. 473-484: the pseudo-priors do not
  affect the model probabilities, and pseudo-priors close to the
  conditional posteriors make the sampler efficient),
  [REF-GODSILL-2001] (pp. 230-248: the composite-space sampler and its
  pseudo-priors).
  - [REF-CARLIN-CHIB-1995](../references.md#ref-carlin-chib-1995): Bradley P. Carlin and Siddhartha Chib. "Bayesian Model Choice via Markov Chain Monte Carlo Methods." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society, Series B (Methodological)* 57(3), 473-484, 1995. Model choice on the product space of the parameters of all candidate models, with pseudo-prior densities for the parameters of the models not in use, which do not change the posterior model probabilities. DOI: [10.1111/j.2517-6161.1995.tb02042.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.2517-6161.1995.tb02042.x).
  - [REF-GODSILL-2001](../references.md#ref-godsill-2001): Simon J. Godsill. "On the Relationship Between Markov Chain Monte Carlo Methods for Model Uncertainty." *Journal of Computational and Graphical Statistics* 10(2), 230-248, 2001. A composite model space that holds the parameters of every model and relates the product-space method of Carlin and Chib to reversible-jump samplers. DOI: [10.1198/10618600152627924](https://doi.org/10.1198/10618600152627924).
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-PSEUDO-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-pseudo-001--moment-matched-normal-pseudo-priors-from-a-pilot-run)

## `probstat.inference.ppl._discrete`

### EQ-PPL-MARKOV-002 — Smoothed marginals and forward filtering, backward sampling

With the forward variables of [EQ-PPL-MARKOV-001], the backward variables
`beta_(n-1)(j) = 0`, `beta_t(i) = log sum_j exp(l_(t+1)(i, j) +
beta_(t+1)(j))`, and `log L = log sum_j exp(alpha_(n-1)(j))`:
`p(z_t = j | theta, y) = exp(alpha_t(j) + beta_t(j) - log L)` and
`p(z_(t-1) = i, z_t = j | theta, y) = exp(alpha_(t-1)(i) + l_t(i, j) +
beta_t(j) - log L)`. A joint draw of the path takes
`z_(n-1) ~ exp(alpha_(n-1)(j) - log L)` and, for `t = n - 2, ..., 0`,
`z_t | z_(t+1) = k ~ exp(alpha_t(i) + l_(t+1)(i, k))` normalized over `i`.

- Domain: as [EQ-PPL-MARKOV-001]; `theta` fixed (one continuous draw).
- Meaning: the exact conditional law of the path given the continuous
  parameters; posterior draws of the discrete states, Rao-Blackwellized
  posterior state probabilities (the average of the smoothed marginals
  over continuous draws), and the weights of the plate iterations nested in
  a step.
- Code: `inference.ppl._discrete.backward_log`,
  `inference.ppl._discrete.recover_discrete`, `inference.ppl.DiscreteDraws`.
- Source: [REF-RABINER-1989] (pp. 257-286: the backward variables, the
  state probabilities from the product of forward and backward variables,
  and the pairwise state probabilities), [REF-CHIB-1996] (pp. 79-97: the
  joint draw of the hidden states of a Markov mixture model by a forward
  pass of filtered probabilities and a backward pass of conditional
  draws).
  - [REF-RABINER-1989](../references.md#ref-rabiner-1989): Lawrence R. Rabiner. "A Tutorial on Hidden Markov Models and Selected Applications in Speech Recognition." *Proceedings of the IEEE* 77(2), 257-286, 1989. The forward and backward procedures, the state and pairwise state probabilities, the Viterbi algorithm, and scaling for hidden Markov models. DOI: [10.1109/5.18626](https://doi.org/10.1109/5.18626).
  - [REF-CHIB-1996](../references.md#ref-chib-1996): Siddhartha Chib. "Calculating Posterior Distributions and Modal Estimates in Markov Mixture Models." *Journal of Econometrics* 75(1), 79-97, 1996. Gibbs sampling of the hidden states of a Markov mixture model as one block, by forward filtered probabilities and backward conditional draws. DOI: [10.1016/0304-4076(95)01770-4](https://doi.org/10.1016/0304-4076(95)01770-4).
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-MARKOV-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-markov-002--smoothed-marginals-and-forward-filtering-backward-sampling)

### EQ-PPL-MARKOV-003 — Viterbi recursion and joint max-product elimination

`delta_0(j) = l_0(j)`, `delta_t(j) = max_i delta_(t-1)(i) + l_t(i, j)` with
back-pointers `psi_t(j) = argmax_i (delta_(t-1)(i) + l_t(i, j))`; the most
probable path ends in `argmax_j delta_(n-1)(j)` and is read back through
`psi`. Across the whole enumeration record, each plate iteration or chain
`g` with log-sum-exp `S_g` and maximum `M_g` contributes the gain
`M_g - S_g` to the log-weight that contains `S_g`, so the jointly most
probable configuration maximizes `l_c + sum_g (M_g - S_g)` over the global
configurations `c`, with nested gains added to the branch and pair weights.

- Domain: as [EQ-PPL-MARKOV-001] and [EQ-PPL-ENUM-004]; ties go to the
  smallest index.
- Meaning: the maximum a posteriori values of every discrete site given the
  continuous parameters, at the cost of the forward pass.
- Code: `inference.ppl._discrete.viterbi`,
  `inference.ppl._discrete.most_probable`,
  `inference.ppl.ModelIR.most_probable_discrete`.
- Source: [REF-VITERBI-1967] (pp. 260-269: the maximum-likelihood decoding
  recursion), [REF-RABINER-1989] (pp. 257-286: the Viterbi algorithm for the
  single best state sequence, with back-pointers).
  - [REF-VITERBI-1967](../references.md#ref-viterbi-1967): Andrew J. Viterbi. "Error Bounds for Convolutional Codes and an Asymptotically Optimum Decoding Algorithm." *IEEE Transactions on Information Theory* 13(2), 260-269, 1967. The dynamic-programming recursion for the most probable state sequence. DOI: [10.1109/TIT.1967.1054010](https://doi.org/10.1109/TIT.1967.1054010).
  - [REF-RABINER-1989](../references.md#ref-rabiner-1989): Lawrence R. Rabiner. "A Tutorial on Hidden Markov Models and Selected Applications in Speech Recognition." *Proceedings of the IEEE* 77(2), 257-286, 1989. The forward and backward procedures, the state and pairwise state probabilities, the Viterbi algorithm, and scaling for hidden Markov models. DOI: [10.1109/5.18626](https://doi.org/10.1109/5.18626).
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-MARKOV-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-markov-003--viterbi-recursion-and-joint-max-product-elimination)

## `probstat.inference.ppl._program`

### EQ-PPL-TRANSFORM-001 — Log bijections for half-bounded supports

For a support `(a, inf)`: `x = a + exp(u)`, `log |dx/du| = u`,
`u = log(x - a)`. For `(-inf, b)`: `x = b - exp(u)`, `log |dx/du| = u`,
`u = log(b - x)`. For `R`: `x = u` with zero log-Jacobian.

- Domain: `u` in `R`; `exp(u)` overflows for `u > ~709` (mapped to a
  log-density of `-inf`).
- Meaning: a smooth bijection from `R` onto the interior of the support, so
  samplers and optimizers work on an unconstrained space; the log-Jacobian
  turns the density of `x` into a density of `u`.
- Code: `inference.ppl.Bijection`, `inference.ppl.bijection_for`.
- Source: [REF-CARPENTER-ETAL-2017], [REF-KUCUKELBIR-ETAL-2017].
  - [REF-CARPENTER-ETAL-2017](../references.md#ref-carpenter-etal-2017): Bob Carpenter, Andrew Gelman, Matthew D. Hoffman, Daniel Lee, Ben Goodrich, Michael Betancourt, Marcus A. Brubaker, Jiqiang Guo, Peter Li, and Allen Riddell. "Stan: A Probabilistic Programming Language." *Journal of Statistical Software* 76(1), 1-32, 2017. DOI: [10.18637/jss.v076.i01](https://doi.org/10.18637/jss.v076.i01).
  - [REF-KUCUKELBIR-ETAL-2017](../references.md#ref-kucukelbir-etal-2017): Alp Kucukelbir, Dustin Tran, Rajesh Ranganath, Andrew Gelman, and David M. Blei. "Automatic Differentiation Variational Inference." *Journal of Machine Learning Research* 18(14), 1-45, 2017.
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-TRANSFORM-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-transform-001--log-bijections-for-half-bounded-supports)

### EQ-PPL-TRANSFORM-002 — Scaled logit bijection for an interval

For a support `(a, b)`: `x = a + (b - a) s(u)` with `s(u) = 1/(1 + exp(-u))`,
`log |dx/du| = log(b - a) + log s(u) + log(1 - s(u))
= log(b - a) - softplus(u) - softplus(-u)`, `softplus(t) = log(1 + exp(t))`,
and `u = log(x - a) - log(b - x)`.

- Domain: `u` in `R`, `a < b` finite.
- Meaning: maps `R` onto `(a, b)`; the softplus form avoids overflow for
  large `|u|`. Closed endpoints have probability zero under a continuous law
  and are not reached.
- Code: `inference.ppl.Bijection`, `inference.ppl.bijection_for`.
- Source: [REF-CARPENTER-ETAL-2017], [REF-KUCUKELBIR-ETAL-2017].
  - [REF-CARPENTER-ETAL-2017](../references.md#ref-carpenter-etal-2017): Bob Carpenter, Andrew Gelman, Matthew D. Hoffman, Daniel Lee, Ben Goodrich, Michael Betancourt, Marcus A. Brubaker, Jiqiang Guo, Peter Li, and Allen Riddell. "Stan: A Probabilistic Programming Language." *Journal of Statistical Software* 76(1), 1-32, 2017. DOI: [10.18637/jss.v076.i01](https://doi.org/10.18637/jss.v076.i01).
  - [REF-KUCUKELBIR-ETAL-2017](../references.md#ref-kucukelbir-etal-2017): Alp Kucukelbir, Dustin Tran, Rajesh Ranganath, Andrew Gelman, and David M. Blei. "Automatic Differentiation Variational Inference." *Journal of Machine Learning Research* 18(14), 1-45, 2017.
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-TRANSFORM-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-transform-002--scaled-logit-bijection-for-an-interval)

### EQ-PPL-JOINT-001 — Joint log-density on the unconstrained space

With latent elements `x_k = T_k(u_k; x_1..x_{k-1})` in execution order and
observed data `y`,
`log p(u) = sum_k log p(x_k | x_1..x_{k-1}) + sum_i log p(y_i | x)
+ sum_k log |dT_k/du_k|`.

- Domain: `u` in `R^d`; the bijection `T_k` may depend on earlier latent
  values (bounds that are parameters), which keeps the map lower triangular
  so its log-determinant is the sum of the diagonal terms.
- Meaning: the density that the samplers, ADVI, and the Laplace
  approximation target; draws of `u` are mapped back through `T`.
- Code: `inference.ppl.ModelIR.log_density`, `inference.ppl.ModelIR.terms`.
- Source: [REF-CARPENTER-ETAL-2017], [REF-BINGHAM-ETAL-2019].
  - [REF-CARPENTER-ETAL-2017](../references.md#ref-carpenter-etal-2017): Bob Carpenter, Andrew Gelman, Matthew D. Hoffman, Daniel Lee, Ben Goodrich, Michael Betancourt, Marcus A. Brubaker, Jiqiang Guo, Peter Li, and Allen Riddell. "Stan: A Probabilistic Programming Language." *Journal of Statistical Software* 76(1), 1-32, 2017. DOI: [10.18637/jss.v076.i01](https://doi.org/10.18637/jss.v076.i01).
  - [REF-BINGHAM-ETAL-2019](../references.md#ref-bingham-etal-2019): Eli Bingham, Jonathan P. Chen, Martin Jankowiak, Fritz Obermeyer, Neeraj Pradhan, Theofanis Karaletsos, Rohit Singh, Paul Szerlip, Paul Horsfall, and Noah D. Goodman. "Pyro: Deep Universal Probabilistic Programming." *Journal of Machine Learning Research* 20(28), 1-6, 2019. [https://jmlr.org/papers/v20/18-403.html](https://jmlr.org/papers/v20/18-403.html).
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-JOINT-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-joint-001--joint-log-density-on-the-unconstrained-space)

### EQ-PPL-NONCENTERED-001 — Non-centred reparameterization of a normal site

`theta = loc + scale z`, `z ~ N(0, 1)`; the site contributes
`log N(z; 0, 1)` to the joint log-density and no Jacobian term, because `z`
is the sampled coordinate.

- Domain: `scale > 0`; `loc` and `scale` may depend on earlier latent sites.
- Meaning: the prior of the sampled coordinate no longer depends on `scale`,
  which removes the funnel between a group scale and the group effects when
  the data are weakly informative.
- Code: `inference.ppl.ModelHandle.sample` (`reparam="noncentered"`).
- Source: [REF-PAPASPILIOPOULOS-ROBERTS-SKOLD-2007],
  [REF-BETANCOURT-GIROLAMI-2013].
  - [REF-PAPASPILIOPOULOS-ROBERTS-SKOLD-2007](../references.md#ref-papaspiliopoulos-roberts-skold-2007): Omiros Papaspiliopoulos, Gareth O. Roberts, and Martin Sköld. "A General Framework for the Parametrization of Hierarchical Models." *Statistical Science* 22(1), 59-73, 2007. DOI: [10.1214/088342307000000014](https://doi.org/10.1214/088342307000000014).
  - [REF-BETANCOURT-GIROLAMI-2013](../references.md#ref-betancourt-girolami-2013): M. J. Betancourt and Mark Girolami. "Hamiltonian Monte Carlo for Hierarchical Models." arXiv preprint arXiv:1312.0906, 2013.
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-NONCENTERED-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-noncentered-001--non-centred-reparameterization-of-a-normal-site)

### EQ-PPL-ENUM-001 — Marginalization of finite discrete sites by enumeration

For discrete sites `z` with finite supports and continuous parameters
`theta`, `log p(theta, y) = log sum_z exp(log p(theta, z, y))`, evaluated as
`m + log sum_z exp(l_z - m)` with `m = max_z l_z` held constant. Global
sites are enumerated jointly (`prod_k |Z_k|` executions of the model); the
sites of plate iteration `i` are enumerated with that iteration only:
`log p(theta, y) = l_0(theta) + sum_i log sum_(z_i) exp(l_i(theta, z_i))`,
exact when the iterations are conditionally independent given `theta` and
the global sites.

- Domain: every discrete site follows the continuous latent and
  deterministic sites of its scope; at most 4096 configurations per scope.
- Meaning: the density that the gradient-based engines sample is the
  marginal of the continuous parameters, so HMC, NUTS, ADVI, and Laplace
  apply unchanged; per-iteration enumeration costs `sum_i |Z_i|` instead of
  `prod_i |Z_i|` executions.
- Code: `inference.ppl.ModelHandle.sample` (`marginalize=True`),
  `inference.ppl.ModelHandle.plate`, `inference.ppl.ModelIR.log_density`.
- Source: [REF-STAN-USERS-GUIDE-2-36] (section "The benefits of
  marginalization"), [REF-OBERMEYER-ETAL-2019] (plated variable
  elimination).
  - [REF-STAN-USERS-GUIDE-2-36](../references.md#ref-stan-users-guide-2-36): Stan Development Team. *Stan User's Guide*, Version 2.36, chapter "Latent Discrete Parameters," sections "The benefits of marginalization," "Posterior distribution of the discrete change point," and "The mathematics of recovering marginalized parameters" (marginalization by `log_sum_exp`; recovery of `Pr[Z = k | Theta]` by Bayes's theorem given the continuous draws, averaged over the draws). [https://mc-stan.org/docs/2_36/stan-users-guide/latent-discrete.html](https://mc-stan.org/docs/2_36/stan-users-guide/latent-discrete.html).
  - [REF-OBERMEYER-ETAL-2019](../references.md#ref-obermeyer-etal-2019): Fritz Obermeyer, Eli Bingham, Martin Jankowiak, Neeraj Pradhan, Justin Chiu, Alexander Rush, and Noah Goodman. "Tensor Variable Elimination for Plated Factor Graphs." In *Proceedings of the 36th International Conference on Machine Learning*, Proceedings of Machine Learning Research 97, 4871-4880, 2019. Exact inference in discrete latent-variable models with repeated (plated) structure by variable elimination. [https://proceedings.mlr.press/v97/obermeyer19a.html](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v97/obermeyer19a.html).
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-ENUM-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-enum-001--marginalization-of-finite-discrete-sites-by-enumeration)

### EQ-PPL-ENUM-002 — Recovery of marginalized discrete sites

For a draw `theta^(s)`:
`p(c | theta^(s), y) = exp(l_c - logsumexp_c' l_c')` over global
configurations `c`, and
`p(z_i | theta^(s), c, y) = exp(l_i(z_i) - logsumexp l_i)` for plate
iteration `i`. A joint draw takes `c^(s)` and then each `z_i^(s)`. The
posterior probability of a value is estimated by
`(1/S) sum_s p(z = v | theta^(s), y)` (local sites averaged over `c`), the
Rao-Blackwellized form of the indicator average.

- Domain: as [EQ-PPL-ENUM-001]; `theta^(s)` from any engine.
- Meaning: the exact conditional law gives posterior draws of the discrete
  sites for predictive simulation and lower-variance estimates of their
  posterior probabilities.
- Code: `inference.ppl.ModelIR.discrete_conditional`,
  `inference.ppl.DiscreteDraws`, `inference.ppl.PosteriorSamples.discrete_probabilities`.
- Source: [REF-STAN-USERS-GUIDE-2-36] (sections "Posterior distribution of
  the discrete change point" and "The mathematics of recovering marginalized
  parameters").
  - [REF-STAN-USERS-GUIDE-2-36](../references.md#ref-stan-users-guide-2-36): Stan Development Team. *Stan User's Guide*, Version 2.36, chapter "Latent Discrete Parameters," sections "The benefits of marginalization," "Posterior distribution of the discrete change point," and "The mathematics of recovering marginalized parameters" (marginalization by `log_sum_exp`; recovery of `Pr[Z = k | Theta]` by Bayes's theorem given the continuous draws, averaged over the draws). [https://mc-stan.org/docs/2_36/stan-users-guide/latent-discrete.html](https://mc-stan.org/docs/2_36/stan-users-guide/latent-discrete.html).
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-ENUM-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-enum-002--recovery-of-marginalized-discrete-sites)

### EQ-PPL-ENUM-003 — Joint support of a vector-valued discrete site

A discrete site `z = (z_1, ..., z_n)` of independent elements with finite
supports `Z_k` has the joint support `Z_1 x ... x Z_n` (enumerated in
lexicographic order) and log-mass `log p(z) = sum_k log p_k(z_k)`. A
multinomial count vector `n ~ Mult(N, p)` with `K` cells has the joint
support `{n in N^K : sum_k n_k = N}` of `C(N + K - 1, K - 1)` vectors and the
log-mass of [EQ-DIST-MULT-001]. The site is enumerated as one site with
these joint values in [EQ-PPL-ENUM-001].

- Domain: at most 4096 joint values per site and per scope.
- Meaning: indicators that act on one observation together (a sum, a
  pattern) are written as one vector site; the exact joint conditional law
  of the vector is recovered by [EQ-PPL-ENUM-002].
- Code: `inference.ppl.ModelHandle.sample` (`marginalize=True` with
  `shape`, a list of laws, or `multivariate.Multinomial`),
  `inference.ppl._discrete.product_support`,
  `inference.ppl._discrete.multinomial_support`.
- Source: [REF-STAN-USERS-GUIDE-2-36] (chapter "Latent Discrete
  Parameters": the marginal is a sum over every value of the discrete
  parameters), [REF-JOHNSON-KOTZ-BALAKRISHNAN-1997, Chapter 35]
  (multinomial support and mass).
  - [REF-STAN-USERS-GUIDE-2-36](../references.md#ref-stan-users-guide-2-36): Stan Development Team. *Stan User's Guide*, Version 2.36, chapter "Latent Discrete Parameters," sections "The benefits of marginalization," "Posterior distribution of the discrete change point," and "The mathematics of recovering marginalized parameters" (marginalization by `log_sum_exp`; recovery of `Pr[Z = k | Theta]` by Bayes's theorem given the continuous draws, averaged over the draws). [https://mc-stan.org/docs/2_36/stan-users-guide/latent-discrete.html](https://mc-stan.org/docs/2_36/stan-users-guide/latent-discrete.html).
  - [REF-JOHNSON-KOTZ-BALAKRISHNAN-1997](../references.md#ref-johnson-kotz-balakrishnan-1997): Norman L. Johnson, Samuel Kotz, and N. Balakrishnan. *Discrete Multivariate Distributions*. Wiley, New York, 1997. ISBN 978-0-471-12844-1.
- Also names: [`multivariate`](multivariate.md)
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-ENUM-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-enum-003--joint-support-of-a-vector-valued-discrete-site)

### EQ-PPL-ENUM-004 — Enumeration in nested plates

For outer plate iterations `g` with local discrete sites `c_g` and inner
iterations `j` with local discrete sites `z_gj`, conditionally independent
given the sites declared before them,
`log p(theta, y) = l_0(theta) + sum_g log sum_(c_g) exp(l_g(theta, c_g)
+ sum_j log sum_(z_gj) exp(l_gj(theta, c_g, z_gj)))`,
where `l_g` collects the terms of iteration `g` after its first discrete
site and `l_gj` those of inner iteration `j`. The recovery of
[EQ-PPL-ENUM-002] draws `c_g` from `p(c_g | theta, y)` and then each `z_gj`
from `p(z_gj | theta, c_g, y)`; the marginal probability of `z_gj` is
`sum_(c_g) p(c_g | theta, y) p(z_gj | theta, c_g, y)`.

- Domain: as [EQ-PPL-ENUM-001] in every scope; the sites of an iteration do
  not depend on the values of its own discrete sites.
- Meaning: the cost is `sum_g |C_g| (1 + sum_j |Z_gj|)` body executions
  instead of the product over all discrete sites, and the result equals the
  sum over every joint configuration.
- Code: `inference.ppl.ModelHandle.plate`,
  `inference.ppl.ModelIR.discrete_conditional`,
  `inference.ppl.DiscreteDraws`.
- Source: [REF-OBERMEYER-ETAL-2019] (variable elimination in plated factor
  graphs, which allows nested plates).
  - [REF-OBERMEYER-ETAL-2019](../references.md#ref-obermeyer-etal-2019): Fritz Obermeyer, Eli Bingham, Martin Jankowiak, Neeraj Pradhan, Justin Chiu, Alexander Rush, and Noah Goodman. "Tensor Variable Elimination for Plated Factor Graphs." In *Proceedings of the 36th International Conference on Machine Learning*, Proceedings of Machine Learning Research 97, 4871-4880, 2019. Exact inference in discrete latent-variable models with repeated (plated) structure by variable elimination. [https://proceedings.mlr.press/v97/obermeyer19a.html](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v97/obermeyer19a.html).
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-ENUM-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-enum-004--enumeration-in-nested-plates)

### EQ-PPL-DYNAMIC-001 — Padded density under dynamic site structure

Let the configurations `c` of the discrete sites outside plates declare the
continuous latent sites `S_c`, and let `u` hold the union `S` of all `S_c`.
For a site `s` in `S` but not in `S_c`, let `q_s(u_s | u_<s)` be the density
on `R^(d_s)` of site `s` (prior times Jacobian) in the first configuration
`r(s)` that declares it. Then
`log p(u) = log sum_c exp(log p(c, T_c(u_(S_c)), y) + log |J_c(u)|
+ sum_(s not in S_c) log q_s(u_s | u_<s))`.
Each `q_s` integrates to one over `u_s`, so `int p_c(u) du = p(c, y)` for
every `c`: the marginal of the configurations and the conditional law of
the sites `S_c` given `c` are those of the model, and `p(c | u, y)` is
proportional to the summand of `c`.

- Domain: every prior of a continuous site is proper; at most 4096
  configurations; the structure depends on data and on discrete sites
  outside plates, not on continuous values.
- Meaning: the product-space (composite-space) construction with each
  site's own prior as its pseudo-prior; the engines sample a fixed-dimension
  density, and the posterior probability of a structure is the average of
  `p(c | u^(s), y)` over the draws.
- Code: `inference.ppl.trace`, `inference.ppl.TracedStructure`,
  `inference.ppl.ModelIR.log_density`,
  `inference.ppl.PosteriorSamples.presence_probability`.
- Source: [REF-CARLIN-CHIB-1995] (pp. 473-484: the product space of the
  parameters of all models, with pseudo-priors for the parameters of the
  models not in use that leave the posterior model probabilities
  unchanged), [REF-GODSILL-2001] (pp. 230-248: the composite model space
  that contains the Carlin-Chib method).
  - [REF-CARLIN-CHIB-1995](../references.md#ref-carlin-chib-1995): Bradley P. Carlin and Siddhartha Chib. "Bayesian Model Choice via Markov Chain Monte Carlo Methods." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society, Series B (Methodological)* 57(3), 473-484, 1995. Model choice on the product space of the parameters of all candidate models, with pseudo-prior densities for the parameters of the models not in use, which do not change the posterior model probabilities. DOI: [10.1111/j.2517-6161.1995.tb02042.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.2517-6161.1995.tb02042.x).
  - [REF-GODSILL-2001](../references.md#ref-godsill-2001): Simon J. Godsill. "On the Relationship Between Markov Chain Monte Carlo Methods for Model Uncertainty." *Journal of Computational and Graphical Statistics* 10(2), 230-248, 2001. A composite model space that holds the parameters of every model and relates the product-space method of Carlin and Chib to reversible-jump samplers. DOI: [10.1198/10618600152627924](https://doi.org/10.1198/10618600152627924).
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-DYNAMIC-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-dynamic-001--padded-density-under-dynamic-site-structure)

### EQ-PPL-MARKOV-001 — Forward algorithm for a Markov chain of discrete sites

For a chain of steps `t = 0, ..., n - 1` whose discrete sites take the joint
states `j in {1, ..., K_t}`, let `l_0(j)` collect the log-terms of step `0`
in state `j` and `l_t(i, j)` those of step `t` when step `t - 1` is in state
`i` (discrete log-masses, observations, and the log-sum-exp of nested plate
iterations). Then `alpha_0(j) = l_0(j)`,
`alpha_t(j) = log sum_i exp(alpha_(t-1)(i) + l_t(i, j))`, and
`log sum_(z_0..z_(n-1)) exp(l_0(z_0) + sum_t l_t(z_(t-1), z_t))
= log sum_j exp(alpha_(n-1)(j))`, evaluated with a shifted log-sum-exp.

- Domain: first-order dependence (the terms of step `t` depend on the
  discrete sites of steps `t - 1` and `t` only); at most 4096 states per
  step; the sites of a step do not change with its discrete values.
- Meaning: the chain is summed out in `K_0 + sum_(t >= 1) K_(t-1) K_t`
  executions of the step body instead of `prod_t K_t`, so the
  gradient-based engines sample the marginal density of the continuous
  parameters of a hidden Markov model; the recursion is transparent to dual
  numbers and reverse-mode variables.
- Code: `inference.ppl.ModelHandle.markov`,
  `inference.ppl._discrete.forward_log`, `inference.ppl.ModelIR.log_density`.
- Source: [REF-RABINER-1989] (pp. 257-286: the forward procedure, an
  induction over time on the forward variables at `O(n K^2)` cost),
  [REF-BAUM-ETAL-1970] (pp. 164-171: the forward and backward recursions
  for probabilistic functions of Markov chains).
  - [REF-RABINER-1989](../references.md#ref-rabiner-1989): Lawrence R. Rabiner. "A Tutorial on Hidden Markov Models and Selected Applications in Speech Recognition." *Proceedings of the IEEE* 77(2), 257-286, 1989. The forward and backward procedures, the state and pairwise state probabilities, the Viterbi algorithm, and scaling for hidden Markov models. DOI: [10.1109/5.18626](https://doi.org/10.1109/5.18626).
  - [REF-BAUM-ETAL-1970](../references.md#ref-baum-etal-1970): Leonard E. Baum, Ted Petrie, George Soules, and Norman Weiss. "A Maximization Technique Occurring in the Statistical Analysis of Probabilistic Functions of Markov Chains." *The Annals of Mathematical Statistics* 41(1), 164-171, 1970. The forward and backward recursions for the likelihood of a hidden Markov chain and the re-estimation procedure built on them. DOI: [10.1214/aoms/1177697196](https://doi.org/10.1214/aoms/1177697196).
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-MARKOV-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-markov-001--forward-algorithm-for-a-markov-chain-of-discrete-sites)

### EQ-PPL-LOCAL-001 — Per-iteration padding of plate branches

Let the branches `z` of plate iteration `i` declare, after its first
discrete site, the continuous latent sites `S_i(z)`, and let
`S_i = union_z S_i(z)`. For `s` in `S_i` but not in `S_i(z)`, let
`q_s(u_s | u_<s)` be the prior times Jacobian of `s` in the first branch
that declares it (or a tuned pseudo-prior, [EQ-PPL-PSEUDO-001]). Then the
iteration contributes
`log sum_z exp(l_i(z, u) + sum_(s in S_i, s not in S_i(z)) log q_s(u_s))`
to the log-density, where `l_i(z, u)` holds the terms of branch `z` after
its first discrete site (the priors and log-Jacobians of `S_i(z)`
included). Nested iterations pad their own branches, and their union of
sites is declared by the enclosing branch.

- Domain: the discrete sites of an iteration and their supports are the same
  in every branch; a site declared in several branches has the same support
  and transform in each; at most 4096 branches per iteration; the structure
  of a branch depends on the iteration's discrete values, data, and the
  enclosing sites, not on continuous values; every padding density is
  proper.
- Meaning: each `q_s` integrates to one, so integrating `u_(S_i)` out of the
  summand of `z` gives `p(z, y_i | theta)` for every branch: the product-space
  construction of [EQ-PPL-DYNAMIC-001] applied inside each conditionally
  independent iteration. Recovery reports a site of `S_i` only in the draws
  whose drawn branch declares it.
- Code: `inference.ppl.ModelHandle.plate`, `inference.ppl.TracedStructure`,
  `inference.ppl.PosteriorSamples.present`,
  `inference.ppl.PosteriorSamples.presence_probability`.
- Source: [REF-CARLIN-CHIB-1995] (pp. 473-484: pseudo-priors for the
  parameters of the models not in use leave the posterior model
  probabilities unchanged), [REF-OBERMEYER-ETAL-2019] (plated variable
  elimination: each iteration is summed out on its own).
  - [REF-CARLIN-CHIB-1995](../references.md#ref-carlin-chib-1995): Bradley P. Carlin and Siddhartha Chib. "Bayesian Model Choice via Markov Chain Monte Carlo Methods." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society, Series B (Methodological)* 57(3), 473-484, 1995. Model choice on the product space of the parameters of all candidate models, with pseudo-prior densities for the parameters of the models not in use, which do not change the posterior model probabilities. DOI: [10.1111/j.2517-6161.1995.tb02042.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.2517-6161.1995.tb02042.x).
  - [REF-OBERMEYER-ETAL-2019](../references.md#ref-obermeyer-etal-2019): Fritz Obermeyer, Eli Bingham, Martin Jankowiak, Neeraj Pradhan, Justin Chiu, Alexander Rush, and Noah Goodman. "Tensor Variable Elimination for Plated Factor Graphs." In *Proceedings of the 36th International Conference on Machine Learning*, Proceedings of Machine Learning Research 97, 4871-4880, 2019. Exact inference in discrete latent-variable models with repeated (plated) structure by variable elimination. [https://proceedings.mlr.press/v97/obermeyer19a.html](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v97/obermeyer19a.html).
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-LOCAL-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-local-001--per-iteration-padding-of-plate-branches)

## `probstat.inference.ppl._structured`

### EQ-PPL-TRANSFORM-003 — Stick-breaking bijection onto the simplex

For `y` in `R^(K-1)` and one-based `k = 1..K-1`:
`z_k = logistic(y_k - log(K - k))`, `x_k = r_(k-1) z_k`,
`r_k = r_(k-1) (1 - z_k)` with `r_0 = 1`, and `x_K = r_(K-1)`. The absolute
Jacobian determinant of `y -> (x_1, ..., x_(K-1))` is
`prod_k z_k (1 - z_k) r_(k-1)`. Logarithms are carried through the map
(`log z_k = -softplus(-t)`, `log(1 - z_k) = -softplus(t)`), so
`log x_k = log r_(k-1) + log z_k` is exact even when `x_k` underflows.

- Domain: `K >= 2`; `y = 0` maps to the centre `x = (1/K, ..., 1/K)`.
- Meaning: a smooth bijection from `R^(K-1)` onto the open simplex; a
  density on the first `K - 1` coordinates (the Dirichlet convention of
  [EQ-DIST-DIR-001]) becomes a density on `y` after adding the log-Jacobian.
- Code: `inference.ppl.StructuredSupport` (kind `"simplex"`), used for
  `multivariate.Dirichlet` sites.
- Source: [REF-STAN-REFERENCE-MANUAL-2-36] (section "Unit simplex").
  - [REF-STAN-REFERENCE-MANUAL-2-36](../references.md#ref-stan-reference-manual-2-36): Stan Development Team. *Stan Reference Manual*, Version 2.36, chapter "Constraint Transforms," sections "Ordered vector," "Unit simplex" (stick-breaking inverse transform and its Jacobian determinant), "Covariance matrices," and "Cholesky factors of correlation matrices." From version 2.37 the manual replaces the stick-breaking simplex transform by a softmax of a sum-to-zero vector; the stick-breaking form is the one cited. [https://mc-stan.org/docs/2_36/reference-manual/transforms.html](https://mc-stan.org/docs/2_36/reference-manual/transforms.html).
- Also names: [`multivariate`](multivariate.md)
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-TRANSFORM-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-transform-003--stick-breaking-bijection-onto-the-simplex)

### EQ-PPL-TRANSFORM-004 — Ordered-vector bijection and the order-statistics density

`x_1 = y_1` (or `x_1 = a + exp(y_1)` on `(a, inf)`),
`x_k = x_(k-1) + exp(y_k)` for `k >= 2`;
`log |det J| = sum_(k>=2) y_k` (plus `y_1` with the lower bound). The law of
the sorted vector of `n` i.i.d. draws from a density `f` has density
`n! prod_i f(x_i)` on `x_1 < ... < x_n`.

- Domain: `n >= 2`; the base law has support `R` or `(a, inf)`.
- Meaning: maps `R^n` onto strictly increasing vectors; the order-statistics
  density gives a proper prior that removes the label symmetry of mixture
  components.
- Code: `inference.ppl.StructuredSupport` (kind `"ordered"`),
  `inference.ppl.OrderedVector`.
- Source: [REF-STAN-REFERENCE-MANUAL-2-36] (section "Ordered vector") and
  [REF-DAVID-NAGARAJA-2003, Chapter 2] (joint density of order statistics).
  - [REF-STAN-REFERENCE-MANUAL-2-36](../references.md#ref-stan-reference-manual-2-36): Stan Development Team. *Stan Reference Manual*, Version 2.36, chapter "Constraint Transforms," sections "Ordered vector," "Unit simplex" (stick-breaking inverse transform and its Jacobian determinant), "Covariance matrices," and "Cholesky factors of correlation matrices." From version 2.37 the manual replaces the stick-breaking simplex transform by a softmax of a sum-to-zero vector; the stick-breaking form is the one cited. [https://mc-stan.org/docs/2_36/reference-manual/transforms.html](https://mc-stan.org/docs/2_36/reference-manual/transforms.html).
  - [REF-DAVID-NAGARAJA-2003](../references.md#ref-david-nagaraja-2003): H. A. David and H. N. Nagaraja. *Order Statistics*. Third edition. Wiley Series in Probability and Statistics. John Wiley & Sons, Hoboken, NJ, 2003.
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-TRANSFORM-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-transform-004--ordered-vector-bijection-and-the-order-statistics-density)

### EQ-PPL-TRANSFORM-005 — Tanh canonical-partial-correlation bijection onto correlation factors

The `d (d - 1) / 2` coordinates `y_ij` (`i > j`, row by row) give
`z_ij = tanh(y_ij)`; row `i` of the lower-triangular factor is
`L_ij = z_ij sqrt(r_ij)` with `r_ij = 1 - sum_(j'<j) L_ij'^2` (computed as
`prod_(j'<j) (1 - z_ij'^2)`) and `L_ii = sqrt(r_ii)`. The log absolute
Jacobian onto the strictly lower entries of `L` is
`sum log(1 - z_ij^2) + (1/2) sum_(i>j) log r_ij`, with
`log(1 - tanh(y)^2) = 2 log 2 - 2|y| - 2 log1p(exp(-2|y|))`. For the
correlation matrix `Omega = L L'` the map from the strictly lower entries of
`L` to those of `Omega` adds `sum_(k=2..d) (d - k) log L_kk`.

- Domain: `d >= 2`; `L L'` has a unit diagonal and is positive definite.
- Meaning: the `z_ij` are canonical partial correlations; a smooth bijection
  from `R^(d(d-1)/2)` onto Cholesky factors of correlation matrices and onto
  correlation matrices.
- Code: `inference.ppl.StructuredSupport` (kinds `"cholesky_correlation"`
  and `"correlation"`), `multivariate.LKJCholesky.sample`.
- Source: [REF-STAN-REFERENCE-MANUAL-2-36] (section "Cholesky factors of
  correlation matrices"); the `L -> L L'` factor is the ratio of the two
  densities in [REF-STAN-FUNCTIONS-REFERENCE-2-36] ([EQ-DIST-LKJ-002]).
  - [REF-STAN-REFERENCE-MANUAL-2-36](../references.md#ref-stan-reference-manual-2-36): Stan Development Team. *Stan Reference Manual*, Version 2.36, chapter "Constraint Transforms," sections "Ordered vector," "Unit simplex" (stick-breaking inverse transform and its Jacobian determinant), "Covariance matrices," and "Cholesky factors of correlation matrices." From version 2.37 the manual replaces the stick-breaking simplex transform by a softmax of a sum-to-zero vector; the stick-breaking form is the one cited. [https://mc-stan.org/docs/2_36/reference-manual/transforms.html](https://mc-stan.org/docs/2_36/reference-manual/transforms.html).
  - [REF-STAN-FUNCTIONS-REFERENCE-2-36](../references.md#ref-stan-functions-reference-2-36): Stan Development Team. *Stan Functions Reference*, Version 2.36, chapter "Correlation Matrix Distributions," sections "LKJ correlation distribution" (`LkjCorr(Sigma | eta)` proportional to `det(Sigma)^(eta - 1)`) and "Cholesky LKJ correlation distribution" (`LkjCholesky(L | eta)` proportional to `prod_(k=2..K) L_kk^(K - k + 2 eta - 2)`). [https://mc-stan.org/docs/2_36/functions-reference/correlation_matrix_distributions.html](https://mc-stan.org/docs/2_36/functions-reference/correlation_matrix_distributions.html).
- Also names: [`multivariate`](multivariate.md)
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-TRANSFORM-005](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-transform-005--tanh-canonical-partial-correlation-bijection-onto-correlation-factors)

### EQ-PPL-TRANSFORM-006 — Cholesky bijection with a log diagonal onto covariance matrices

The `d (d + 1) / 2` coordinates (row by row, `j <= i`) give
`L_ii = exp(y_ii)`, `L_ij = y_ij` for `i > j`, and `Sigma = L L'`. The
absolute Jacobian determinant onto the lower entries of `Sigma` is
`2^d prod_(k=1..d) L_kk^(d - k + 2)` (one-based `k`).

- Domain: `d >= 2`; `Sigma` is symmetric positive definite.
- Meaning: a smooth bijection from `R^(d(d+1)/2)` onto covariance matrices;
  Wishart and inverse-Wishart densities ([EQ-DIST-WISH-001],
  [EQ-DIST-IWISH-001]) on the lower entries become densities on `y`.
- Code: `inference.ppl.StructuredSupport` (kind `"covariance"`).
- Source: [REF-STAN-REFERENCE-MANUAL-2-36] (section "Covariance matrices").
  - [REF-STAN-REFERENCE-MANUAL-2-36](../references.md#ref-stan-reference-manual-2-36): Stan Development Team. *Stan Reference Manual*, Version 2.36, chapter "Constraint Transforms," sections "Ordered vector," "Unit simplex" (stick-breaking inverse transform and its Jacobian determinant), "Covariance matrices," and "Cholesky factors of correlation matrices." From version 2.37 the manual replaces the stick-breaking simplex transform by a softmax of a sum-to-zero vector; the stick-breaking form is the one cited. [https://mc-stan.org/docs/2_36/reference-manual/transforms.html](https://mc-stan.org/docs/2_36/reference-manual/transforms.html).
- Registry: [EQ-PPL-TRANSFORM-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppl-transform-006--cholesky-bijection-with-a-log-diagonal-onto-covariance-matrices)

## `probstat.inference.predictive_checks`

### EQ-PPC-001 — Simulation-based posterior predictive p-value

`p_B = (1/S) sum_s 1[T(y_rep^s, theta^s) >= T(y, theta^s)]`,
`y_rep^s ~ p(y | theta^s)`; lower tail with `<=`; Monte Carlo standard error
`sqrt(p_B (1 - p_B) / S_eff)` with `S_eff` the effective sample size of the
indicator chains (or `S` for independent draws).

- Domain: posterior draws `theta^s`, a simulator, data-only statistics
  `T(y)` or realized discrepancies `T(y, theta)`.
- Meaning: tail probability of the observed test quantity under the
  posterior predictive law. Not uniform under a correct model in general
  (conservative toward 0.5); uniform when `T`'s sampling law is free of the
  unknown parameters.
- Code: `inference.posterior_predictive_pvalues`.
- Source: [REF-RUBIN-1984], [REF-GELMAN-MENG-STERN-1996],
  [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013, Chapter 6].
  - [REF-RUBIN-1984](../references.md#ref-rubin-1984): Donald B. Rubin. "Bayesianly Justifiable and Relevant Frequency Calculations for the Applied Statistician." *The Annals of Statistics* 12(4), 1151-1172, 1984. DOI: [10.1214/aos/1176346785](https://doi.org/10.1214/aos/1176346785).
  - [REF-GELMAN-MENG-STERN-1996](../references.md#ref-gelman-meng-stern-1996): Andrew Gelman, Xiao-Li Meng, and Hal Stern. "Posterior Predictive Assessment of Model Fitness via Realized Discrepancies." *Statistica Sinica* 6(4), 733-807 (with discussion), 1996.
  - [REF-GELMAN-ETAL-2013](../references.md#ref-gelman-etal-2013): Andrew Gelman, John B. Carlin, Hal S. Stern, David B. Dunson, Aki Vehtari, and Donald B. Rubin. *Bayesian Data Analysis*. 3rd edition. CRC Press, 2013. ISBN 978-1-4398-4095-5.
- Registry: [EQ-PPC-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppc-001--simulation-based-posterior-predictive-p-value)

### EQ-PPC-002 — Probability integral transform from replicates

`PIT_i = (#{s : y_rep,i^s < y_i} + c_i #{s : y_rep,i^s = y_i}) / S`, with
`c_i = 1/2` or `c_i ~ Uniform(0, 1)` (randomized PIT).

- Domain: `S >= 1` replicated datasets aligned with the observations.
- Meaning: uniform for a calibrated predictive law of independent outcomes.
  When the replicates come from a posterior fitted to the same data (not
  leave-one-out), PIT values are pulled toward `1/2` and a KS test of
  uniformity is descriptive only.
- Code: `inference.pit_values`, `inference.pit_uniformity`.
- Source: [REF-DAWID-1984], [REF-GNEITING-BALABDAOUI-RAFTERY-2007].
  - [REF-DAWID-1984](../references.md#ref-dawid-1984): A. P. Dawid. "Present Position and Potential Developments: Some Personal Views. Statistical Theory: The Prequential Approach." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series A* 147(2), 278-292 (with discussion), 1984. JSTOR 2981683.
  - [REF-GNEITING-BALABDAOUI-RAFTERY-2007](../references.md#ref-gneiting-balabdaoui-raftery-2007): Tilmann Gneiting, Fadoua Balabdaoui, and Adrian E. Raftery. "Probabilistic Forecasts, Calibration and Sharpness." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B* 69(2), 243-268, 2007. DOI: [10.1111/j.1467-9868.2007.00587.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1467-9868.2007.00587.x).
- Registry: [EQ-PPC-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-ppc-002--probability-integral-transform-from-replicates)

## `probstat.inference.rank_tests`

### EQ-INF-MWU-001 — Mann-Whitney U statistic and null law

`U = R_1 - m(m+1)/2` (mid-ranks); exact counts are the coefficients of
`prod_{i=1}^{m} (1 - q^(n+i))/(1 - q^i)`; normal approximation
`E U = mn/2`, `Var U = (mn/12)(N + 1 - sum(t^3 - t)/(N(N - 1)))`, with a
half-unit continuity correction; rank-biserial `r = 2U/(mn) - 1`.

- Domain: `m, n >= 1`; exact law only without ties.
- Meaning: `U/(mn)` estimates `P(X > Y) + P(X = Y)/2`.
- Code: `inference.mann_whitney_u_test`, `inference.mann_whitney_u_distribution`.
- Source: [REF-MANN-WHITNEY-1947] and
  [REF-HOLLANDER-WOLFE-CHICKEN-2014, Chapter 4].
  - [REF-MANN-WHITNEY-1947](../references.md#ref-mann-whitney-1947): H. B. Mann and D. R. Whitney. "On a Test of Whether One of Two Random Variables Is Stochastically Larger than the Other." *The Annals of Mathematical Statistics* 18(1), 50-60, 1947.
  - [REF-HOLLANDER-WOLFE-CHICKEN-2014](../references.md#ref-hollander-wolfe-chicken-2014): Myles Hollander, Douglas A. Wolfe, and Eric Chicken. *Nonparametric Statistical Methods*. 3rd edition. Wiley, 2014.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-MWU-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-mwu-001--mann-whitney-u-statistic-and-null-law)

### EQ-INF-WSR-001 — Wilcoxon signed-rank statistic and null law

Zero differences dropped; `W+ = sum_{d_i > 0} rank(|d_i|)`; exact counts are
the coefficients of `prod_{k=1}^{n} (1 + q^k)`; normal approximation
`E W+ = n(n+1)/4`, `Var W+ = n(n+1)(2n+1)/24 - sum(t^3 - t)/48`, with a
half-unit continuity correction; rank-biserial `(W+ - W-)/(W+ + W-)`.

- Domain: `n >= 1` non-zero differences; exact law only without ties.
- Meaning: tests symmetry of differences about the null location.
- Code: `inference.wilcoxon_signed_rank_test`,
  `inference.signed_rank_distribution`.
- Source: [REF-WILCOXON-1945] and [REF-HOLLANDER-WOLFE-CHICKEN-2014, Chapter 3].
  - [REF-WILCOXON-1945](../references.md#ref-wilcoxon-1945): Frank Wilcoxon. "Individual Comparisons by Ranking Methods." *Biometrics Bulletin* 1(6), 80-83, 1945.
  - [REF-HOLLANDER-WOLFE-CHICKEN-2014](../references.md#ref-hollander-wolfe-chicken-2014): Myles Hollander, Douglas A. Wolfe, and Eric Chicken. *Nonparametric Statistical Methods*. 3rd edition. Wiley, 2014.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-WSR-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-wsr-001--wilcoxon-signed-rank-statistic-and-null-law)

## `probstat.inference.regression`

### EQ-REG-LINE-001 — Straight-line response model

`E[Y | x] = alpha + beta x`.

- Domain: finite scalar predictor `x` and finite coefficients `alpha`, `beta`.
- Meaning: conditional response mean for simple linear calibration.
- Code: `inference.LinearRegressionResult.predict` and
  `inverse.LinearCalibrationResult.design_rows`.
- Source: [REF-MONTGOMERY-PECK-VINING-2021, Chapter 1].
  - [REF-MONTGOMERY-PECK-VINING-2021](../references.md#ref-montgomery-peck-vining-2021): Douglas C. Montgomery, Elizabeth A. Peck, and G. Geoffrey Vining. *Introduction to Linear Regression Analysis*. 6th edition. Wiley, 2021. ISBN 978-1-119-57872-7.
- Also names: [`inverse`](inverse.md)
- Registry: [EQ-REG-LINE-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-reg-line-001--straight-line-response-model)

### EQ-REG-OLS-001 — Simple linear least-squares estimates

`beta_hat = sum((x_i-x_bar)(y_i-y_bar)) / sum((x_i-x_bar)^2)` and
`alpha_hat = y_bar - beta_hat x_bar`.

- Domain: at least three paired finite observations with non-constant
  predictors.
- Meaning: the unique intercept and slope minimizing residual sum of squares.
- Code: `inference.fit_simple_linear_regression`.
- Source: [REF-MONTGOMERY-PECK-VINING-2021, Chapters 1-2].
  - [REF-MONTGOMERY-PECK-VINING-2021](../references.md#ref-montgomery-peck-vining-2021): Douglas C. Montgomery, Elizabeth A. Peck, and G. Geoffrey Vining. *Introduction to Linear Regression Analysis*. 6th edition. Wiley, 2021. ISBN 978-1-119-57872-7.
- Registry: [EQ-REG-OLS-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-reg-ols-001--simple-linear-least-squares-estimates)

### EQ-REG-DIAG-001 — Regression residual variance and coefficient of determination

`s^2 = SSE/(n-2)` and `R^2 = 1 - SSE/SST`.

- Domain: [EQ-REG-OLS-001]; `R^2=1` by convention for a constant response
  fitted exactly.
- Meaning: residual scale estimate and in-sample variance-explanation summary.
- Code: `inference.fit_simple_linear_regression`.
- Source: [REF-MONTGOMERY-PECK-VINING-2021, Chapter 2].
  - [REF-MONTGOMERY-PECK-VINING-2021](../references.md#ref-montgomery-peck-vining-2021): Douglas C. Montgomery, Elizabeth A. Peck, and G. Geoffrey Vining. *Introduction to Linear Regression Analysis*. 6th edition. Wiley, 2021. ISBN 978-1-119-57872-7.
- Registry: [EQ-REG-DIAG-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-reg-diag-001--regression-residual-variance-and-coefficient-of-determination)

## `probstat.inference.regularization`

### EQ-PEN-STD-001 — Standardization for penalized regression

`x~_ij = (x_ij - x_bar_j)/s_j`, `s_j^2 = (1/n) sum_i (x_ij - x_bar_j)^2`,
`y_c = y - y_bar`; the unpenalized intercept is
`b_0 = y_bar - sum_j b_j x_bar_j` with original-scale slopes
`b_j = b~_j / s_j`.

- Domain: every predictor varies (`s_j > 0`); without an intercept no
  centring is done and `s_j^2 = (1/n) sum x_ij^2`.
- Meaning: the penalty acts on comparable scales, and the intercept is not
  shrunk.
- Code: `inference.fit_ridge`, `inference.fit_elastic_net`,
  `inference.elastic_net_path`, `inference.cross_validate_elastic_net`.
- Source: [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009, Section 3.4],
  [REF-FRIEDMAN-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-2010, Section 2].
