  - [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009](../references.md#ref-hastie-tibshirani-friedman-2009): Trevor Hastie, Robert Tibshirani, and Jerome Friedman. *The Elements of Statistical Learning: Data Mining, Inference, and Prediction*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2009.
  - [REF-FRIEDMAN-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-2010](../references.md#ref-friedman-hastie-tibshirani-2010): Jerome Friedman, Trevor Hastie, and Robert Tibshirani. "Regularization Paths for Generalized Linear Models via Coordinate Descent." *Journal of Statistical Software* 33(1), 1-22, 2010.
- Registry: [EQ-PEN-STD-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-pen-std-001--standardization-for-penalized-regression)

### EQ-PEN-RIDGE-001 — Ridge regression and effective degrees of freedom

`b~ = argmin ||y_c - X~b||^2 + lambda ||b||^2 = (X~'X~ + lambda I)^-1 X~'y_c`,
solved as least squares on `[X~; sqrt(lambda) I]`;
`df(lambda) = tr[X~(X~'X~ + lambda I)^-1 X~'] = sum_j d_j^2/(d_j^2 + lambda)`
with singular values `d_j` of `X~`.

- Domain: `lambda >= 0`; `lambda > 0` makes any design full rank; `lambda = 0`
  is OLS and gives `df = p`.
- Meaning: shrinkage that trades bias for variance; `df` counts the effective
  number of fitted parameters (intercept excluded).
- Code: `inference.fit_ridge`, `inference.RidgeResult.predict`.
- Source: [REF-HOERL-KENNARD-1970],
  [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009, Section 3.4.1].
  - [REF-HOERL-KENNARD-1970](../references.md#ref-hoerl-kennard-1970): Arthur E. Hoerl and Robert W. Kennard. "Ridge Regression: Biased Estimation for Nonorthogonal Problems." *Technometrics* 12(1), 55-67, 1970.
  - [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009](../references.md#ref-hastie-tibshirani-friedman-2009): Trevor Hastie, Robert Tibshirani, and Jerome Friedman. *The Elements of Statistical Learning: Data Mining, Inference, and Prediction*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2009.
- Registry: [EQ-PEN-RIDGE-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-pen-ridge-001--ridge-regression-and-effective-degrees-of-freedom)

### EQ-PEN-EN-001 — Elastic net and its coordinate-descent update

Minimize `(1/2n)||y_c - X~b||^2 + lambda[(1-alpha)/2 ||b||^2 + alpha ||b||_1]`
by cycling `b_j <- S(rho_j, lambda alpha)/(v_j + lambda(1 - alpha))`, with
`rho_j = (1/n) x~_j'(y_c - X~b) + v_j b_j`, `v_j = (1/n) x~_j'x~_j`, and
`S(z, g) = sign(z) max(|z| - g, 0)`.

- Domain: `lambda >= 0`, `0 <= alpha <= 1`; `alpha = 1` is the lasso and
  `alpha = 0` equals ridge [EQ-PEN-RIDGE-001] with penalty `n lambda`.
- Meaning: exact coordinatewise minimization of a convex objective; the soft
  threshold sets small coefficients exactly to zero.
- Code: `inference.fit_elastic_net`, `inference.elastic_net_path`,
  `inference.ElasticNetResult.predict`.
- Source: [REF-TIBSHIRANI-1996], [REF-ZOU-HASTIE-2005],
  [REF-FRIEDMAN-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-2010, Section 2].
  - [REF-TIBSHIRANI-1996](../references.md#ref-tibshirani-1996): Robert Tibshirani. "Regression Shrinkage and Selection via the Lasso." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B* 58(1), 267-288, 1996.
  - [REF-ZOU-HASTIE-2005](../references.md#ref-zou-hastie-2005): Hui Zou and Trevor Hastie. "Regularization and Variable Selection via the Elastic Net." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B* 67(2), 301-320, 2005.
  - [REF-FRIEDMAN-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-2010](../references.md#ref-friedman-hastie-tibshirani-2010): Jerome Friedman, Trevor Hastie, and Robert Tibshirani. "Regularization Paths for Generalized Linear Models via Coordinate Descent." *Journal of Statistical Software* 33(1), 1-22, 2010.
- Registry: [EQ-PEN-EN-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-pen-en-001--elastic-net-and-its-coordinate-descent-update)

### EQ-PEN-KKT-001 — Elastic-net optimality (KKT) conditions and lambda_max

With `g_j = (1/n) x~_j'(y_c - X~b) - lambda(1-alpha) b_j`:
`g_j = lambda alpha sign(b_j)` when `b_j != 0` and `|g_j| <= lambda alpha`
when `b_j = 0`. Hence `b = 0` is optimal iff
`lambda >= lambda_max = max_j |x~_j'y_c|/(n alpha)`.

- Domain: [EQ-PEN-EN-001] with `alpha > 0` for `lambda_max`.
- Meaning: certificate of optimality checked by the tests, and the start of
  the default log-spaced penalty path.
- Code: `inference.elastic_net_path`, `inference.fit_elastic_net`.
- Source: [REF-FRIEDMAN-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-2010, Section 2].
  - [REF-FRIEDMAN-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-2010](../references.md#ref-friedman-hastie-tibshirani-2010): Jerome Friedman, Trevor Hastie, and Robert Tibshirani. "Regularization Paths for Generalized Linear Models via Coordinate Descent." *Journal of Statistical Software* 33(1), 1-22, 2010.
- Registry: [EQ-PEN-KKT-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-pen-kkt-001--elastic-net-optimality-kkt-conditions-and-lambda_max)

### EQ-PEN-CV-001 — K-fold cross-validation and the one-standard-error rule

`CV(lambda) = (1/K) sum_k MSE_k(lambda)`, `SE(lambda) = sd_k(MSE_k)/sqrt(K)`;
`lambda_min = argmin CV` and `lambda_1se` is the largest `lambda` with
`CV(lambda) <= CV(lambda_min) + SE(lambda_min)`. Folds assign the `i`-th
element of a seeded permutation to fold `i mod K`.

- Domain: `2 <= K <= n`; each training part is re-standardized on its own.
- Meaning: an estimate of out-of-sample prediction error along the path and
  a deliberately more parsimonious choice within its noise.
- Code: `inference.cross_validate_elastic_net`, `inference.kfold_assignment`.
- Source: [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009, Section 7.10],
  [REF-FRIEDMAN-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-2010].
  - [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009](../references.md#ref-hastie-tibshirani-friedman-2009): Trevor Hastie, Robert Tibshirani, and Jerome Friedman. *The Elements of Statistical Learning: Data Mining, Inference, and Prediction*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2009.
  - [REF-FRIEDMAN-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-2010](../references.md#ref-friedman-hastie-tibshirani-2010): Jerome Friedman, Trevor Hastie, and Robert Tibshirani. "Regularization Paths for Generalized Linear Models via Coordinate Descent." *Journal of Statistical Software* 33(1), 1-22, 2010.
- Registry: [EQ-PEN-CV-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-pen-cv-001--k-fold-cross-validation-and-the-one-standard-error-rule)

## `probstat.inference.resampling`

### EQ-INF-BOOT-001 — Nonparametric bootstrap bias and standard error

Resample `x*_b` of size `n` with replacement, `theta*_b = s(x*_b)`;
`bias = mean(theta*) - theta_hat`, `se = sd(theta*)` (divisor `B - 1`).

- Domain: `n >= 2` observations, `B >= 2` replicates.
- Meaning: plug-in estimates of the sampling bias and spread of a statistic.
- Code: `inference.bootstrap`, `inference.BootstrapResult`.
- Source: [REF-EFRON-1979] and [REF-EFRON-TIBSHIRANI-1993, Chapters 6 and 10].
  - [REF-EFRON-1979](../references.md#ref-efron-1979): Bradley Efron. "Bootstrap Methods: Another Look at the Jackknife." *The Annals of Statistics* 7(1), 1-26, 1979.
  - [REF-EFRON-TIBSHIRANI-1993](../references.md#ref-efron-tibshirani-1993): Bradley Efron and Robert J. Tibshirani. *An Introduction to the Bootstrap*. Chapman & Hall, 1993.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-BOOT-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-boot-001--nonparametric-bootstrap-bias-and-standard-error)

### EQ-INF-BOOT-002 — Percentile, basic, and normal bootstrap intervals

Percentile `[q_(alpha/2), q_(1-alpha/2)]`; basic
`[2 theta_hat - q_(1-alpha/2), 2 theta_hat - q_(alpha/2)]`; normal
`theta_hat - bias +- z_(1-alpha/2) se`, with type-7 replicate quantiles `q`.

- Domain: bootstrap replicates of a smooth statistic.
- Meaning: first-order accurate approximate confidence procedures.
- Code: `inference.bootstrap_confidence_interval`.
- Source: [REF-EFRON-TIBSHIRANI-1993, Chapter 13] and
  [REF-DAVISON-HINKLEY-1997, Chapter 5].
  - [REF-EFRON-TIBSHIRANI-1993](../references.md#ref-efron-tibshirani-1993): Bradley Efron and Robert J. Tibshirani. *An Introduction to the Bootstrap*. Chapman & Hall, 1993.
  - [REF-DAVISON-HINKLEY-1997](../references.md#ref-davison-hinkley-1997): A. C. Davison and D. V. Hinkley. *Bootstrap Methods and Their Application*. Cambridge University Press, 1997.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-BOOT-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-boot-002--percentile-basic-and-normal-bootstrap-intervals)

### EQ-INF-BOOT-003 — Studentized (bootstrap-t) interval

`t*_b = (theta*_b - theta_hat)/se*_b`;
`[theta_hat - t*_(1-alpha/2) se, theta_hat - t*_(alpha/2) se]`, with `se`
from a supplied function or the jackknife.

- Domain: positive standard errors for the data and every resample.
- Meaning: second-order accurate for approximately pivotal statistics.
- Code: `inference.bootstrap_confidence_interval(method="studentized")`.
- Source: [REF-EFRON-TIBSHIRANI-1993, Chapter 12] and
  [REF-DAVISON-HINKLEY-1997, Chapter 5].
  - [REF-EFRON-TIBSHIRANI-1993](../references.md#ref-efron-tibshirani-1993): Bradley Efron and Robert J. Tibshirani. *An Introduction to the Bootstrap*. Chapman & Hall, 1993.
  - [REF-DAVISON-HINKLEY-1997](../references.md#ref-davison-hinkley-1997): A. C. Davison and D. V. Hinkley. *Bootstrap Methods and Their Application*. Cambridge University Press, 1997.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-BOOT-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-boot-003--studentized-bootstrap-t-interval)

### EQ-INF-BOOT-004 — Bias-corrected and accelerated (BCa) interval

`z_0 = Phi^-1((#{theta* < theta_hat} + #{theta* = theta_hat}/2)/B)`;
`a = sum (m - theta_(i))^3 / (6 (sum (m - theta_(i))^2)^(3/2))` from
jackknife values `theta_(i)` with mean `m`;
`alpha_j = Phi(z_0 + (z_0 + z_j)/(1 - a(z_0 + z_j)))`, `z_j = Phi^-1` of
`alpha/2` and `1 - alpha/2`; interval `[q_(alpha_1), q_(alpha_2)]`.

- Domain: `0 < share < 1`, non-constant jackknife values,
  `1 - a(z_0 + z_j) > 0`.
- Meaning: transformation-respecting, second-order accurate correction of
  the percentile interval for bias and skewness.
- Code: `inference.bootstrap_confidence_interval(method="bca")`.
- Source: [REF-EFRON-1987] and [REF-EFRON-TIBSHIRANI-1993, Chapter 14].
  - [REF-EFRON-1987](../references.md#ref-efron-1987): Bradley Efron. "Better Bootstrap Confidence Intervals." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 82(397), 171-185, 1987.
  - [REF-EFRON-TIBSHIRANI-1993](../references.md#ref-efron-tibshirani-1993): Bradley Efron and Robert J. Tibshirani. *An Introduction to the Bootstrap*. Chapman & Hall, 1993.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-BOOT-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-boot-004--bias-corrected-and-accelerated-bca-interval)

### EQ-INF-JACK-001 — Jackknife bias and standard error

`theta_(i) = s(x without x_i)`, `theta_(.) = mean theta_(i)`;
`bias = (n - 1)(theta_(.) - theta_hat)`,
`se = sqrt((n - 1)/n sum (theta_(i) - theta_(.))^2)`.

- Domain: `n >= 2`; smooth statistics (unreliable for the median).
- Meaning: deterministic leave-one-out estimates of bias and spread.
- Code: `inference.jackknife`.
- Source: [REF-EFRON-TIBSHIRANI-1993, Chapter 11].
  - [REF-EFRON-TIBSHIRANI-1993](../references.md#ref-efron-tibshirani-1993): Bradley Efron and Robert J. Tibshirani. *An Introduction to the Bootstrap*. Chapman & Hall, 1993.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-JACK-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-jack-001--jackknife-bias-and-standard-error)

### EQ-INF-PERM-001 — Permutation p-value for a difference in means

`T = mean(x) - mean(y)`; exact `p = #{g : T_g extreme} / C(m+n, m)` over all
relabelings `g`; Monte Carlo `p = (b + 1)/(M + 1)` for `b` of `M` random
relabelings at least as extreme (two-sided uses `|T|`).

- Domain: exchangeable pooled observations under the null.
- Meaning: exact-level test without distributional assumptions; the `+1`
  terms keep the Monte Carlo test valid and the p-value positive.
- Code: `inference.permutation_test`.
- Source: [REF-GOOD-2005] and [REF-PHIPSON-SMYTH-2010].
  - [REF-GOOD-2005](../references.md#ref-good-2005): Phillip I. Good. *Permutation, Parametric, and Bootstrap Tests of Hypotheses*. 3rd edition. Springer, 2005.
  - [REF-PHIPSON-SMYTH-2010](../references.md#ref-phipson-smyth-2010): Belinda Phipson and Gordon K. Smyth. "Permutation P-values Should Never Be Zero: Calculating Exact P-values When Permutations Are Randomly Drawn." *Statistical Applications in Genetics and Molecular Biology* 9(1), Article 39, 2010.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-PERM-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-perm-001--permutation-p-value-for-a-difference-in-means)

## `probstat.inference.sampling_distributions`

### EQ-INF-DIST-NORM-001 — Standard normal distribution and survival functions

`Phi(z) = erfc(-z/sqrt(2))/2`, `1 - Phi(z) = erfc(z/sqrt(2))/2`.

- Domain: finite real `z`.
- Meaning: reference law of z statistics; the complementary error function
  keeps both tails accurate without `1 - Phi` cancellation.
- Code: `inference.standard_normal_cdf`, `inference.standard_normal_sf`.
- Source: [REF-NIST-DLMF, Section 7.2] and [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Chapter 3].
  - [REF-NIST-DLMF](../references.md#ref-nist-dlmf): NIST Digital Library of Mathematical Functions, Chapter 7, "Error Functions, Dawson's and Fresnel Integrals." National Institute of Standards and Technology. [https://dlmf.nist.gov/7](https://dlmf.nist.gov/7).
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-DIST-NORM-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-dist-norm-001--standard-normal-distribution-and-survival-functions)

### EQ-INF-DIST-CHI2-001 — Chi-square upper tail

`P(X >= x) = Q(k/2, x/2)` for `X ~ chi^2_k`.

- Domain: `k > 0`, `x >= 0`.
- Meaning: null reference law of Pearson statistics; the upper regularized
  incomplete gamma function returns small p-values directly.
- Code: `inference.chi_square_sf`.
- Source: [REF-NIST-DLMF-GAMMA, Section 8.2] and
  [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Chapter 5].
  - [REF-NIST-DLMF-GAMMA](../references.md#ref-nist-dlmf-gamma): NIST Digital Library of Mathematical Functions, Chapters 5 and 8, "Gamma Function" and "Incomplete Gamma and Related Functions." National Institute of Standards and Technology. [https://dlmf.nist.gov/8](https://dlmf.nist.gov/8).
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-DIST-CHI2-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-dist-chi2-001--chi-square-upper-tail)

### EQ-INF-DIST-F-001 — F upper tail

`P(F >= f) = I_{d2/(d2 + d1 f)}(d2/2, d1/2)` for `F ~ F(d1, d2)`.

- Domain: `d1, d2 > 0`, `f >= 0`.
- Meaning: null reference law of the ANOVA ratio of mean squares.
- Code: `inference.f_sf`, `inference.one_way_anova`.
- Source: [REF-NIST-DLMF-BETA, Section 8.17] and
  [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002, Chapter 5].
  - [REF-NIST-DLMF-BETA](../references.md#ref-nist-dlmf-beta): NIST Digital Library of Mathematical Functions, Section 8.17, "Incomplete Beta Functions." National Institute of Standards and Technology. [https://dlmf.nist.gov/8.17](https://dlmf.nist.gov/8.17).
  - [REF-CASELLA-BERGER-2002](../references.md#ref-casella-berger-2002): George Casella and Roger L. Berger. *Statistical Inference*. 2nd edition. Duxbury, 2002.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-DIST-F-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-dist-f-001--f-upper-tail)

### EQ-INF-DIST-NCT-001 — Noncentral t distribution function

`T' = (Z + delta)/sqrt(V/nu)`; with `S = sqrt(V/nu)`,
`P(T' <= t) = integral_0^inf Phi(t s - delta) g_nu(s) ds`,
`g_nu(s) = 2 (nu/2)^(nu/2) s^(nu-1) exp(-nu s^2/2) / Gamma(nu/2)`;
integrated by adaptive Gauss-Legendre quadrature [EQ-INF-DIST-NCT-002] on
`[max(0, 1 - 12/sqrt(nu)), 1 + 12/sqrt(nu)]`.

- Domain: `nu >= 1`, finite `t` and `delta`; `delta = 0` returns the central
  CDF exactly.
- Meaning: law of the t statistic under a shifted mean; drives t-test power.
- Code: `inference.noncentral_t_cdf`.
- Source: [REF-JOHNSON-KOTZ-BALAKRISHNAN-1995] (noncentral t chapter) and
  [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984, Chapter 2].
  - [REF-JOHNSON-KOTZ-BALAKRISHNAN-1995](../references.md#ref-johnson-kotz-balakrishnan-1995): Norman L. Johnson, Samuel Kotz, and N. Balakrishnan. *Continuous Univariate Distributions, Volume 2*. 2nd edition. Wiley, 1995.
  - [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984](../references.md#ref-davis-rabinowitz-1984): Philip J. Davis and Philip Rabinowitz. *Methods of Numerical Integration*. 2nd edition. Academic Press, 1984. ISBN 978-0-12-206360-2.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-DIST-NCT-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-dist-nct-001--noncentral-t-distribution-function)

### EQ-INF-DIST-NCT-002 — Adaptive Gauss-Legendre evaluation of the noncentral t

`P(T' <= t) = integral Phi(t s - delta) g_nu(s) ds` by 10-point
Gauss-Legendre panels, bisected until the two halves agree with the panel
estimate within the panel's share of `1e-13`; breakpoints at
`1 + {-3, 0, 3}/sqrt(2 nu)` and `delta/t + {-8, -1, 0, 1, 8}/|t|`; for
`nu < 2` the variable `u = s^nu` gives the bounded integrand
`Phi(t u^(1/nu) - delta) C exp(-nu u^(2/nu)/2)/nu`.

- Domain: `nu >= 1`, finite `t` and `delta != 0`.
- Meaning: resolves both the chi-scale bump and the normal step of width
  `1/|t|`; a fixed 2000-panel Simpson rule erred by up to `7e-4`.
- Code: `inference.noncentral_t_cdf`,
  `inference.sampling_distributions._adaptive_integral`,
  `inference.sampling_distributions._gauss_legendre_rule`.
- Source: [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984, Chapters 2 and 6].
  - [REF-DAVIS-RABINOWITZ-1984](../references.md#ref-davis-rabinowitz-1984): Philip J. Davis and Philip Rabinowitz. *Methods of Numerical Integration*. 2nd edition. Academic Press, 1984. ISBN 978-0-12-206360-2.
- Registry: [EQ-INF-DIST-NCT-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-inf-dist-nct-002--adaptive-gauss-legendre-evaluation-of-the-noncentral-t)

## `probstat.inference.sbc`

### EQ-SBC-RANK-001 — Simulation-based calibration rank statistic

`theta~ ~ p(theta)`, `y~ ~ p(y | theta~)`, `theta_1..L ~ p(theta | y~)`:
`r = #{l : theta_l < theta~}` is uniform on `{0, ..., L}` for every scalar
parameter when the posterior draws are exact and independent. Bins
`b = floor(r B / (L + 1))`; Pearson's statistic against the exact expected
counts has `B - 1` degrees of freedom.

- Domain: continuous parameters (ties broken uniformly at random);
  posterior draws thinned to near independence.
- Meaning: detects biased or mis-dispersed inference algorithms.
- Code: `inference.simulation_based_calibration`, `inference.rank_statistic`.
- Source: [REF-TALTS-ETAL-2018].
  - [REF-TALTS-ETAL-2018](../references.md#ref-talts-etal-2018): Sean Talts, Michael Betancourt, Daniel Simpson, Aki Vehtari, and Andrew Gelman. "Validating Bayesian Inference Algorithms with Simulation-Based Calibration." Preprint, 2018. [arXiv:1804.06788](https://arxiv.org/abs/1804.06788).
- Registry: [EQ-SBC-RANK-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-sbc-rank-001--simulation-based-calibration-rank-statistic)

## `probstat.inference.smoothing`

### EQ-SMOOTH-009 — Locally weighted regression (LOESS)

`f_hat(x0) = a_0`, where `(a_0, ..., a_d)` minimizes
`sum_k delta_k T(|x_k - x0| / h(x0)) (y_k - sum_r a_r (x_k - x0)^r)^2`,
`T(u) = (1 - |u|^3)^3` for `|u| < 1` and `0` otherwise, `h(x0)` the
distance from `x0` to its `q`-th nearest `x_k`, `q = round(f n)`.

- Domain: `0 < f <= 1`, degree `d` in `{0, 1, 2}`, at least `d + 1`
  distinct points with positive weight; `delta_k = 1` without robustness.
- Meaning: a polynomial fitted near each point; the span `f` trades bias
  for variance, and degree 1 avoids the boundary bias of local averaging.
- Code: `inference.smoothing.loess`, `inference.smoothing.LoessFit.predict`.
- Source: [REF-CLEVELAND-1979].
  - [REF-CLEVELAND-1979](../references.md#ref-cleveland-1979): William S. Cleveland. "Robust Locally Weighted Regression and Smoothing Scatterplots." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 74(368), 829-836, 1979. LOWESS: tricube-weighted local polynomial fits over the nearest `f n` points and bisquare robustness iterations. DOI: [10.1080/01621459.1979.10481038](https://doi.org/10.1080/01621459.1979.10481038).
- Registry: [EQ-SMOOTH-009](../equation-registry.md#eq-smooth-009--locally-weighted-regression-loess)

### EQ-SMOOTH-010 — Bisquare robustness weights

`delta_k = B(e_k / (6 s))`, `B(u) = (1 - u^2)^2` for `|u| < 1` and `0`
otherwise, `s = median |e_k|`, `e_k` the current residuals.

- Domain: `s > 0`; for `s = 0` points with zero residual keep weight one
  and the others weight zero.
- Meaning: residuals beyond six median absolute residuals get zero weight
  in the next local fits, so isolated outliers stop pulling the curve.
- Code: `inference.smoothing.loess`.
- Source: [REF-CLEVELAND-1979].
  - [REF-CLEVELAND-1979](../references.md#ref-cleveland-1979): William S. Cleveland. "Robust Locally Weighted Regression and Smoothing Scatterplots." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 74(368), 829-836, 1979. LOWESS: tricube-weighted local polynomial fits over the nearest `f n` points and bisquare robustness iterations. DOI: [10.1080/01621459.1979.10481038](https://doi.org/10.1080/01621459.1979.10481038).
- Registry: [EQ-SMOOTH-010](../equation-registry.md#eq-smooth-010--bisquare-robustness-weights)

### EQ-SMOOTH-011 — Isotonic regression and the min-max formula

`f_hat = argmin_{f_1 <= ... <= f_n} sum_i w_i (y_i - f_i)^2`,
`f_hat_i = max_{j <= i} min_{k >= i} (sum_{l=j}^{k} w_l y_l) / (sum_{l=j}^{k} w_l)`.

- Domain: `w_i > 0`; the non-increasing fit is minus the isotonic fit of
  `-y`.
- Meaning: the fit is a step function whose levels are weighted means of
  consecutive blocks; pooling adjacent violators finds it in one pass.
- Code: `inference.smoothing.isotonic_regression`.
- Source: [REF-AYER-ETAL-1955], [REF-DE-LEEUW-HORNIK-MAIR-2009].
  - [REF-AYER-ETAL-1955](../references.md#ref-ayer-etal-1955): Miriam Ayer, H. D. Brunk, G. M. Ewing, W. T. Reid, and Edward Silverman. "An Empirical Distribution Function for Sampling with Incomplete Information." *The Annals of Mathematical Statistics* 26(4), 641-647, 1955. The pool-adjacent-violators construction of the monotone least-squares estimate. DOI: [10.1214/aoms/1177728423](https://doi.org/10.1214/aoms/1177728423).
  - [REF-DE-LEEUW-HORNIK-MAIR-2009](../references.md#ref-de-leeuw-hornik-mair-2009): Jan de Leeuw, Kurt Hornik, and Patrick Mair. "Isotone Optimization in R: Pool-Adjacent-Violators Algorithm (PAVA) and Active Set Methods." *Journal of Statistical Software* 32(5), 2009. The weighted PAVA, its block structure, and the min-max characterization of the solution. DOI: [10.18637/jss.v032.i05](https://doi.org/10.18637/jss.v032.i05).
- Registry: [EQ-SMOOTH-011](../equation-registry.md#eq-smooth-011--isotonic-regression-and-the-min-max-formula)

### EQ-SMOOTH-012 — Nadaraya-Watson and local-linear kernel regression

Nadaraya-Watson: `m_hat(x0) = sum_i K_i y_i / sum_i K_i`,
`K_i = K((x_i - x0)/h)`; local linear:
`m_hat(x0) = (S_2 T_0 - S_1 T_1)/(S_0 S_2 - S_1^2)`,
`S_r = sum_i K_i u_i^r`, `T_r = sum_i K_i u_i^r y_i`, `u_i = (x_i - x0)/h`.

- Domain: `h > 0`; `K` Gaussian `exp(-u^2/2)` or Epanechnikov
  `(1 - u^2)_+` (constants cancel); a positive denominator.
- Meaning: the intercept of a kernel-weighted constant or straight-line
  fit at `x0`; the local-linear form reproduces straight lines exactly
  and removes the first-order boundary bias of Nadaraya-Watson.
- Code: `inference.smoothing.kernel_regression`,
  `inference.smoothing.KernelRegressionFit.predict`.
- Source: [REF-NADARAYA-1964], [REF-FAN-GIJBELS-1996],
  [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009, Section 6.1].
  - [REF-NADARAYA-1964](../references.md#ref-nadaraya-1964): E. A. Nadaraya. "On Estimating Regression." *Theory of Probability and Its Applications* 9(1), 141-142, 1964. The kernel-weighted average estimate of a regression function. DOI: [10.1137/1109020](https://doi.org/10.1137/1109020).
  - [REF-FAN-GIJBELS-1996](../references.md#ref-fan-gijbels-1996): Jianqing Fan and Irène Gijbels. *Local Polynomial Modelling and Its Applications*. Chapman and Hall, London, 1996 (Routledge reissue 2018). Local-linear and local-polynomial kernel regression, boundary behaviour, and bandwidth selection. DOI: [10.1201/9780203748725](https://doi.org/10.1201/9780203748725).
  - [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009](../references.md#ref-hastie-tibshirani-friedman-2009): Trevor Hastie, Robert Tibshirani, and Jerome Friedman. *The Elements of Statistical Learning: Data Mining, Inference, and Prediction*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2009.
- Registry: [EQ-SMOOTH-012](../equation-registry.md#eq-smooth-012--nadaraya-watson-and-local-linear-kernel-regression)

### EQ-SMOOTH-013 — Leave-one-out cross-validation of the bandwidth

`CV(h) = (1/n) sum_i (y_i - m_hat_{-i}(x_i; h))^2`, `m_hat_{-i}` the
estimate computed without observation `i`; `h_hat = argmin CV(h)` over a
candidate grid.

- Domain: every leave-one-out estimate is defined (otherwise
  `CV(h) = infinity`).
- Meaning: an almost unbiased estimate of the prediction error at the
  design points; the curve is often flat, so neighbouring bandwidths are
  equally supported.
- Code: `inference.smoothing.kernel_regression`.
- Source: [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009, Section 7.10],
  [REF-FAN-GIJBELS-1996].
  - [REF-HASTIE-TIBSHIRANI-FRIEDMAN-2009](../references.md#ref-hastie-tibshirani-friedman-2009): Trevor Hastie, Robert Tibshirani, and Jerome Friedman. *The Elements of Statistical Learning: Data Mining, Inference, and Prediction*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2009.
  - [REF-FAN-GIJBELS-1996](../references.md#ref-fan-gijbels-1996): Jianqing Fan and Irène Gijbels. *Local Polynomial Modelling and Its Applications*. Chapman and Hall, London, 1996 (Routledge reissue 2018). Local-linear and local-polynomial kernel regression, boundary behaviour, and bandwidth selection. DOI: [10.1201/9780203748725](https://doi.org/10.1201/9780203748725).
- Registry: [EQ-SMOOTH-013](../equation-registry.md#eq-smooth-013--leave-one-out-cross-validation-of-the-bandwidth)

## `probstat.inference.splines`

### EQ-SMOOTH-001 — Cox-de Boor recursion for B-splines

`B_{i,0}(x) = 1` for `t_i <= x < t_{i+1}` and `0` otherwise;
`B_{i,p}(x) = (x - t_i)/(t_{i+p} - t_i) B_{i,p-1}(x)
+ (t_{i+p+1} - x)/(t_{i+p+1} - t_{i+1}) B_{i+1,p-1}(x)`, with `0/0 = 0`.

- Domain: non-decreasing knots `t_0, ..., t_{m-1}`, no knot repeated more
  than `p + 1` times; `x` in `[t_p, t_{m-p-1}]`, the last interval closed.
- Meaning: the `m - p - 1` functions are non-negative, sum to one, vanish
  outside `p + 1` knot intervals, and span every spline of degree `p` with
  these knots, including all polynomials of degree `<= p`; only `p + 1`
  of them are non-zero at any `x`.
- Code: `inference.splines.bspline_basis`,
  `inference.splines.bspline_design_matrix`.
- Source: [REF-DE-BOOR-1972].
  - [REF-DE-BOOR-1972](../references.md#ref-de-boor-1972): Carl de Boor. "On Calculating with B-splines." *Journal of Approximation Theory* 6(1), 50-62, 1972. The stable recurrence for the values of the B-splines of a knot vector and its triangular evaluation scheme. DOI: [10.1016/0021-9045(72)90080-9](https://doi.org/10.1016/0021-9045(72)90080-9).
- Registry: [EQ-SMOOTH-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-smooth-001--cox-de-boor-recursion-for-b-splines)

### EQ-SMOOTH-006 — Bayesian pointwise confidence band

`V_beta = phi_hat (X'WX + S_lambda)^-1`,
`f_hat(x) +/- z_{(1+level)/2} sqrt(b(x)' V_beta b(x))`, `b(x)` the basis row.

- Domain: as [EQ-SMOOTH-005]; `phi_hat` from [EQ-SMOOTH-003].
- Meaning: the posterior covariance under the prior implied by the
  penalty; it includes smoothing bias, so the coverage averaged across the
  function is close to nominal, while at a single point it can be lower
  or higher. The band ignores the uncertainty of `lambda` itself.
- Code: `inference.splines.PSplineFit.band`,
  `inference.splines.SmoothingSplineFit.band`,
  `inference.additive_models.AdditiveTerm.band`.
- Source: [REF-WAHBA-1983], [REF-NYCHKA-1988], [REF-WOOD-2017, Chapter 6].
  - [REF-WAHBA-1983](../references.md#ref-wahba-1983): Grace Wahba. "Bayesian 'Confidence Intervals' for the Cross-Validated Smoothing Spline." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society Series B: Statistical Methodology* 45(1), 133-150, 1983. Posterior covariance of a smoothing spline under the equivalent Gaussian prior. DOI: [10.1111/j.2517-6161.1983.tb01239.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.2517-6161.1983.tb01239.x).
  - [REF-NYCHKA-1988](../references.md#ref-nychka-1988): Douglas Nychka. "Bayesian Confidence Intervals for Smoothing Splines." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 83(404), 1134-1143, 1988. Bayesian pointwise intervals for smoothing splines and their average coverage across the function. DOI: [10.1080/01621459.1988.10478711](https://doi.org/10.1080/01621459.1988.10478711).
  - [REF-WOOD-2017](../references.md#ref-wood-2017): Simon N. Wood. *Generalized Additive Models: An Introduction with R*. 2nd edition. Chapman and Hall/CRC, Boca Raton, 2017. Penalized regression splines, additive models with identifiability constraints, effective degrees of freedom, GCV and REML smoothness selection, and Bayesian confidence intervals. DOI: [10.1201/9781315370279](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781315370279).
- Registry: [EQ-SMOOTH-006](../equation-registry.md#eq-smooth-006--bayesian-pointwise-confidence-band)

### EQ-SMOOTH-007 — Cubic smoothing spline (Reinsch algorithm)

Minimizer of `sum_i w_i (y_i - g(x_i))^2 + lambda int g''(x)^2 dx`: the
natural cubic spline with knots `x_1 < ... < x_n`, values `g` and interior
second derivatives `gamma` solving
`(R + lambda Q'W^-1 Q) gamma = Q'y`, `g = y - lambda W^-1 Q gamma`, where
`h_i = x_{i+1} - x_i`, `Q` (`n x (n-2)`) has `q_{j-1,j} = 1/h_{j-1}`,
`q_{jj} = -1/h_{j-1} - 1/h_j`, `q_{j+1,j} = 1/h_j`, and `R` is tridiagonal
with `r_jj = (h_{j-1} + h_j)/3`, `r_{j,j+1} = h_j/6`;
`int g''^2 = gamma' R gamma`.

- Domain: distinct `x_i`, `n >= 3`, `w_i > 0`, `lambda > 0`.
- Meaning: the penalty `g'Kg`, `K = Q R^-1 Q'`, leaves straight lines
  unpenalized; the pentadiagonal system costs `O(n)`. Beyond the end knots
  the spline is linear.
- Code: `inference.splines.fit_smoothing_spline`,
  `inference.splines.SmoothingSplineFit.predict`.
- Source: [REF-REINSCH-1967], [REF-GREEN-SILVERMAN-1994].
  - [REF-REINSCH-1967](../references.md#ref-reinsch-1967): Christian H. Reinsch. "Smoothing by Spline Functions." *Numerische Mathematik* 10(3), 177-183, 1967. The natural cubic smoothing spline computed from a banded linear system in `O(n)` operations. DOI: [10.1007/BF02162161](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02162161).
  - [REF-GREEN-SILVERMAN-1994](../references.md#ref-green-silverman-1994): Peter J. Green and Bernard W. Silverman. *Nonparametric Regression and Generalized Linear Models: A Roughness Penalty Approach*. Chapman and Hall, London, 1994 (Springer US). The value-second-derivative representation of natural cubic splines with the matrices `Q` and `R`, and the Reinsch algorithm. DOI: [10.1007/978-1-4899-4473-3](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4899-4473-3).
- Registry: [EQ-SMOOTH-007](../equation-registry.md#eq-smooth-007--cubic-smoothing-spline-reinsch-algorithm)

### EQ-SMOOTH-008 — Central band of the inverse of a banded matrix

For `M = U'DU` with `U` unit upper triangular of bandwidth `b`:
`(M^-1)_ij = delta_ij / d_i - sum_{k=1}^{b} U_{i,i+k} (M^-1)_{i+k,j}`,
`j = i, ..., i + b`, computed from the last row upward;
`tr H = n - lambda sum_{|j-k|<=2} (M^-1)_jk (Q'W^-1Q)_kj`,
`H_ii = 1 - (lambda / w_i) sum_{j,k} Q_ij (M^-1)_jk Q_ik`.

- Domain: `M` symmetric positive definite and banded.
- Meaning: the entries of `M^-1` within the band are all that the trace
  and the diagonal of the smoother matrix need, so GCV, REML, and the
  pointwise variances `phi H_ii / w_i` of a smoothing spline cost `O(n)`.
- Code: `inference.splines._Band.inverse_band`,
  `inference.splines._ReinschSystem.solve`.
- Source: [REF-HUTCHINSON-DEHOOG-1985].
  - [REF-HUTCHINSON-DEHOOG-1985](../references.md#ref-hutchinson-dehoog-1985): M. F. Hutchinson and F. R. de Hoog. "Smoothing Noisy Data with Spline Functions." *Numerische Mathematik* 47(1), 99-106, 1985. The central band of the inverse of a banded symmetric matrix from its `U'DU` factor, which gives the trace and leverages of a smoothing spline in `O(n)`. DOI: [10.1007/BF01389878](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01389878).
- Registry: [EQ-SMOOTH-008](../equation-registry.md#eq-smooth-008--central-band-of-the-inverse-of-a-banded-matrix)

## `probstat.inference.survival.cox`

### EQ-SURV-COX-001 — Cox partial likelihood with Breslow ties

`l(beta) = sum_j [sum_{i in D_j} x_i' beta - d_j log S0(R_j)]`,
`S0(A) = sum_{i in A} exp(x_i' beta)`; score
`U = sum_j [sum_{D_j} x_i - d_j S1/S0]`, information
`I = sum_j d_j [S2/S0 - (S1/S0)(S1/S0)']` over `R_j = {i : t_i >= t_j}`.

- Domain: proportional hazards `h(t | x) = h_0(t) exp(x' beta)`;
  non-informative censoring; covariates fixed in time.
- Meaning: Cox's partial likelihood; with ties every tied event is compared
  with the full risk set, which biases `beta` toward zero when ties are many.
- Code: `inference.survival.cox.cox_partial_likelihood`,
  `inference.survival.cox.fit_cox_model`.
- Source: [REF-COX-1972], [REF-BRESLOW-1974],
  [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003, Chapter 8].
  - [REF-COX-1972](../references.md#ref-cox-1972): D. R. Cox. "Regression Models and Life-Tables." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B (Methodological)* 34(2), 187-220, 1972.
  - [REF-BRESLOW-1974](../references.md#ref-breslow-1974): N. Breslow. "Covariance Analysis of Censored Survival Data." *Biometrics* 30(1), 89-99, 1974.
  - [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003](../references.md#ref-klein-moeschberger-2003): John P. Klein and Melvin L. Moeschberger. *Survival Analysis: Techniques for Censored and Truncated Data*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2003. DOI: [10.1007/b97377](https://doi.org/10.1007/b97377).
- Registry: [EQ-SURV-COX-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-surv-cox-001--cox-partial-likelihood-with-breslow-ties)

### EQ-SURV-COX-002 — Efron tie correction

`l(beta) = sum_j [sum_{i in D_j} x_i' beta - sum_{r=0}^{d_j - 1}
log(S0(R_j) - (r/d_j) S0(D_j))]`, with the score and information obtained by
replacing `S_k(R_j)` by `S_k(R_j) - (r/d_j) S_k(D_j)` term by term.

- Domain: as EQ-SURV-COX-001; identical to it when there are no ties.
- Meaning: the tied events leave the risk set in equal fractions, a close
  approximation to the exact (discrete-time) partial likelihood.
- Code: `inference.survival.cox.cox_partial_likelihood`,
  `inference.survival.cox.fit_cox_model`.
- Source: [REF-EFRON-1977], [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003, Chapter 8].
  - [REF-EFRON-1977](../references.md#ref-efron-1977): Bradley Efron. "The Efficiency of Cox's Likelihood Function for Censored Data." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 72(359), 557-565, 1977.
  - [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003](../references.md#ref-klein-moeschberger-2003): John P. Klein and Melvin L. Moeschberger. *Survival Analysis: Techniques for Censored and Truncated Data*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2003. DOI: [10.1007/b97377](https://doi.org/10.1007/b97377).
- Registry: [EQ-SURV-COX-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-surv-cox-002--efron-tie-correction)

### EQ-SURV-COX-003 — Wald hazard-ratio interval and global tests

`HR_k = exp(beta_k)`, interval `exp(beta_k +/- z se_k)` with
`se_k = sqrt([I(beta_hat)^-1]_kk)`; likelihood ratio `2[l(beta_hat) - l(0)]`
and score `U(0)' I(0)^-1 U(0)`, both chi-square with `p` degrees of freedom.

- Domain: large-sample normality of `beta_hat`; finite MLE (no monotone
  likelihood).
- Meaning: multiplicative change in the hazard per unit covariate. With one
  binary covariate, untied times, and Breslow ties, the score statistic equals
  the two-sample log-rank statistic.
- Code: `inference.survival.cox.fit_cox_model`.
- Source: [REF-COX-1972], [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003, Chapter 8].
  - [REF-COX-1972](../references.md#ref-cox-1972): D. R. Cox. "Regression Models and Life-Tables." *Journal of the Royal Statistical Society: Series B (Methodological)* 34(2), 187-220, 1972.
  - [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003](../references.md#ref-klein-moeschberger-2003): John P. Klein and Melvin L. Moeschberger. *Survival Analysis: Techniques for Censored and Truncated Data*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2003. DOI: [10.1007/b97377](https://doi.org/10.1007/b97377).
- Registry: [EQ-SURV-COX-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-surv-cox-003--wald-hazard-ratio-interval-and-global-tests)

### EQ-SURV-COX-004 — Breslow baseline cumulative hazard

`H_0_hat(t) = sum_{t_j <= t} d_j / sum_{i in R_j} exp(x_i' beta_hat)` and
`S_hat(t | x) = exp(-H_0_hat(t) exp(x' beta_hat))`.

- Domain: as EQ-SURV-COX-001; reduces to Nelson-Aalen at `beta = 0`.
- Meaning: the baseline needed to turn hazard ratios into survival curves.
- Code: `inference.survival.cox.fit_cox_model`,
  `inference.survival.cox.CoxModelResult.survival`.
- Source: [REF-BRESLOW-1974], [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003, Chapter 8].
  - [REF-BRESLOW-1974](../references.md#ref-breslow-1974): N. Breslow. "Covariance Analysis of Censored Survival Data." *Biometrics* 30(1), 89-99, 1974.
  - [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003](../references.md#ref-klein-moeschberger-2003): John P. Klein and Melvin L. Moeschberger. *Survival Analysis: Techniques for Censored and Truncated Data*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2003. DOI: [10.1007/b97377](https://doi.org/10.1007/b97377).
- Registry: [EQ-SURV-COX-004](../equation-registry.md#eq-surv-cox-004--breslow-baseline-cumulative-hazard)

## `probstat.inference.survival.nonparametric`

### EQ-SURV-KM-001 — Kaplan-Meier product-limit estimator

`S_hat(t) = prod_{t_j <= t} (1 - d_j / n_j)` over the distinct event times
`t_j`, with `n_j = #{i : t_i >= t_j}` at risk and `d_j` events at `t_j`.

- Domain: independent right-censored observations `(t_i, delta_i)`;
  censoring independent of the event time (non-informative); a subject
  censored at `t_j` counts in `n_j`.
- Meaning: the nonparametric MLE of the survival function; a right-continuous
  step function that drops only at event times and is undefined beyond the
  last observed time when that time is censored.
- Code: `inference.survival.nonparametric.kaplan_meier`,
  `inference.survival.nonparametric.KaplanMeierEstimate.survival_at`,
  `inference.survival.data.SurvivalData.risk_table`.
- Source: [REF-KAPLAN-MEIER-1958], [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003, Chapter 4].
  - [REF-KAPLAN-MEIER-1958](../references.md#ref-kaplan-meier-1958): E. L. Kaplan and Paul Meier. "Nonparametric Estimation from Incomplete Observations." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 53(282), 457-481, 1958.
  - [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003](../references.md#ref-klein-moeschberger-2003): John P. Klein and Melvin L. Moeschberger. *Survival Analysis: Techniques for Censored and Truncated Data*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2003. DOI: [10.1007/b97377](https://doi.org/10.1007/b97377).
- Registry: [EQ-SURV-KM-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-surv-km-001--kaplan-meier-product-limit-estimator)

### EQ-SURV-KM-002 — Greenwood variance

`Var_hat(S_hat(t)) = S_hat(t)^2 sum_{t_j <= t} d_j / (n_j (n_j - d_j))`.

- Domain: as EQ-SURV-KM-001; large-sample (delta-method) approximation. When
  `n_j = d_j` the curve reaches zero and the project reports variance `0`.
- Meaning: sampling variance of the product-limit estimate at a fixed `t`.
- Code: `inference.survival.nonparametric.kaplan_meier`,
  `inference.survival.nonparametric.KaplanMeierEstimate.standard_errors`.
- Source: [REF-GREENWOOD-1926], [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003, Chapter 4].
  - [REF-GREENWOOD-1926](../references.md#ref-greenwood-1926): Major Greenwood. "The Natural Duration of Cancer." *Reports on Public Health and Medical Subjects* 33, 1-26. His Majesty's Stationery Office, London, 1926.
  - [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003](../references.md#ref-klein-moeschberger-2003): John P. Klein and Melvin L. Moeschberger. *Survival Analysis: Techniques for Censored and Truncated Data*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2003. DOI: [10.1007/b97377](https://doi.org/10.1007/b97377).
- Registry: [EQ-SURV-KM-002](../equation-registry.md#eq-surv-km-002--greenwood-variance)

### EQ-SURV-KM-003 — Log-log pointwise confidence interval

`[S_hat^{exp(z w)}, S_hat^{exp(-z w)}]` with
`w = sqrt(sum_{t_j <= t} d_j / (n_j (n_j - d_j))) / |log S_hat(t)|` and
`z = Phi^-1((1 + confidence) / 2)`.

- Domain: `0 < S_hat(t) < 1`; the project returns `(1, 1)` before the first
  event and `(0, 0)` once `S_hat = 0`.
- Meaning: a delta-method interval on the `log(-log S)` scale; it always lies
  inside `(0, 1)`, unlike the plain `S_hat +/- z se` interval.
- Code: `inference.survival.nonparametric.kaplan_meier`,
  `inference.survival.nonparametric.KaplanMeierEstimate.interval_at`.
- Source: [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003, Chapter 4].
  - [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003](../references.md#ref-klein-moeschberger-2003): John P. Klein and Melvin L. Moeschberger. *Survival Analysis: Techniques for Censored and Truncated Data*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2003. DOI: [10.1007/b97377](https://doi.org/10.1007/b97377).
- Registry: [EQ-SURV-KM-003](../equation-registry.md#eq-surv-km-003--log-log-pointwise-confidence-interval)

### EQ-SURV-NA-001 — Nelson-Aalen cumulative hazard

`H_hat(t) = sum_{t_j <= t} d_j / n_j`, `Var_hat(H_hat(t)) = sum_{t_j <= t}
d_j / n_j^2`, log-transformed interval `H_hat exp(+/- z sqrt(Var) / H_hat)`,
and survival estimate `exp(-H_hat(t))`.

- Domain: as EQ-SURV-KM-001.
- Meaning: the nonparametric estimate of the integrated hazard; `exp(-H_hat)`
  is never below the Kaplan-Meier value.
- Code: `inference.survival.nonparametric.nelson_aalen`,
  `inference.survival.nonparametric.NelsonAalenEstimate.cumulative_hazard_at`,
  `inference.survival.nonparametric.NelsonAalenEstimate.survival_at`.
- Source: [REF-NELSON-1972], [REF-AALEN-1978],
  [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003, Chapter 4].
  - [REF-NELSON-1972](../references.md#ref-nelson-1972): Wayne Nelson. "Theory and Applications of Hazard Plotting for Censored Failure Data." *Technometrics* 14(4), 945-966, 1972.
  - [REF-AALEN-1978](../references.md#ref-aalen-1978): Odd Aalen. "Nonparametric Inference for a Family of Counting Processes." *The Annals of Statistics* 6(4), 701-726, 1978.
  - [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003](../references.md#ref-klein-moeschberger-2003): John P. Klein and Melvin L. Moeschberger. *Survival Analysis: Techniques for Censored and Truncated Data*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2003. DOI: [10.1007/b97377](https://doi.org/10.1007/b97377).
- Registry: [EQ-SURV-NA-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-surv-na-001--nelson-aalen-cumulative-hazard)

### EQ-SURV-MED-001 — Survival quantiles and band-inversion interval

`t_p = min{t_j : S_hat(t_j) <= 1 - p}` (median: `p = 1/2`); the interval is
`[min{t_j : S_L(t_j) <= 1 - p}, min{t_j : S_U(t_j) <= 1 - p}]` with the
log-log pointwise bands `S_L <= S_hat <= S_U` of EQ-SURV-KM-003.

- Domain: `0 < p < 1`; a limit is `+inf` when the corresponding curve never
  reaches `1 - p` during follow-up. If a curve equals `1 - p` exactly on a
  horizontal segment `[t_j, t_{j+1})`, the midpoint `(t_j + t_{j+1}) / 2` is
  used (the convention of `quantile.survfit` in the R survival package),
  which gives the ordinary sample median for uncensored data.
- Meaning: the set of times at which `1 - p` lies inside the pointwise band.
- Code: `inference.survival.nonparametric.KaplanMeierEstimate.quantile`,
  `inference.survival.nonparametric.KaplanMeierEstimate.median`.
- Source: [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003, Chapter 4].
  - [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003](../references.md#ref-klein-moeschberger-2003): John P. Klein and Melvin L. Moeschberger. *Survival Analysis: Techniques for Censored and Truncated Data*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2003. DOI: [10.1007/b97377](https://doi.org/10.1007/b97377).
- Registry: [EQ-SURV-MED-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-surv-med-001--survival-quantiles-and-band-inversion-interval)

### EQ-SURV-RMST-001 — Restricted mean survival time

`mu_hat(tau) = int_0^tau S_hat(u) du` with
`Var_hat = sum_{t_j <= tau} A_j^2 d_j / (n_j (n_j - d_j))`,
`A_j = int_{t_j}^tau S_hat(u) du`, and a normal interval
`mu_hat +/- z sqrt(Var_hat)`.

- Domain: `tau > 0`, not beyond the last observed time while `S_hat > 0`.
- Meaning: the expected event-free time up to `tau`, `E[min(T, tau)]`; it is
  identified even when the median is not.
- Code: `inference.survival.nonparametric.KaplanMeierEstimate.restricted_mean`.
- Source: [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003, Chapter 4].
  - [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003](../references.md#ref-klein-moeschberger-2003): John P. Klein and Melvin L. Moeschberger. *Survival Analysis: Techniques for Censored and Truncated Data*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2003. DOI: [10.1007/b97377](https://doi.org/10.1007/b97377).
- Registry: [EQ-SURV-RMST-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-surv-rmst-001--restricted-mean-survival-time)

### EQ-SURV-LR-001 — k-sample log-rank statistic

`U_g = sum_j (d_gj - d_j n_gj / n_j)`,
`V_gh = sum_j d_j (n_j - d_j) / (n_j - 1) (n_gj / n_j) (1[g = h] - n_hj / n_j)`,
`X^2 = U' V^-1 U` over the first `k - 1` groups, referred to chi-square with
`k - 1` degrees of freedom. For two groups `X^2 = (O_1 - E_1)^2 / V_11`.

- Domain: independent groups with non-informative censoring; large-sample
  chi-square approximation; terms with `n_j = 1` contribute no variance.
- Meaning: compares observed with expected events under equal hazards using
  the hypergeometric moments of each risk-set table; most powerful against
  proportional-hazards alternatives.
- Code: `inference.survival.nonparametric.log_rank_test`.
- Source: [REF-MANTEL-1966], [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003, Chapter 7].
  - [REF-MANTEL-1966](../references.md#ref-mantel-1966): Nathan Mantel. "Evaluation of Survival Data and Two New Rank Order Statistics Arising in Its Consideration." *Cancer Chemotherapy Reports* 50(3), 163-170, 1966.
  - [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003](../references.md#ref-klein-moeschberger-2003): John P. Klein and Melvin L. Moeschberger. *Survival Analysis: Techniques for Censored and Truncated Data*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2003. DOI: [10.1007/b97377](https://doi.org/10.1007/b97377).
- Registry: [EQ-SURV-LR-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-surv-lr-001--k-sample-log-rank-statistic)

## `probstat.inference.survival.parametric`

### EQ-SURV-AFT-001 — Censored accelerated-failure-time log-likelihood

`log T_i = x_i' beta + sigma W`, `z_i = (log t_i - x_i' beta) / sigma`,
`l = sum_i delta_i [log f_W(z_i) - log sigma - log t_i] + (1 - delta_i)
log S_W(z_i)`, with `f_W(z) = exp(z - e^z)`, `S_W(z) = exp(-e^z)` (Weibull;
`sigma = 1` is exponential) or `f_W = phi`, `S_W = 1 - Phi` (lognormal).
Weibull shape `= 1/sigma`, scale `= exp(x' beta)`; exponential rate
`= exp(-x' beta)`.

- Domain: strictly positive times, non-informative censoring, at least one
  event; observed-information standard errors are large-sample.
- Meaning: covariates multiply survival time by `exp(beta_k)` per unit; the
  likelihood uses the density for events and the survival function for
  censored subjects.
- Code: `inference.survival.parametric.fit_parametric_survival`,
  `inference.survival.parametric.ParametricSurvivalFit.distribution`,
  `inference.survival.parametric.ParametricSurvivalFit.weibull_parameters`.
- Source: [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003, Chapter 12].
  - [REF-KLEIN-MOESCHBERGER-2003](../references.md#ref-klein-moeschberger-2003): John P. Klein and Melvin L. Moeschberger. *Survival Analysis: Techniques for Censored and Truncated Data*. 2nd edition. Springer, 2003. DOI: [10.1007/b97377](https://doi.org/10.1007/b97377).
- Registry: [EQ-SURV-AFT-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-surv-aft-001--censored-accelerated-failure-time-log-likelihood)

## `probstat.inference.variational`

### EQ-VI-ELBO-001 — Evidence lower bound

`ELBO(q) = E_q[log p(x)] + H[q] = log Z - KL(q || p/Z)`; for
`q = N(mu, L L')`, `H[q] = sum_i log L_ii + (d/2)(1 + log 2 pi)`.

- Domain: unnormalized `log p` on `R^d` integrable under `q`.
- Meaning: maximizing the ELBO minimizes the reverse KL divergence; the gap
  to `log Z` is the KL divergence (for a mean-field fit of a Gaussian,
  `(1/2)(sum_i log Lambda_ii - log det Lambda)`).
- Code: `inference.advi`, `inference.variational._entropy`.
- Source: [REF-BLEI-KUCUKELBIR-MCAULIFFE-2017] and
  [REF-KUCUKELBIR-ETAL-2017].
  - [REF-BLEI-KUCUKELBIR-MCAULIFFE-2017](../references.md#ref-blei-kucukelbir-mcauliffe-2017): David M. Blei, Alp Kucukelbir, and Jon D. McAuliffe. "Variational Inference: A Review for Statisticians." *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 112(518), 859-877, 2017. DOI: [10.1080/01621459.2017.1285773](https://doi.org/10.1080/01621459.2017.1285773).
  - [REF-KUCUKELBIR-ETAL-2017](../references.md#ref-kucukelbir-etal-2017): Alp Kucukelbir, Dustin Tran, Rajesh Ranganath, Andrew Gelman, and David M. Blei. "Automatic Differentiation Variational Inference." *Journal of Machine Learning Research* 18(14), 1-45, 2017.
- Registry: [EQ-VI-ELBO-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-vi-elbo-001--evidence-lower-bound)

### EQ-VI-REPARAM-001 — Reparameterization gradient

With `x = mu + L eta`, `eta ~ N(0, I)`:
`grad_mu ELBO = E[g]`, `grad_{L_ij} ELBO = E[g_i eta_j]` (`i > j`),
`grad_{lambda_i} ELBO = E[g_i eta_i] L_ii + 1` for `L_ii = exp(lambda_i)`,
where `g = grad log p(x)`.

- Domain: differentiable `log p`; estimated by averaging over a few `eta`
  draws per iteration.
- Meaning: unbiased low-variance stochastic gradients of the ELBO from
  gradients of `log p` alone; the `+1` is the entropy gradient.
- Code: `inference.advi`, `inference.variational._transform`.
- Source: [REF-KUCUKELBIR-ETAL-2017].
  - [REF-KUCUKELBIR-ETAL-2017](../references.md#ref-kucukelbir-etal-2017): Alp Kucukelbir, Dustin Tran, Rajesh Ranganath, Andrew Gelman, and David M. Blei. "Automatic Differentiation Variational Inference." *Journal of Machine Learning Research* 18(14), 1-45, 2017.
- Registry: [EQ-VI-REPARAM-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-vi-reparam-001--reparameterization-gradient)

### EQ-VI-ADVI-001 — Gaussian variational families

Mean-field: `L = diag(exp(omega))`; full-rank: `L` lower triangular with
`L_ii = exp(lambda_i)` and free strictly-lower entries; covariance `L L'`.

- Domain: unconstrained parameters on `R^d` (constrained ones transformed by
  the caller with the Jacobian in `log p`).
- Meaning: mean-field ignores correlations and, for a Gaussian target,
  recovers the mean and the variances `1/Lambda_ii` (too small); full-rank
  can represent the target exactly.
- Code: `inference.advi`, `inference.VariationalResult.covariance`.
- Source: [REF-KUCUKELBIR-ETAL-2017].
  - [REF-KUCUKELBIR-ETAL-2017](../references.md#ref-kucukelbir-etal-2017): Alp Kucukelbir, Dustin Tran, Rajesh Ranganath, Andrew Gelman, and David M. Blei. "Automatic Differentiation Variational Inference." *Journal of Machine Learning Research* 18(14), 1-45, 2017.
- Registry: [EQ-VI-ADVI-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-vi-advi-001--gaussian-variational-families)

### EQ-OPT-ADAM-001 — Adam update with Polyak-Ruppert tail averaging

`m_t = b1 m_{t-1} + (1 - b1) g_t`, `v_t = b2 v_{t-1} + (1 - b2) g_t^2`,
`theta_t = theta_{t-1} + alpha (m_t / (1 - b1^t)) / (sqrt(v_t / (1 - b2^t)) + eps)`
(ascent); the reported parameters average the iterates over the most recent
half of the iterations.

- Domain: stochastic gradients `g_t`; `0 <= b1, b2 < 1`, `alpha, eps > 0`.
- Meaning: per-coordinate step sizes normalized by a running gradient scale;
  averaging the tail removes most of the step noise that a constant `alpha`
  leaves around the optimum.
- Code: `inference.advi`.
- Source: [REF-KINGMA-BA-2015, Algorithm 1] and [REF-POLYAK-JUDITSKY-1992].
  - [REF-KINGMA-BA-2015](../references.md#ref-kingma-ba-2015): Diederik P. Kingma and Jimmy Ba. "Adam: A Method for Stochastic Optimization." In *Proceedings of the 3rd International Conference on Learning Representations (ICLR)*, 2015. [arXiv:1412.6980](https://arxiv.org/abs/1412.6980).
  - [REF-POLYAK-JUDITSKY-1992](../references.md#ref-polyak-juditsky-1992): B. T. Polyak and A. B. Juditsky. "Acceleration of Stochastic Approximation by Averaging." *SIAM Journal on Control and Optimization* 30(4), 838-855, 1992. DOI: [10.1137/0330046](https://doi.org/10.1137/0330046).
- Registry: [EQ-OPT-ADAM-001](../equation-registry.md#eq-opt-adam-001--adam-update-with-polyak-ruppert-tail-averaging)
